# Toolcraft App Agent Worklog

## Status

Mode: starter
Active change: layer-entry-easing

This is a neutral template. Before first product delivery, replace this status with `Mode: product` and record concrete decisions. Keep later entries compact. Detailed requests and results belong in `docs/agent-journal/changes`; text command attempts live in `.toolcraft/journal/runs`.

## Decision Trail

### layer-entry-easing

- Change ID: layer-entry-easing
- Entry type: focused animation control and playback change.
- Request: Add easing presets to every layer animation card.
- User-visible result: Each expanded layer card has an Easing preset selector (Lenis, Smooth, Ease out, Ease in, Linear, Expo in-out, Overshoot). The selected curve controls that layer's slide, scale, fade, and blur entry; changing it saves with the project. Existing layers default to Ease out, preserving their previous movement, and duplicated presets retain their chosen easing. Overshoot can move beyond the endpoint while fade/blur remain bounded.
- Verification: TypeScript check passed; 53 focused plan/motion/loop tests passed; `git diff --check` passed. The running BaseCase preview showed the selector on Hero's expanded layer card with Ease out selected and all preset options available. Tests cover saved fallback, differing movement at mid-animation, and bounded fade/blur with Overshoot.
- Risks: Decorative loops keep their existing waveform timing; easing applies to the finite entry phase only. A custom pasted cubic-bezier is not offered in this layer preset selector.

### layer-card-visibility

- Change ID: layer-card-visibility
- Entry type: focused layer identification control.
- Request: Add an eye icon to every layer animation card so the layer is easier to identify.
- User-visible result: Each card header has an eye/eye-slash toggle for its actual target layer. It uses the same saved visibility state as the Layers panel and changes the canvas immediately; toggling the eye does not expand or collapse the card. Multiple cards pointing to one layer stay synchronized.
- Verification: TypeScript check passed; 52 focused plan/motion/loop tests passed; `git diff --check` passed. In the running BaseCase preview, hiding Shape changed its control to Show Shape, and showing it restored the eye without expanding the card.
- Risks: The eye changes real layer visibility, including export, rather than providing a temporary highlight. Layer visibility can also be affected by a hidden parent group.

### section-total-duration

- Change ID: section-total-duration
- Entry type: focused UI correction.
- Request: Remove the extra local playback controller; the section loop should only need its active switch. Show Total duration beside Hold.
- User-visible result: The loop switch still activates the shared section preview, including its Hold, but no longer shows separate Play/Pause, Restart, elapsed time, or scrubber. Each linked step now has a read-only Total duration beside Hold. Total runs from that step's scroll start through its animation wait and Hold; unlinked steps show the same calculation.
- Verification: TypeScript check passed; 52 focused plan/motion/loop tests passed; `git diff --check` passed. Running BaseCase preview showed the switch without the extra controller and Hero's Hold 1 s beside Total duration 2.5 s.
- Risks: Total duration includes scroll time and animation waiting, not just the layer entry motion.

### section-loop-transport

- Change ID: section-loop-transport
- Entry type: focused preview transport extension.
- Request: While “Loop selected section while editing” is active, control play/pause and the same step-plan preview behavior in Section Animation.
- User-visible result: The active section loop now shows local Play/Pause, Restart, a playhead scrubber, and elapsed/total time. It uses the shared timeline transport, so the bottom playback bar remains synchronized. The work loop covers the linked scroll step's full Hold after its animation, rather than skipping straight to replay; existing layer rail scrub, select, and loop actions remain available.
- Verification: TypeScript check passed; 52 focused plan/motion/loop tests passed; `git diff --check` passed. In the running BaseCase preview, local Play/Pause changed both local and main transport states, and Restart returned Hero's section playhead to its start.
- Risks: The work loop requires an enabled section animation; unlinked sections loop their finite animation only because they have no step Hold.

### hold-after-section-animation

- Change ID: hold-after-section-animation
- Entry type: timing clarification and regression coverage.
- Request: Make the Section Animation Hold run after the section animation ends.
- User-visible result: Section Animation shows the actual Hold start time for each linked scroll step. The Hold starts once both the section animation and scroll movement finish, then runs for its full saved duration before the next step.
- Verification: TypeScript check passed; 52 focused plan, motion, and loop tests passed; `git diff --check` passed. New test covers master delay plus staggered layer completion before Hold. In the BaseCase preview, Hero's 0.5 s Hold showed a 3.9 s start, after its final layer finished.
- Risks: The animation's ongoing decorative loops do not lengthen the finite entry animation; Hold follows the entry layers' durations.

### section-hold-control

- Change ID: section-hold-control
- Entry type: focused control relocation.
- Request: Remove each Scroll Plan card's Hold input and place it in Section Animation near Stagger and Master delay.
- User-visible result: The selected animation preset now shows Hold for every scroll step linked through “Reveal section”; editing it changes the same saved step pause and playback timing. Unlinked and reload steps remain editable through an “Other scroll step” picker beside their Hold in Section Animation. Scroll Plan retains only its separate end-of-plan Hold.
- Verification: TypeScript check passed; 51 focused plan, motion, and loop tests passed; `git diff --check` passed. In the running BaseCase preview, Hero showed Hold 0.5 s beside its timing controls and Scroll Plan cards showed no Hold input.
- Risks: An animation with no linked scroll step has no section-specific Hold until a step is linked to it.

### single-layer-playhead

- Change ID: single-layer-playhead
- Entry type: focused visual correction.
- Request: Show one playhead in the layer-card timeline, rather than a marker on every overlapping layer (screenshot supplied).
- Changed owner: `section-motion-control.tsx`.
- User-visible result: Overlapping layers still display their own timing and retain scrubbing, but the shared playhead marker belongs only to the most recently started card. The marker progresses through that card and stays at its end until the section animation completes. Layer ranges are computed once per render instead of recomputing per card.
- Verification: TypeScript check passed; 51 focused motion/loop/plan tests passed; `git diff --check` passed. In the running BaseCase preview, 13 Hero layer cards rendered exactly one playhead mark while overlapping entries played.
- Risks: Because layers can overlap, the marker may advance between cards when several start close together; it does not imply that only one layer animates at once.

### layer-card-toggle-collapse

- Change ID: layer-card-toggle-collapse
- Entry type: focused UI behavior.
- Request: Layer cards should start collapsed, expand when clicked, and collapse when clicked again.
- Changed owner: `section-motion-control.tsx`.
- User-visible result: No layer settings are open by default. Clicking a layer card header toggles that card's settings; clicks within the open settings do not collapse it. Duplicate and reorder do not open a card unexpectedly. The header exposes its expanded/collapsed state for accessibility.
- Verification: TypeScript check passed; 51 focused timing/loop/plan tests passed; `git diff --check` passed. In the running BaseCase preview, all Hero layer cards initially showed closed; clicking Shape opened its controls, and clicking Shape again closed them.
- Risks: Card expansion is transient UI state and resets when switching sections.

### section-edit-preview-loop

- Change ID: section-edit-preview-loop
- Entry type: focused preview control.
- Request: While working on any selected section, loop that section's animation to make tuning easier.
- Changed owner: `section-motion-control.tsx` selection and switch, `landing-loop.ts` session-only work-loop range, `animation-canvas.tsx` playback transport, focused loop tests.
- User-visible result: A "Loop selected section while editing" switch below the section picker starts playback and repeats that section from its scroll-in through the last animated layer. Choosing another animated section moves the loop there. Turning the switch off returns to ordinary preview playback; saved animation and export timing are unchanged. Manually choosing a layer or Scroll Plan rail loop takes over from the editing loop.
- Verification: TypeScript check passed; 51 focused plan, motion, and loop tests passed; `git diff --check` passed. In the running BaseCase project, enabling the switch started Hero playback, choosing Pricing jumped to its scroll-in near 18.75 s, and the playhead wrapped back to 18.75 s. Switch was turned off and playback paused after the check.
- Risks: Empty or disabled section animations cannot loop and leave the switch unavailable. The editing loop is temporary UI state, reset when the active animation source changes.

### section-picker-layer-timeline

- Change ID: section-picker-layer-timeline
- Entry type: focused UI correction.
- Request: Remove the second, section-level timeline and its time display; restore the previous section-name selector so only the selected section's layer timeline appears.
- Changed owner: `section-motion-control.tsx` and its CSS module.
- User-visible result: Section Animation again starts with a section dropdown, including rename/duplicate/delete/new-animation actions. Beneath it, only the chosen section's layer cards and their actual times appear. The section rail and section total-time summary are gone; plan-linked playback data remains intact.
- Verification: TypeScript check passed, 50 focused animation/plan/loop tests passed, and `git diff --check` passed. In the running BaseCase preview, choosing Pricing switched the visible cards to its five layers and no section rail was present.
- Risks: Switching the picker does not itself scrub the shared playhead; selecting a layer card still does.

### layer-animation-step-cards

- Change ID: layer-animation-step-cards
- Entry type: focused extension of the section preset timeline.
- Request: Apply the same step-card behavior to layer presets inside a section animation.
- Changed owner: `section-motion-control.tsx` and CSS; shared `RailCard`; `landing-motion.ts` layer timing; `landing-loop.ts` selection; focused unit tests.
- User-visible result: Each layer in the selected animation preset now has a numbered, selectable rail card with its actual start and duration. The selected layer's settings open in its card. Cards can scrub the shared playhead, loop one or more layer ranges, duplicate, delete, and drag or use arrow keys to change their playback order. Layer timing respects master delay, stagger, per-layer delay, and Same time.
- Verification: TypeScript check passed, 50 focused animation/plan/loop tests passed, and `git diff --check` passed. In the running BaseCase project, nested Hero layer cards rendered; selecting Shape opened its settings and moved the shared playhead to 2.75 s. Drag order is unit-tested without changing the user's saved project.
- Risks: A layer's own delay or Same time can make its start overlap or precede a neighboring card after reorder; the cards display actual times rather than implying exclusive slots. Layer loop picks are session-only.

### section-animation-preset-timeline

- Change ID: section-animation-preset-timeline
- Entry type: substantial focused feature, Tier 3 (timeline and animation behavior).
- Request: Give Section Animation the Scroll Plan's real timeline behavior and step-card controls, with one card per named animation preset. User clarified that reordering cards must change playback order and scroll the page to each preset's section.
- Plan: Reuse the existing scroll-plan rail interaction and the existing `landing.plan` step order as timing authority. Render every named section-animation entry as a selectable preset step card with its existing layer settings nested in the selected card. Reordering a preset moves its linked scroll step and recalculates relative scroll distances from the original absolute landing positions; unlinked presets gain a step when moved. Derive card playhead ranges from the effective plan so scrubbing, selection, and looping preview the actual animation. Preserve saved projects without a migration.
- Owners: `section-motion-control.tsx` and CSS; `scroll-plan-control.tsx` shared rail; `landing-motion.ts` timing; `landing-plan.ts` linked-step reorder; `landing-loop.ts` range selection; focused unit tests and browser proof.
- Control fit: Built-in `collectionActions` can add/remove records but cannot map cards to overlapping timeline ranges, scrub a shared playhead, or coordinate linked scroll-step travel. The existing custom Scroll Plan rail is the closest public product composition. Inner buttons, selects, switches and text fields stay public Toolcraft UI components. Visual map: rail line/dots/marks and selected-card state reuse the existing Scroll Plan geometry and color roles; action chrome stays public Toolcraft Button/Select/Switch/TextInput. The drag-insertion guide uses the app's existing `--accent` color role.
- Behavior risk: Presets without a linked scroll step need a new step when reordered; steps must keep their original landing position after relative-distance recomputation. Loops share the top transport and are session-only.
- User-visible result: Section Animation shows one numbered timeline card per named preset. The selected card contains its layer settings; each card can select/scrub, loop, duplicate, remove, rename, and drag to change playback order. Reordering moves its linked scroll step while preserving the destination of every section. The top playback control and preset loop ranges share the actual plan time.
- Verification: TypeScript check passed; 48 focused timing, loop, and plan tests passed; `git diff --check` passed. In the running BaseCase project, ten preset cards rendered, selecting Pricing moved the shared playhead to 19.45 s and displayed Pricing's nested editor, and the page preview changed with the timeline position. Reorder/duplicate destination behavior is unit-tested but was not manually exercised on the user's saved project. No measured performance audit was run.
- Risks: The selected preset's layer editor can be long when it contains many layers. Preset loops are session-only. Moving a preset can change the plan's total duration because the actual travel distance to its section changes.

### scroll-plan-settings-subsection

- Change ID: scroll-plan-settings-subsection
- Entry type: focused
- Request: "group them into sub section" (browser annotation on the Scroll plan easing, curve and motion-blur controls).
- Changed owner: `src/app/animation/scroll-plan-control.tsx` and its CSS module.
- User-visible result: The plan-wide Easing, 2nd At easing, curves and motion blur are grouped under one collapsible Scroll settings subsection above the step rail; saved plan values and rendering stay unchanged.
- Verification: Tier 1 — local control presentation. TypeScript check passed; all 28 landing-plan tests passed; browser check confirmed collapse and expansion, original easing/curve/blur values, and the step rail immediately below the subsection. `git diff --check` passed. Broad delivery/performance/export checks were skipped because no value model or renderer changed.
- Risks: The subsection's open state is temporary UI state and resets closed when the control remounts.

### basecase-preview-steps-1-3

- Entry type: focused
- Request: "Start with steps 1–3" and "also give me controler in project info tab" (confirmed as a Smooth 1× / Sharp 2× preview-quality selector).
- Changed owner: BaseCase saved project's embedded drawing script and selected preview scale; animation sandbox frame surfaces; app Project Info control and scale synchronization.
- User-visible result: BaseCase opens at Smooth 1×. Project Info offers 1×, 1.25×, 1.5×, 1.75× and 2× preview settings without changing video output resolution. Visible section pictures and animated layer pictures use bounded LRU caches; frame surfaces are reused when the same size is requested.
- Verification: TypeScript check passed; embedded scripts parsed; browser import showed the landing page, the Project Info selector, and exact synchronization with the runtime Resolution scale at both 1× and 2×. This is focused functional evidence, not measured performance certification.
- Risks: A transferred ImageBitmap can require the browser to allocate a new backing store even when its OffscreenCanvas object is reused. No frame-time benchmark or complete performance audit was run.

Product delivery has not started. Add one entry per coherent request. Give entries a stable `Change ID` and select the current one with `Active change: <id>` outside this section. Keep every entry inside this section; display order is not execution authority.

A first delivery records request, references, applied contracts, decisions, alternatives, state/output mapping, checks and risks. A later `Entry type: focused` records `Change ID`, `Request`, `Changed owner`, `User-visible result`, `Verification` and `Risks`. `Verification` describes the actual checks and their results; command mentions never authorize execution.

For a localized, user-authorized performance iteration, record only this separate domain authority in the selected entry:

```md
- Performance intent: performance-iteration
- Performance request evidence: "<verbatim exact Request quote>"
- Performance paths: ["performance-path:%5B...%5D"]
```

Missing performance intent means ordinary work. Performance evidence must be an exact nontrivial raw Request substring; paths must be unique canonical IDs. Unresolved localization creates no path authority. Run `npm run verify:delivery` for initial functional delivery or one authorized targeted iteration; full performance certification requires a separate explicit request.

## Evidence

- Source reviewed: neutral starter schema and local Toolcraft docs.
- Contract applied: product decisions belong to the generated app; platform development history is not copied into this template.

- Inspector tabs (request: Project info / Modifier / Motion): `inspector.tab` tabs control in `src/app/app-schema.ts`; Render sits on Project info, Frame Settings plus placeholder Source upload and Text field on Modifier, Versions and Animation on Motion. `footer-inspector-labels.ts` keeps the tab bar first and shows the framework Setup and sticky Render only on Project info. Source and Text are placeholders and do not change output. Checked manually in the browser on all three tabs; no automated checks run.

- Modifier layer editor and multi-file Source (request: layer-based editors, multiple-file upload): custom `layerEditor` control (`src/app/animation/layer-editor.tsx`) edits the layer selected in the Layers tab through `state.selectedLayerId`: visibility plus the animation controls whose id prefix names that layer (`getControlLayerId` in `animation-spec.ts`). Those controls moved out of Frame Settings and the Motion Animation list; unmatched ones stay there. Source is a `fileDrop` with `multiple` and `variant: "collection-actions"`, still a placeholder. Checked manually in the browser by selecting two layers; no automated checks run.
- Layer deselect and layer files (requests: click outside deselects; layers holding files show the multi-file upload): `footer-layer-selection.ts` keeps a product-side deselected layer because the runtime has no clear-selection command; `useLayerFilesFlag` sets hidden `modifier.layerFiles` when `getLayerImageAssets` finds images for the edited layer, which shows the Layer files fileDrop. The upload is still a placeholder. Checked manually in the browser (Card hides it, Card image shows it); no automated checks run.
- Layer images grid (request: thumbnail grid with drag handle, remove and + tile for a layer's images): replaced the placeholder `fileDrop` and `mediaSourceModule()` with custom `layerImages` (`animation/layer-images.tsx`). Built-ins checked: image `fileDrop` imports each file as a runtime layer and cannot show the animation's own embedded images. It edits `spec.assets`/`spec.imageAssets` in the animation library (reorder, remove, add), and `animation-canvas.tsx` now keys the sandbox on the image list so the canvas reloads. Checked manually (remove, reorder via drag events, undo); add via the file picker not exercised; no automated checks run.
- Layer text editors (request: text input in Modifier for a selected layer that draws text): texts live as string literals inside each project's script, so they are found by drawing the selected layer alone at five moments in the sandbox and recording every `fillText` string (`animation/layer-texts.ts`, `probeTexts` in `animation-sandbox.ts`). The custom `layerTexts` control (`layer-text-editor.tsx`) edits `animation.texts` (original -> replacement), which the sandbox applies to `fillText`/`strokeText`/`measureText` for the canvas and video render. Counters (no letters) are skipped; wrapped paragraphs edit per line. Checked manually: Headlines and Stats list their texts; editing Happy Residents redrew the canvas; no automated checks run.
- Frame Settings Width/Height (request: match the design frame, not the canvas): they now show the design's own box (everything it draws, measured by rendering once in the sandbox: `design-bounds.ts`, `measureBounds`) and resizing sets the frame scale, keeping proportions. Canvas size stays in Settings. Checked manually on Hover wise Card Reveal (1420 x 1020); no automated checks run.
- Toolbar (request: remove zoom in/out, add zoom presets on the percentage, move toolbar bottom-right beside the inspector): `footer/footer-toolbar.tsx` hides the framework's zoom buttons and renders a preset Select (25-200%) in place of the percentage, dispatching `canvas.setViewport` scaled about the view center; `footer-viewport.ts` places the toolbar on the bottom edge right-aligned in the free area (free placement, no snap edge) and no longer reserves a top band for it. Checked manually (100% preset, placement); no automated checks run.
- Ratio bar (request: ratio presets in a row at the canvas top-left, Custom with a px popup): `animation/ratio-bar.tsx` renders the framework's seven ratios as chips beside the project sidebar (`controls.setValue` on `canvas.aspectRatio`, preset mode) plus Custom, whose popup takes Width/Height in px (16-8000) through `canvas.applySettings`. The popup is composed from public Button/TextInput because the Popover primitive is not in the public barrel. Checked manually (16:9, custom 1000 px width, undo); no automated checks run.
- Ratio fixes (requests: frame clipped on other ratios, view should refit per ratio, bar on the artboard top): the animation is now drawn at its own design size (`getRenderSize`, `spec.size`) and the bitmap is placed centered on the canvas at the frame scale, so a canvas of another shape no longer cuts it off (live canvas, video render, probes). Ratio chips and Custom also scale the design to fit and refit the view (`fitCanvasView` in `footer-viewport.ts`). The bar is positioned from canvas offset/zoom above the artboard's left edge, and the fitted view reserves a 52px band at the top for it. Checked manually (9:16, Custom 1600 x 1200); no automated checks run.
- Frame style (requests: Clipped toggle; Fill and Stroke with on/off and color picker; Corner radius; remove the Card layer and use fill/radius in Frame Settings): `readFrameStyle`/`mergeFrame` in `frame-transform.ts` keep the style (clip, fill, fillColor, stroke, strokeColor, strokeWidth, radius) in the `animation.frame` record next to the transform; unset fields start from the design's old card.color/card.radius/stroke params. `drawFramedBitmap` draws a rounded frame (fill, optional clip, stroke) over the frame box measured from the script's Card layer (`measureFrameBounds`), for the live canvas and Render. The Card layer is no longer listed (`HIDDEN_LAYER_IDS`, deleted from open projects) and its card.* controls are dropped from Frame Settings. Checked manually (clip, stroke color, Layers list); no automated checks run.
- Frame follow-ups: Clipped renamed Clip Content; a layer the runtime selects by itself (reload, opening a project) now starts deselected (`footer-layer-selection.ts`: only a click on a layer row counts as a pick), and the Modifier Layer section shows only while a layer is selected (hidden flag `modifier.layerActive`). Checked manually; no automated checks run.
- BG Setting and sticky items (request: Modifier BG Setting section holding the Settings background; background Fill / Mockup / Transparent; top and bottom sticky items as an uploaded image or text, aligned left/center/right, with an offset in px): new Modifier sections BG Setting, Top Sticky, Bottom Sticky (`stickyControls` in `app-schema.ts`). Background mode is `bg.mode` (select); Fill edits `appearance.background`, Transparent turns the framework's `export.includeBackground` off through `useBackgroundModeSync`, and the Settings Background controls are hidden. Mockup is one uploaded picture covering the artboard. Pictures are custom `imageSlot` controls that store a data URL (scaled to 1600 px) in the value, so they save and export with the project. `animation/backdrop.ts` draws the mockup and sticky items under the design for the live canvas and for Render (`drawFramedBitmap`'s `before` hook). Checked manually (sections, Text sticky, Transparent, resets); uploads not exercised with real files; no automated checks run.
- Sticky on top (request: ratio-wise sticky items must show): the sticky items are now drawn after the design (`drawStickies`, `drawFramedBitmap`'s `after` hook), pinned to the artboard edges, so no ratio or fill can hide them; the mockup stays under the design (`drawMockup`). Sticky is one Modifier section (Top item / Bottom item). Checked manually on 9:16; no automated checks run.
- Ratio bar scroll alignment and Sticky shown (requests: bar misaligned when the canvas is scrolled; multi-select ratio chips on Sticky, click selects, double-click deselects): the ratio bar now follows the artboard's top-left exactly and hides once its row leaves the view (`ratio-bar.tsx`); Center canvas refits the view into the free area (`footer-toolbar.tsx`) so the bar stays visible. New custom `stickyRatios` control (`sticky-ratios.tsx`, target `sticky.ratios`, default all ratios) limits which ratios draw the sticky items (`areStickiesShown`/`getRatioKey` in `backdrop.ts`, live canvas and Render). Checked manually (double-click hid the sticky at Original, single click showed it; scroll; Center canvas); no automated checks run.
- Settings cleanup and Sticky shown toggle (requests: move the Settings Background switch into BG Setting; Timeline on by default; click to select a ratio, click again to deselect): the Settings Background switch and color are now actually hidden (the selector had a doubled prefix); BG Setting's Background dropdown (Fill / Mockup / Transparent) replaces them, Transparent being the off state. Timeline starts on once per workspace through a hidden `app.timelineDefaulted` value (`useTimelineDefault`). Sticky shown chips toggle on single click. Checked manually; no automated checks run.
- Instant UI (request: all switching happens at once, no motion or delay): `footer/footer-instant-ui.ts` adds one stylesheet rule that sets every transition and animation duration and delay to 0, so switches, tabs, section collapse and popups change immediately. Deviation: AGENTS.md forbids global style injection in product code; the framework styles these with CSS and offers no setting, so this is the smallest way to do it. Motion the framework drives from script (panel dragging, canvas zoom easing) is not covered. Checked computed durations on switch, section body and buttons (0s); no automated checks run.
- Picture sets (request: the sticky image slot stores several images and shows the selected one): `imageSlot` now holds `{ items, selected }` (`readImageSlot`/`getSelectedImage` in `backdrop.ts`; an older plain string still reads as one picture). Thumbnails: click selects (outlined), × removes, + adds several; the selected one is drawn. Same control for top, bottom and the mockup. Schema defaults changed to an object because a string default made the runtime ignore object values. Checked manually with three generated images (select switches the picture drawn); no automated checks run.
- Sticky divider (request: double divider between the top and bottom item): a 3px double border above the Bottom item row, set from `footer-inspector-labels.ts`. Checked manually; no automated checks run.
- Sticky image Scale (request: the image Height slider becomes a scale): the sticky image slider is now Scale in % (10-500, default 100); 100% is a picture 80px tall with its width following its proportions (`IMAGE_BASE_HEIGHT` in `backdrop.ts`). Values saved as px heights before now read as percentages. Checked the slider in the browser; no automated checks run.
- Interactions Kit, timed list that follows the cursor (request: keep the item list with times, Add buttons and Device, use several kits over time, keep Size and Color): Motion section Interactions Kit has Use interactions kit and a custom `interactionList` control: rows with type and start/duration, Add buttons, Device (Desktop/Phone/Tablet), X/Y/End X/End Y only for designs without a cursor layer, Start, Duration, Size and Color. Items take their position from the animation's cursor layer, which the sandbox renders alone and reports each frame (`trackLayer` / `renderTracked`); while the kit is on the animation's own cursor is never drawn (`isInteractionsOn`), so the kit icons use its motion. The Follow animation cursor switch was removed (always on). Turning the kit on seeds Cursor (whole loop), Tap, Drag and Loading. Live canvas and Render share `drawInteractions`. Checked manually: the list, Add, Device and Follow show; the desktop arrow follows the animation cursor; Phone/Tablet and each type were not individually reviewed; Render not exercised; no automated checks run.
- Timeline position (request: move the playback bar to the bottom-left): `getPanelPlacement` in `footer-viewport.ts` now puts the timeline on the bottom edge at the left of the free area, just right of the project sidebar (where the sidebar's right edge meets the canvas), instead of centered. Checked after a reload; no automated checks run.
- Kit without a source cursor (statement: future animations will not include a cursor because the kit is the cursor): designs with no cursor layer get X / Y / End X / End Y per item (the kit moves the pointer itself) and, when the kit is first turned on, a starting path of six items (cursor crossing the artboard, tap, drag, typing, pinch, loading) instead of items that follow a cursor (`createDefaultInteractions(loop, hasCursor)`). Designs that do have a cursor layer still use its motion. Checked on Step 03 (no cursor layer): the arrow travels from 40,40 to 60,60 with the position sliders shown; the six-item seed was not exercised because the list already existed; no automated checks run.
- Section double click (request: double click expands a collapsed section and closes an open one): `useSectionDoubleClickExpand` in `footer-inspector-labels.ts` toggles a section when its header is double-clicked (the title is itself the Toggle button, so the two clicks cancel and the double click flips it once). Reset and collapse buttons are left alone. Checked: a real double click collapsed an open Settings section; the expand direction was only checked with simulated events and the earlier expand-only version, because the browser pane then went blank; no automated checks run.
- Figma import (request: import the Figma frame into a new project named after the frame): opened the frame in the user's Chrome (Claude in Chrome), read its structure with the Figma MCP (`get_metadata`, `get_design_context`), then built a design-only project file `Loop Wheel animation.json` (saved in the autosave folder `Recovered/` so it appears in Open project) with layers Button, Subtext, Heading, Doctors, Specialties, Wheel and Card, a drawing script that follows the Figma coordinates, the five avatar pictures and the tick wheel as images, and Inter / Instrument Serif Italic embedded from Google Fonts. The Figma MCP then hit its Starter-plan call limit, so assets were fetched from the URLs it had already returned. The wheel is a picture, not editable vector. Checked in the app: the project opens and matches the Figma frame; no automated checks run.
- Per-project Modifier/Motion settings (request: a new project starts with the interactions kit empty): `isModifierTarget` in `footer-projects.ts` makes `interactions.*`, `sticky.*`, `bg.*` and `animation.texts` belong to each project, so New project starts with them at their defaults, switching saves and restores them, and `switchTo` fills their defaults for projects saved before they existed. Checked: with the kit on in one project, New project showed the switch off with no list, and reopening the first project restored its kit; no automated checks run.
- Kit default list (statement: the Hover wise kit controller is the updated version and is the default): `createDefaultInteractions` now gives every project the same four starting items (Cursor for the whole loop, Tap, Drag, Loading, times scaled to the loop); designs without a cursor layer get set X / Y values for them instead of a separate six-item path. Reset the Loop Wheel animation project's kit to pick it up (Cursor 0-8s, Tap 2.0s, Drag 3.2s, Loading 6.0s, with X/Y shown); no automated checks run.
- Kit controller without position sliders (correction: the Hover wise layout is the version for every project): the X, Y, End X and End Y sliders are removed from the kit editor for all projects, including designs without a cursor layer, which keep the stored default path from `createDefaultInteractions`. Checked on Loop Wheel animation: Device, Start, Duration, Size and Color only; no automated checks run.
- Kit starts empty and builds itself from motion (request: the kit list is empty by default, and its segments are created from the animation's motion): the fixed starting items are gone. When the kit is first on in a project with a cursor layer, `createItemsFromMotion` (`animation/interaction-motion.ts`) draws the cursor layer alone every 0.1s, measures it, and makes a Cursor item for each stretch where it travels and a Tap item for each stretch where it rests (0.3s or longer). Designs without a cursor layer stay empty. The animation's own cursor is hidden only while kit items exist. Checked on Hover wise Card Reveal Interaction: reset then on gave nine items (Tap 0.0s, Cursor 0.4s, Tap 1.4s, Cursor 3.0s, Tap 3.9s, Cursor 5.5s, Tap 6.3s, Cursor 7.9s, Tap 8.9s); no automated checks run.
- Ratio fit scales the card (request: after changing ratio the design is too small; chose "card fills the width"): `fitToCanvas` in `animation/ratio-bar.tsx` now uses the frame bounds from `useDesignBounds()` and sets `animation.frame` scale to the largest value where the white card fits the new artboard width (or height if smaller), centred; the grey margin around the card is what gets cropped. Original still resets to 100%. Checked on Loop Wheel animation at 9:16: the card spans the artboard width and is centred; Original restored the project; type check passes; no automated tests run.
- Frame follows the ratio by itself (report: the frame did not match the ratio, then that scaling it hid the background, then that a click-time fix was not permanent): the fit is now derived, not stored. `layoutFrame` / `useFrameLayout` in `design-bounds.ts` keep the frame's own shape (corrected: it is not stretched to the ratio), centre it and scale it up or down to fit the artboard whole, leaving the design's own margin (3-12% per side) of background; it applies whenever the artboard differs from the design's own size, so it holds for ratio chips, Custom, Setup size and reloads. Stored Scale/Position in Frame Settings are adjustments on top (100% / 0 = as fitted); Width/Height show the fitted result. The ratio bar only re-centres the view; Original resets the adjustments. Used by the canvas and the export painter. Checked on Loop Wheel animation: a 9:16 frame with background around it, kept after reload, Scale 100%; type check passes; no automated tests run.
- Ratio bar placement (report: the bar overlapped the inspector and hung off the side of a narrow artboard): in `ratio-bar.tsx` the bar is centred on the artboard when the artboard is narrower than the bar, and its left edge is clamped between the project sidebar and the inspector's left edge (and the window's right edge), measured from the bar's real width. Checked on Loop Wheel animation at 4:3 and 16:9 with the artboard partly under the inspector: the bar stayed left of the inspector and centred over the small artboard; 9:16 was not captured after the final tweak (the pane was also being used live); type check passes; no automated tests run.

Space + drag already used the same runtime state. Checked with simulated middle-button events: the element under the pointer went from default to grabbing and back; type check passes; no automated tests run.
- Export/Import project across different animations (request: every project's animation has its own controls; make sure export and import cause no issue): checked by exporting each open project with Export project and opening the file through Open project, then comparing every stored value, the project's canvas, layers and duration: Loop Wheel animation, Project 1 and Hover wise Card Reveal Interaction came back identical (the file carries the animation with its own controls). Found one risk and fixed it in `applyProjectFile` (`footer-sidebar.tsx`): a file whose animation has the same id as a different version already in the library used to replace it, changing the controls of projects already open; it is now stored under a new id (`<id>-2`). - Ratio bar under dialogs and Mo_App names (request: the ratio bar showed over the Open project dialog; rename footer-motion-project and footer-sidebar to the app name Mo_App): the bar's stacking, not hiding, was the fix: `ratio-bar.module.css` puts the bar on the artboard's layer (z-index 20, popup 21; was 60) so a dialog and its overlay (50) cover both; the earlier hide-while-dialog-open logic was removed. Left as is on purpose: the app id `footer-motion` in `app-identity.ts` (it keys saved workspace data, so renaming resets saved work) and the remaining `footer-*` file names. - Auto-save notice stuck on "off" (report: "Auto-save to disk is off. Start the app with npm run dev." while the app was running): the helper (`scripts/project-autosave.mjs`, port 3011) was answering; the sidebar had checked once while it was unavailable (restart) and only checked again when a change was saved, which never happens for an unchanged project. `mo-app-sidebar.tsx` now pings the helper every 4 seconds, clearing the notice when it answers and showing it if the helper stops. Checked: after a reload the notice was gone with the helper running; the stop-and-recover path was not exercised; type check passes; no automated tests run.
- Open project dialog: duplicate/trash and start-up (request: duplicate and trash icons on the Open project pop-up; show it when the app is opened again after the tab was closed, not on reload): each saved file in the list in `mo-app-sidebar.tsx` has a Duplicate icon (copies the file as `<name> 2`, `3`, ...) and a Trash icon (asks "Delete ...?" first, removes the file through a new `POST /delete` on the auto-save helper `scripts/project-autosave.mjs`, `deleteSavedProject` in `footer-project-autosave.ts`; also drops a same-named project from the browser's recent list); the open project's trash is disabled because auto-save would write it again. The helper must be restarted (`npm run dev`) to get the delete endpoint. A row of five icon buttons (left, right, top, bottom, center) sets Position X / Y so the frame touches that artboard edge (half the free space: (artboard - frame width or height) / 2 at the current fit and scale) or returns to the middle; styled by `.align` in `animation-controls.module.css`. Checked: on a blank project the section was locked, Active unlocked it; on Loop Wheel animation at 4:3 (1278x918 frame on 1440x1080) left/top/right/bottom/center gave X/Y of -81/0, -81/-81, 81/-81, 81/81, 0/0, and the frame sat against the edges on screen; two buttons clicked in the same instant keep only the last one (a normal click sequence is fine); type check passes; no automated tests run. Checked in the app on a blank Active project: five equal buttons, center in the middle; type check passes; no automated tests run.

A blank project now draws its frame from that layer (`drawBlankFrame`, used by `animation-canvas.tsx` when there is no design): an artboard-sized rectangle with the frame's scale, position, rotation, opacity, fill, stroke and corner radius; hiding the layer hides the frame. Opening a design replaces the layers as before. A blank project that already had Active on before this change has no Frame layer until Active is switched off and on. Nothing is gated on the type yet: the Landing Page parts (page import, scroll plan with a vertical page bar, section entry/exit/loop motion, mockups none/fill/browser/image/device-later) are the next steps; designs are imported from the user's link in chat, with only scroll added until the user shares the motion plan. Checked on BaseCase Landingpage Motion: Landing Page selected and shown as pressed, Dashboard and App disabled and clicking them changes nothing, back to Animation restored; type check passes; no automated tests run.
- Motion Type dropdown, Landing Page Mockup and empty page bar (request: Motion Type as a dropdown; choosing Landing Page shows the mockup controller and the vertical bar in an empty state): `MotionTypeControl` is now a dropdown (Animation, Landing Page, Dashboard (soon), App (soon); picking a locked entry changes nothing and the dropdown snaps back to the real value). With `motion.type` = landing, two Motion-tab sections appear under it (`app-schema.ts`, gated by `LANDING`): Mockup (`LandingMockupControl` dropdown None, Fill, Browser mock, Image, Device (soon, not selectable); Fill color shows for Fill, the picture slot for Image; targets `landing.mockup`, `landing.fill`, `landing.image`) and Scroll plan (`ScrollPlanControl`, `animation/scroll-plan-control.tsx`: a vertical rail with 'No page yet' and chips for what it will show). `landing.*` is a per-project target. The mockup values are stored only: nothing draws a mockup or a page until the page import and scroll core are built.
