- Section order (request: Scroll plan above Mockup): in `app-schema.ts` the Scroll plan section now comes before Mockup in the Motion tab for Landing Page; ids and targets unchanged. Checked in the app; type check passes; no automated tests run.
- Lock ratio in Frame Settings (request; chosen: unlocked changes the frame box only, locked works as before): a `Lock ratio` switch (default on) above Width in `frame-settings.tsx`, stored as `lockRatio` in the `animation.frame` record with two new frame-only factors `boxW` / `boxH` (percent, default 100) in `frame-transform.ts`. Locked: Width and Height still move together and scale the whole design (the Scale value). Unlocked: Width or Height resizes only the frame rectangle on that axis about its centre (`layoutFrame` in `design-bounds.ts` applies the factors to the drawn box, `drawBlankFrame` does the same for a blank project's frame); the design inside keeps its size and stays centred, and Clip Content crops it when the frame is smaller. Slider ranges are taken from the design's own size so they do not move while dragging; alignment uses the displayed size; Original also resets `boxW` / `boxH`. Checked on Loop Wheel animation: unlocked Width 1278 to 639 narrowed the frame and cropped the design with Height unchanged; locked Width moved Height with it (1719 / 2468 at Scale 269%); Original restored 1420 / 1020; type check passes; no automated tests run.
- Duplicate Corner radius in Frame Settings (report: two Corner radius controls): a project with no design showed greyed placeholder rows (another Corner radius, Fill color, Stroke color, Stroke width) under the frame's own controls; `frame-settings.tsx` no longer draws them, so a blank project shows only the frame's own set. Checked all five projects (BaseCase Landingpage Motion, Hover wise Card Reveal Interaction, Project 1, Step 03, Loop Wheel animation): one Corner radius each; type check passes; no automated tests run.
- Frame size presets (request: width/height presets like MacBook Air 1280x832, MacBook Pro 14" 1512x982, MacBook Pro 16" 1728x1117, Desktop 1440x1024, plus Custom; Lock ratio only in Custom): a `Frame size` dropdown in `frame-settings.tsx` (Custom, then the four presets from `FRAME_SIZE_PRESETS` in `frame-transform.ts`), stored as `sizePreset` in the `animation.frame` record (default `custom`). A preset is an absolute frame size in artboard px: `layoutFrame` (`design-bounds.ts`) works out the frame box factors from it at the current fit and scale, so the frame stays that size on any ratio and the design inside keeps its own size and position (Clip Content crops it); the layout also returns the effective `boxW` / `boxH`. With a preset only the dropdown shows; Custom shows Lock ratio, Width and Height, starting from the preset's size so nothing jumps. Original resets to Custom. A blank project's Width / Height display now includes the frame box. Checked: BaseCase (blank, Active) Desktop to Custom gave 1440 x 1024, MacBook Pro 16 gave 1728 x 1117; Loop Wheel animation (with a design) Desktop gave 1440 x 1024 in Custom; undo restored the starting state; type check passes; no automated tests run.
- BaseCase landing page import (request: put the Figma design https://www.figma.com/design/1KZr2YIUtDNt6DWeOsEvLP, node 3324:2466, 1280 x 8825, into the existing project BaseCase Landingpage Motion, inside its Frame layer, frame width = design width; only scroll for now): the Figma MCP call limit (Starter plan) was already used up, so the page was exported from the user's Chrome (Export 1x PNG of the Basecase frame; the temporary export setting was removed again from the Figma file) and saved with the project assets in `~/Documents/Mo_App/Projects/BaseCase Landingpage Motion/` (`Basecase-page.png`, nine `section-*.jpg`, a backup `BaseCase Landingpage Motion.before-import.json`, a copy of the finished project file, and the two Python tools used, `tools/build_landing.py` and `tools/apply_landing.py`, which need Pillow). The page frame is 1280 px wide starting at x=98 in the export (the rest of the 1672 px is overhanging decoration, cropped). Nine sections (Hero 0/832, How it Works 832/1002, Testimonial 1834/745, Service 2579/1952, Benefits 4531/684, About 5215/606, Pricing 5821/889, FAQs 6710/1139, Footer 7849/976) became JPEG layers (about 1 MB in total) of a new animation `basecase-landing-page` (size 1600 x 1200, the project's artboard) whose script draws the page through a 1280 x 832 window in the middle of the artboard and scrolls it top to bottom over the timeline: 0.5 s hold, an even-pace target followed with Lenis-style trailing smoothing (0.35 s), 1.5 s settle at the bottom; computed from time only so preview and export match. The project file in `Recovered/` was rewritten with the animation, the layer tree Main > Screen > Frame (now a group) > the nine sections, empty params, and the frame record reset to scale 100 / position 0 / box 100 (Lock ratio, MacBook Air 1280 x 832 preset, Clip Content and the other frame settings kept). The in-app copy was deleted and the file opened, so the project keeps its name and settings. Checked in the app: Hero at 0.39 s, the contact form and footer at 9.69 s, layer tree correct, frame equals the page window. Not done yet (waiting for the user's motion plan): scroll stops and pauses, loading intro, loop, interactions, section entry/exit/loop motion, Scroll plan bar contents, Mockup rendering. The page is a picture, so its text is not editable; 1x export is soft at high zoom or 4K. Scroll speed follows the 10 s timeline (about 1000 px/s); a longer timeline slows it. Type check not affected (no app code changed); no automated tests run.
- Scroll plan for Landing Pages, made automatically on import (request: the import must include a Scroll plan; the plain glide was boring): new `animation/landing-plan.ts` (plan model, `autoSteps`, `evaluateScroll`, `moveStep` with Ripple, tests in `landing-plan.test.ts`), the real `ScrollPlanControl` (`animation/scroll-plan-control.tsx` + `.module.css`) and `AnimationSpec.page` (page height, window size, sections) in `animation-spec.ts`. The plan is stored per project as `landing.plan` (Loading seconds, Ripple, steps of position px / travel s / pause s / pinned; kinds scroll and reload). With no stored steps the page's automatic plan is used (a stop at every section top, extra stops inside sections taller than the window, a last stop at the bottom, the loader first), so an imported page has a plan with no setup; editing anything stores it. Each step eases with Lenis' own ease (expo-out, normalised to land exactly on the target). The panel shows a vertical page map (ruler in k px, section bands, the live window, draggable numbered step dots, kit-interaction pins when the kit is on, the loading chip), buttons Auto steps / Step / Back / Top / Reload, a Loading seconds field and a Ripple switch, and per-step rows with At (px), Travel, Pause, percent of the way down, a pin toggle and remove. Ripple: moving a step carries the unpinned steps after it (up to the next pinned one); if that would pass the bottom or the next pinned step they squeeze together so the last one still lands on it. The canvas (`animation-canvas.tsx`) and the render path (`footer/footer-render.ts`) pass the script `__scrollY` and `__loading` from the plan at each time, and the canvas sets the timeline duration to the plan's length. The BaseCase page script (`tools/apply_landing.py`) uses them, draws a loading screen (soft window with a spinner), and keeps its own time-based glide as a fallback; the project file was rebuilt with `page` info and reopened. Checked in the app on BaseCase Landingpage Motion: 13 steps, 20.7 s with Loading 1.2 s; loader at the start; editing step 3 to 2000 px pinned it and squeezed the later steps with the last on 7993; Travel and Loading edits changed the timeline length (20.7 -> 23 -> 21.8 -> 23); dragging step 5 moved it and the steps after; Auto steps reset; the plan survived a reload; type check and 8 unit tests pass. Not checked: an actual video or GIF export (it downloads a file), the kit pins on the bar, Mockup rendering, a loop back to the top, entry/exit/loop motion per section (waiting for the user's motion plan).
- Easing presets for the Scroll plan (request): `EASINGS` in `animation/landing-plan.ts` - Lenis (the previous ease, still the default), Smooth, Ease out, Ease in, Linear, Expo in-out, Overshoot - each normalised to start at 0 and end exactly on the target (Overshoot passes it and returns; the position is clamped to the page). The plan has a default `easing` and each scroll step can override it (`easing`, stored as the word `plan` for 'follow the plan' because a removed key is not saved by workspace persistence: found when resetting a step to Plan default did not survive a reload). `ScrollPlanControl` has an Easing select with a small curve at the top of the plan and one per step; `buildSegments`/`evaluateScroll` use the step's own easing or the plan's. Checked in the app: the select lists the seven presets, a step override saved and survived a reload, Plan default saved as `plan`; type check and 9 unit tests pass; no automated tests beyond the plan unit tests. A test slip briefly changed step 1's easing; it was set back and unpinned.
- Scroll plan rail, step selection, Loading/End cards and custom easing (request, from screenshots: the vertical bar looked messy, so make it hug its content with equal room per step; make step cards hover, unhover and select; give Loading at the start and the end point their own controls with presets and editable time; add a field to paste an easing value): `scroll-plan-control.tsx` was rebuilt. The page-proportional bar (crowded numbered dots, drag to move) is gone; instead a vertical rail runs down the left of equal cards - Loading, steps 1..n, End - with a numbered dot per card, a line joining them, a moving mark for the playhead inside the current card and amber pins for Interactions Kit items at the time they happen. Cards hover-highlight (CSS) and can be selected: a click on the card (not on its fields) outlines it and jumps the playhead to that step's stop (Loading: middle of the loader, End: its start). Positions are still edited with At (px), by typing, with Ripple; dragging a dot to move a step was dropped with the old bar. Loading card: Duration (s) and Style preset (Spinner, Bar, Dots; `loadingStyle`, drawn by the page script from `__loadingStyle`). End card (`plan.end`): Ending preset (Hold on the bottom, Back to the top (loop), Reload), Hold (s) and, for Back to the top, Travel (s); they add time after the last step (`buildSegments`, the End segment has index = number of steps), which also sets the timeline length. Easing: a Custom… choice in the plan and per-step dropdowns shows a Curve field that takes `cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1)`, four numbers, or ease / ease-in / ease-out / ease-in-out / linear (`parseBezier`, `cubicBezierEase`, x values 0..1); invalid text is not kept and shows the help line; stored as `customEasing` on the plan or step. Old plans without the new fields read with defaults. The page script/project file were rebuilt (`tools/apply_landing.py`) with the three loaders; the existing plan values were kept. Checked in the app on BaseCase Landingpage Motion: rail with equal cards; a click selected step 2 and moved the playhead to 3.01 s; Bar and Dots loaders drew on the canvas and Spinner was restored; a per-step custom curve showed its field and saved; End Back to the top made the timeline 22.8 s, Reload and Hold 21.2 s, and Hold 0 restored 20.7 s; type check and 13 unit tests pass. Not checked: video/GIF export with the new End and loaders.
- Rail scrubbing (request: hovering the rail line should show a ghost playhead, and clicking should bring the playhead there): in `scroll-plan-control.tsx` the rail beside each card (Loading, every step, End) is now a scrub track. A dashed ghost line with its time (for example '3.04 s') follows the pointer down the rail, and a click sets the playhead there with `timeline.setCurrentTime`; each rail maps top to bottom onto its card's time range, the same mapping the moving playhead mark uses, so Play continues from that point. Styles `.ghost`, `.ghostLabel`, pointer cursor on `.rail`. Checked in the app: hovering the rail of a step showed the dashed ghost line and its time; clicking moved the playhead to 5.81 s inside the Service card and the canvas showed that part of the page; type check passes; no automated tests run.
- Card names and a Travel hint (request: explain Travel; double-click a card name to edit it): `scroll-plan-control.tsx` has an `EditableName` on the Loading, step and End cards. Double-click turns the name into a text field (focused and selected); Enter keeps it, Escape drops it, and clearing it or typing the automatic name goes back to the automatic one (stored as an empty string because a removed key is not saved). Names are `name` on a step and `loadingName` / `endName` on the plan (`landing-plan.ts`). A line under the plan summary explains Travel (seconds the scroll takes to reach a step from the one before) and Pause (seconds it waits there). Checked in the app: double-click showed a focused field, 'Features' replaced 'How it Works' on the card and was stored, and typing the automatic name cleared it; type check and 14 unit tests pass; no automated tests run beyond the plan unit tests.
- Label rename (request: a more understandable name for Travel, from a motion designer's point of view): in the Scroll plan the step fields are now Duration (s) (how long the scroll takes to reach the step; was Travel) and Hold (s) (how long it stays there; was Pause), the same words as the Loading card's Duration and the End card's Hold, and the hint line says so. Stored data is unchanged (`travel`, `pause`). Checked in the app; type check passes; no automated tests run.
- Label rename (request: rename Duration to Scroll time): the step field that was Duration (s) is now 'Scroll time (s)' in the Scroll plan, and the hint line uses it; the Loading card's Duration and the End card's fields are unchanged. Stored data unchanged (`travel`). Type check passes; no automated tests run.
- Start card with presets (request: rename Loading to Start; Start and End titles never editable; more presets such as preloaders and intro transitions): the Loading card is now 'Start' (dot S) and its title, like End's, is plain text; only step cards can be renamed (`loadingName` / `endName` were removed again). New `animation/start-screen.ts` holds `START_PRESETS` - Preloaders: Spinner, Progress bar, Bouncing dots, Progress ring, Percent counter, Pulse; Intro transitions: Fade in, Slide up, Zoom in, Blur in, Wipe across, Open from the centre - and their drawing, done by the app: `drawFramedBitmap` (`frame-transform.ts`) takes an optional `start` (preset, progress 0..1 over the Start's Duration, time, colour); preloaders cover the frame with its fill colour and the loader, fading out over the last 12%, intro transitions change how the page picture is drawn (alpha, offset, scale, blur, or a clip). Canvas and export both pass it (`animation-canvas.tsx`, `footer-render.ts`) and the page script now only receives `__scrollY`, so every imported page gets every preset without being re-imported (the old loader branch in the BaseCase script is no longer reached). The plan keeps the preset in `loadingStyle` (now any preset id) plus `startColor` (hex, shown for preloaders); the Start card has Duration, Preset (grouped labels) and Color. New presets are a table entry plus a drawing case, so ideas or prompts from the user can be added. Checked in the app on BaseCase: Start and End titles do not enter edit mode; the dropdown lists 12 presets; Percent counter showed 50% at 0.6 s; Wipe across revealed the page left to right at 0.29 s; the rail click scrubbed to that time; Spinner restored; type check and 13 unit tests pass. Not checked: the other presets visually, video/GIF export of the Start.
- Loopable segments on the Scroll plan rail (request, from a screenshot: the line between two dots should be a segment that shows on hover and when picked, in a stroke colour; a picked segment plays as a loop; several can be picked with Shift-click; a click outside deselects): each card's stretch of rail (dot to the next dot: Start, each step, End) is a segment (`scroll-plan-control.tsx`, `.segment` styles). Hover draws a blue stroke (`--loop: #4c8dff`); a picked segment keeps a thicker glowing stroke with end ticks and its dot ringed. A plain click picks only that segment (and drops it if it was the only one); Shift (or Cmd/Ctrl) click adds or removes; a pick also moves the playhead to the clicked point. Picks live in `landing-loop.ts` (session only, not saved with the project): `segmentRange`, `loopRanges` (joins touching segments), `nextLoopTime`. While the timeline plays, `animation-canvas.tsx` jumps the playhead to the next picked range when it leaves one, and from the last back to the first, so picked segments loop (several loop one after the other). A bar above the cards says 'Looping N segments while it plays' with Clear; a click anywhere outside the rail, that bar, the timeline controls and open menus clears the picks, and so does Escape. The earlier rail click that only scrubbed is now the pick; the hover ghost playhead stays. Shift-click no longer selects text. Segments with no length (Start of 0 s, an End that holds nothing) cannot be picked. Checked in the app: picking steps 1 and 3 showed 'Looping 2 segments'; pressing Play kept them; over 12 s the playhead only visited 1.2-2.2 s and 3.7-5.2 s and wrapped between them; clicking outside cleared them; one segment looped alone; the Start segment drew in blue; type check and 17 unit tests pass.
- Rail click conflict (report: clicking a point on the rail to play from there also picked the segment): the two jobs were split by where you click (`scroll-plan-control.tsx`). The rail cell is wider (38px) with a narrow pick zone over the line itself (`.pick`, 16px): hovering it draws the blue segment stroke and a click there picks the segment to loop (Shift/Cmd/Ctrl adds), without any ghost playhead. Everywhere else in the rail, beside the line, the ghost playhead shows with its time and a click moves the playhead there (`onScrub`) and leaves the picks alone unless the time is outside the picked segments, which lets go of the loop (`scrubFree`). In `animation-canvas.tsx` the loop only wraps when playback runs a small step past the end of a picked segment; if the timeline plays from, or is moved to, a point outside the picked segments (the Play button after moving the playhead, the timeline bar, or a click beside the line) the picks are released instead of pulling the playhead back. Picking a segment still moves the playhead into it. The hint line says 'Click the line to loop a segment (Shift-click to add more). Click beside it to play from there.' Checked in the app: a click beside the line moved the playhead to 4.45 s with no pick; a click on the line picked the segment (playhead 1.69 s); a click beside the line inside the segment kept the loop; one outside released it; with a segment picked, Play from 12.8 s played on 13.1, 13.6, 14.0 and released the loop; playing from inside looped 1.2-2.2 s; type check and 17 unit tests pass. The hover look of the two zones was not checked with a screenshot.
- Scroll plan panel tidy-up (request, from a screenshot: remove the red-marked texts; put the green-marked buttons in a dropdown; is Ripple important?): removed the Scroll time / Hold explanation under the plan summary, the Curve help text under the custom easing field (the line still appears, as an error, when the pasted curve is not valid), and the idle hint about looping (the 'Looping N segments' bar with Clear shows only while segments are picked). The row of buttons (Auto steps, Step, Back, Top, Reload) is now one dropdown, 'Add to the plan': Auto steps - one stop per section; Add a step further down; Add a step back up; Add a scroll to the top; Add a reload; and Ripple: on/off (click to turn it off/on), which replaces the separate Ripple switch row (the setting and its behaviour are unchanged; it is off in the BaseCase plan). The menu goes back to its own label after each choice. Checked in the app: the texts are gone; the menu lists the six choices; a real click on Add a reload made 14 steps / 21.4 s and the step was removed again; Ripple toggled and was restored to off; type check passes; no automated tests run.
- Nested second scroll ('2nd At') per scroll step (request: every scroll card gets another At with its own Scroll time, Delay and individual easing, to overlap scrolls and feel smoother; the second At is nested, always follows its parent, and Hold comes after it ends): `landing-plan.ts` has `PlanStep.second` (null or `{ offset, travel, delay, easing, customEasing }`; stored as null when absent because a removed key is not saved). The second scroll starts `delay` seconds after its step starts, even while the first is still running, and takes over from wherever the page has got to, towards `step.y + offset` (so moving the parent moves it; clamped to the page end), with its own easing (Plan default, any preset, or a pasted curve); the step's Hold begins when both scrolls are over, the next step starts from where the second landed, and the timeline length includes it. `buildSegments(plan, limit)` now also gives each segment `firstEnd`, `final` and `second`; `evaluateScroll` blends the two scrolls without a jump. In `scroll-plan-control.tsx` each scroll card has a '+ 2nd At' button; a nested block then shows 'At (px)' (absolute, edited as an offset from the parent), 'Scroll time (s)', 'Delay (s)', an Easing select with its curve and a custom-curve field, and a remove button. Tests (3 new, 20 in all) cover timing, continuity at the hand-over, hold after the second ends, the next step starting from the second's landing, the target following the parent, clamping and easing fallbacks. Checked in the app: the button adds the block with the defaults (delay 0.3 s, scroll time 0.8 s) and the timeline grows by the extra time; the block layout looks right. Not confirmed in the app: typing a new value into the 2nd At fields end to end, because the plan was being edited in the same pane at the same time; type check passes. Steps 3 and 7 of the BaseCase plan were left with a 2nd At from testing and the user's own edits.
- Relative scroll steps (request: the At value must be how far a step scrolls, never a total position; 400 is a normal step down, -450 a step back): in `landing-plan.ts` a step now stores `scroll` (px from where the page is; positive down, negative up) instead of `y`, and `buildSegments(plan, limit)` adds the steps up, so each step lands at the previous landing + its scroll (clamped to the page, later steps carry on from where it stopped; a Reload goes back to 0; a second scroll's offset is relative to its step's landing). Changing a step therefore moves every step after it by itself, so Ripple and the pin are gone (`ripple`, `pinned` and `moveStep` removed; the Ripple item left the 'Add to the plan' menu, the pin button left the cards). Plans saved with positions (`y`) are converted on reading by taking the difference from where the step before landed (a second scroll's offset counts), so existing projects keep exactly the same motion and are saved in the new form on their next edit. `autoSteps` and `addStep` make deltas (Step down: a screenful; Step back: minus a screenful; Top: minus however far it is; Reload). In `scroll-plan-control.tsx` the field is 'Scroll by (px)' on a step and on its 2nd At (shown as the extra distance past the step's landing, positive or negative); each card shows 'Lands at N px · P% of the way down' and its section name from where it lands. Tests rewritten for the relative model (22 in all, incl. the negative step, clamping, a Reload, conversion of old plans and the second scroll). Checked in the app on BaseCase: the saved plan converted to 0, 832, 1002, 745, 707, 707, 538, 684, 606, 889, 707, 432, 144 landing at the same positions as before (1,834 px for Testimonial and so on); typing -450 on step 5 made it land at 2,129 px and the next step follow at 2,836 px, 400 gave 2,979 px, restoring 707 gave 3,286 px and the same 30.1 s; type check passes.
- Card deselect (request: a picked card could not be deselected): in `scroll-plan-control.tsx` clicking the picked card again drops the pick (without moving the playhead), a click anywhere outside the cards (not on the timeline controls, an open menu or a dialog) drops it, and so does Escape; clicking another card still switches the pick to it. Cards carry `data-plan-card`. Checked in the app: select, click again, switch between cards (one picked at a time), outside click, Escape and pressing Play (keeps the pick) behaved as described; type check passes; no automated tests run.
- Add-to-plan feedback and the Layer message for page sections (report: after choosing from 'Add to the plan' it was unclear what was picked; the Modifier Layer section said 'This layer has no design controls' for a page section, with the question whether the page was imported as images): the menu always reads 'Add to the plan', so each choice now shows a notice under it for 5 s ('Added step 14: a scroll back 707 px', 'Auto steps: 13 steps, one stop per section'), selects the new card and scrolls it into view (`runAction`, `showCard`, `data-plan-index` in `scroll-plan-control.tsx`). `layer-editor.tsx` shows, for a page section layer, where it starts and how tall it is, says it is a flat picture whose text and shapes cannot be edited and that its scrolling is set in the Scroll plan, instead of the 'no design controls' line. The BaseCase page is imported as nine flat pictures (JPEG slices of a Figma export; Figma's tool limit on the user's plan blocked reading real layers), which is why layers have no editable text or shapes; a re-import from Figma's structure would be needed for editable ones. Checked in the app: the notice, the selected card and the scroll into view after adding a step (removed again), and the new text in the Layer section for How it Works; type check passes; no automated tests run.
- Picked segment vanished while playing (report: after selecting in the scroll plan it disappears after some milliseconds): picking a segment while the timeline was playing outside it made the loop effect in `animation-canvas.tsx` run before the playhead had moved into the segment, and its rule that playing from outside a loop releases it threw the new pick away. The effect now notices that the picks just changed (`previousLoopKeys`) and, instead of clearing, moves the playhead into the picked range; the release rule still applies to the playhead being moved or played from outside an existing loop. Checked in the app: playing at 3.3 s, a click on a segment further on kept 'Looping 1 segment' in all 14 samples over 2 s and the playhead looped inside it (4.5-5.9 s); card selection and idle segment picks were also watched frame by frame and stay; type check and 22 unit tests pass.
- Segment pick area and the loop bar (request, from screenshots: only the stroke itself should pick a segment, a click outside the stroke width deselects, no wider reach; remove the red-marked 'Looping N segments / Clear' bar): `.pick` in `scroll-plan-control.module.css` is now exactly the stroke's width (4px, was 16px) and is the only part of the rail marked `data-loop-keep`; a click anywhere else on the rail now lets go of the picked segments (through the outside-click listener in `animation-canvas.tsx`) and still moves the playhead there. The bar above the cards and its Clear button were removed (a click outside or Esc clears). Checked in the app: the pick zone measured 4px wide; a click on the stroke picked the segment and a pointer-down on it kept it; a click on the rail beside it cleared it; no bar showed while a segment was picked; type check passes; no automated tests run.
- Stroke hover (request: hover interaction on the stroke, then: on hover show exactly the same stroke width, and only a click shows the selected look): the hover style never applied after the pick area was split off, because the stroke element came before the pick area in the markup and the CSS rule used a following-sibling selector; `scroll-plan-control.tsx` now puts the stroke after the pick area. Hover shows the stroke at the clickable width (4px, measured equal to the 4px pick area) in full blue with no glow; a picked segment adds the glow and the end ticks, and hovering a picked one changes nothing. Hovering the stroke hides the ghost playhead. Checked in the app with a real hover and a real click: hover 4px / 4px, no shadow; picked 4px with glow and ticks; type check passes; no automated tests run.
- Idle, hover and click width of the rail line (question: what is the idle stroke width; then the decision: idle 2px, hover 4px): the idle grey line is 2px, hovering shows the blue stroke at 4px, and the click area is exactly that 4px (no reach beyond it); a picked segment keeps the 4px stroke and adds the glow and end ticks. In `scroll-plan-control.module.css`. Checked with a real hover: idle 2px, hover 4px blue, click area 4px, no glow, no ghost playhead; type check passes; no automated tests run.
- Agent guide for motion (request: save an agent file so AI agents understand how motion works in this app): added `docs/mo-app/agent-motion-guide.md` (outside the signed `docs/toolcraft` folder). It explains the project and motion types, the Landing Page model (`AnimationSpec.page`, `__scrollY`, the `landing.plan` JSON with each field and how it is timed), how to turn a motion description into a plan, the user's vocabulary, what the panel already does, the traps found so far (persistence drops removed keys, editing an open project's file, shared browser pane, locked select options), how a landing page is imported, how to verify and ship, and what is not built yet. Files check passes; no code changed.

## Verification

Protected receipts own initial/performance proof. Later edits record focused checks and text journal run IDs. Failed attempts and their retries remain separate; no screenshots, videos or binary traces are required by the journal.

## Risks

- Risk: Replace neutral decisions before first product delivery. Historical source revisions or message references that are unknown must remain explicitly unknown.

### Multi-part Start (Scroll plan)
- Request: Start must hold several parts, e.g. a still thumbnail of the page top, then a preloader transition, so the End "top" loop seams into the thumbnail.
- Owner: `landing-plan.ts` (`start: StartPart[]`, derived `loading`, legacy migration, `startAt`, `withStart`, `newStartPart`), `start-screen.ts` (`thumbnail` preset), `frame-transform.ts`, `animation-canvas.tsx`, `footer-render.ts`, `scroll-plan-control.tsx` (+ CSS): per-part rows and "+ Add a part".
- Checks: tsc passes; vitest `src/app/animation/` 24 pass; in the browser pane, set Part 1 thumbnail 1 s and Part 2 bouncing dots 2 s and read both durations back from the UI (summary 26.1 s).
- Not checked: frame-by-frame visual of the preloader fade-in after the thumbnail, export output, per-part looping (not implemented).

### Scroll step cards: duplicate, delete, reorder; menu rename
- Request: rename "Add to the plan" to "Add to scroll step"; every step card (not Start/End) gets duplicate, delete, drag reorder and Up/Down arrow reordering; smaller "+ 2nd At" button.
- Owner: `landing-plan.ts` (`duplicateStep`, `moveStep`), `scroll-plan-control.tsx` (+ CSS): copy button, drag grip, arrow keys on the selected step (ignored while typing in a field).
- Checks: tsc passes; vitest `src/app/animation/` 25 pass.
- Not checked: drag and arrow keys in the browser by hand.

### Step drag: 70% copy, guide, 60% rule
- Request: dragging a step shows a snapping guide, the card at 70% following the cursor, and only reorders when it covers 60% of another card.
- Owner: `scroll-plan-control.tsx` (`startDrag`, pointer-based, replaces HTML5 drag), CSS grip.
- Checks: tsc passes. Not checked by hand in the browser.

### BaseCase page re-imported as editable layers (not flat pictures)
- Request: the Figma page must arrive as real, editable layers, not flat images.
- Cause: the first import used PNG slices because the Figma MCP was over its call limit (still is). Now the frame is exported from the user's Chrome as SVG with Outline text off, so text stays text.
- How: `tools/build_layered.py` (in the project folder) turns Basecase.svg into 9 section groups with 32 child layers (Figma names), shared defs per section, shrunk images and the Aeonik/Urbanist fonts; `tools/apply_layered.py` writes the animation script (each section is drawn from its on layers as an SVG picture cached per section) into the project. `animation-sandbox.ts` now awaits `render`, so scripts can draw asynchronously. Text edits replace the tspan text; the probe lists each layer's real strings so the Text section works.
- Checks: every section SVG parses; sections rendered in a test page at several scroll positions; opened in the app, Layers shows Hero > Background/BG/Shape/hero content/Nav, Text edit changed the heading live and was reverted; tsc passes, 25 tests pass.
- Limits: Editor's Note and Open Sauce One fonts are not installed (fallback fonts, so the italic words are a little wider); angular gradients (HTML inside the SVG) are left out; shapes are editable as layers (show/hide, text) but not per-shape colours yet; layers are the Figma groups one level below each section.

### BaseCase layers split down to individual layers (via Pencil + SVG)
- Request: import the design from Pencil; the layers must be proper and editable.
- Findings: the Pencil MCP only reaches the Pen app once Pen.app is open with the file; the `.pen` file (Basecase, same design) is plain JSON with auto layout, so positions are not stored. Its layer names equal the Figma/SVG names, so the SVG stays the source for geometry and the layer hierarchy is taken from the SVG groups.
- Change: `tools/build_layered.py` now walks each section into nested groups and leaf layers (a group is opened up only when it has no attributes of its own, so filters, clips and transforms stay intact): 374 leaf layers and 254 groups. `tools/apply_layered.py` writes the nested Layers tree and removes layers from earlier imports (the old step left duplicates).
- Checks: layer ids unique (628); the app shows Frame > Hero > hero content > ... nested; hiding the Footer "BaseCase" layer removed it from the page and showing it brought it back; Text section lists the layer's text.
- Not checked: export of the new layers; per-shape colour edits; a very large project file (16 MB) on slow disks.

### BaseCase imported from the Pencil HTML + Tailwind export (same to same)
- Request: import the design from the Pencil export (`Basecase-export.html`) instead of the SVG.
- How: `tools/build_from_html.py` shrinks the embedded images and serves a slim copy; `tools/html_extract.js` runs in the browser, lets Tailwind build its CSS, and posts back each section's XHTML, the CSS, the Google font files and the named-element tree (1288 layers). `tools/apply_html.py` writes the project: every named element is a layer (groups where it has named parts); each section is drawn from its real HTML + CSS inside an SVG foreignObject and cached as a picture (hidden layers get `visibility:hidden`, edited texts replace the text in the markup). Drawing HTML into the canvas does not taint it (checked), so pixel reads still work.
- Checks: the page matches the original HTML in the browser (same cyan wave); in the app Layers shows Frame > Hero > BG/Shape/hero content/Nav and the other sections; selecting the Footer "BaseCase" text layer shows the Text section; an edit ("Mo Edited") appeared in the footer and the original was restored; Scroll plan and Start parts kept. 1296 layer ids, all unique.
- Limits: Editor's Note, Circular Std and Open Sauce One are not installed (fallback fonts); Aeonik is embedded from this Mac; selecting a group shows no Text section (select the text layer itself); the old SVG import is still in `tools/` (`apply_layered.py`).

### How it Works cards drawn as pictures
- Request: the three step cards had broken text and a missing background; import each card as an image (the backgrounds were given in `00. Resource/How it works`).
- Cause: the italic font (Editor's Note) is not installed, so the HTML card text broke; the export also turns each card element 90 degrees.
- Change: `tools/apply_html.py` replaces the content of the three card elements with a picture of the card as Figma drew it (cropped from `Basecase-page.png`, 2x, `tools/cards/step-0N.jpg`), turned back to upright; the cards' parts are removed from the Layers list and each card is one layer ("Step N card (picture)"). Layers 1296 -> 1120, all unique.
- Checks: opened the project from the file; the three cards show the right text, icons and lavender background and the Scroll plan is unchanged.
- Limits: the pictures are 1x crops enlarged 2x, so they are a little soft at high export sizes; a sharper version needs a new Figma export (Chrome was not connected). The `step 0N.png` backgrounds in the resource folder were not used separately because the card pictures already include that background. "BaseCase Landingpage Motion 2" (the in-app copy) was not changed.

### How it Works cards back to editable layers, with the supplied backgrounds
- Request: the cards must be editable layers (not pictures), with the background images from `00. Resource/How it works`.
- Findings: Pencil's export had already lost the italic styling of one word per card (rich text is flattened), and the Tailwind builder skipped the Editor's Note font class because its name has an apostrophe; Pencil's own PNG export uses fallback fonts too, so it is no better for text.
- Change (`tools/apply_html.py`): each card keeps all its parts as layers; its old swirl/blur/noise parts are replaced by one "Card background" layer (the supplied `step 0N.png`, turned back upright); the italic words ("Subscribe", "deliverables", "satisfied.") are restored as italic spans, drawn with Instrument Serif Italic as a stand-in for Editor's Note, and edit as separate texts; the missing font rule for the Editor's Note class is added. Layers 1284, all unique.
- Also fixed: `scripts/project-autosave.mjs` refused projects over 8 MB (413), so this project never saved to disk; the limit is now 96 MB (dev server restarted).
- Checks: all nine section markups parse; the project reopened from its file shows the three cards with the backgrounds, text and italic words; autosave reported "Last saved".
- Not checked: the italic word sits a little small next to the body text (Instrument Serif has a smaller x-height; a font-size bump would fix it); the stand-in is not the real Editor's Note.

### Cards update applied to "BaseCase Landingpage Motion 2"
- Request: the user was working in the copy "BaseCase Landingpage Motion 2" (it still had the old swirl cards); add the supplied card backgrounds there.
- Change: ran `tools/apply_html.py` on `Recovered/BaseCase Landingpage Motion 2.json` (backup kept in the session scratch folder before running); the italic words now get a slightly larger size and a small right margin so they no longer touch the next word. The original project file was not re-run with this size tweak.
- Checks: the "2" project reopened from its file shows the three cards on the supplied lavender backgrounds, no swirl, italic words spaced; autosave reports saved.
- Note: a leftover layer selection draws a purple outline over the third card in the editor; it is not part of the design.

### "BaseCase Landingpage Motion 2": card backgrounds by step number + italic words fitted
- Request: use the supplied card backgrounds (step 01/02/03 into cards 1/2/3), remove the card's own background parts, keep the card art and text; match the reference image of the section.
- Change: `tools/build_layered.py` removes the first seven background parts of each How it works card and puts the supplied picture (`tools/cards/bg-0N.jpg`) in the card's box; cards are now layers "Step N card"; italic words set in a stand-in font are squeezed (`textLength`) into the width the design gave them so they no longer touch the next word (7 words). `apply_layered.py` replaces picture placeholders in layer markup. Applied to the "2" project (SVG layers version); its file had been overwritten with stale data once by autosave, so it was re-applied after switching away from it.
- Checks: "2" reopened from its file shows the three cards on the supplied backgrounds without the swirl, "Subscribe to a plan or start a special project today" spaced correctly; file re-read after autosave still has the update.
- Not exact: the italic words use Georgia Bold Italic, not Editor's Note, so letter shapes differ from the reference (needs the font file).

### Why card changes kept reverting (found and fixed)
- Symptom: after updating "BaseCase Landingpage Motion 2" the old swirl cards came back after a reload and autosave overwrote the new file.
- Cause: the app keeps all state in one localStorage entry (45+ MB) that includes a shared animation library keyed by animation id; imports reused one id and deleted projects left their library entries, so the entry outgrew what the browser would save and later saves failed silently; on reload the last successful (old) state was restored and autosaved over the file.
- Fix: unused library entries removed from the stored state; `apply_layered.py` and `apply_html.py` now give every import a fresh animation id; the project was reopened from its updated file; verified after a reload that the cards keep the supplied backgrounds and the file on disk still holds the update.
- Remaining risk: unreferenced library entries still accumulate with each import (not pruned by the app); prune them (or keep imports few) if saving starts failing again.

### Duplicate project removed
- Request: keep the updated version, delete the other file with the same name permanently.
- Done: kept "BaseCase Landingpage Motion 2" (card backgrounds, italic fix); removed "BaseCase Landingpage Motion" from the app and deleted `Recovered/BaseCase Landingpage Motion.json` from disk (not the Trash). After a reload only the "2" project remains in the list. The older saves in the project's resource folder (`before-import`, `before-layers`, the Oct 5 `.json`) were not touched.

### Rail segment click: playhead goes to the segment's start
- Request: clicking a (blue) segment on the Scroll plan rail made the playhead land near the segment's end.
- Cause: the click put the playhead at the clicked point and, while playing, it ran out the rest of the segment and wrapped.
- Change: `scroll-plan-control.tsx` `toggle` now moves the playhead to the start of the picked segment (`segmentRange`), not the clicked time; deselecting a segment is unchanged. Clicking beside the line still plays from that point.
- Checks: tsc passes; while playing, a click on the Service segment jumped the playhead to 6.40 s (that segment's start) and it looped from there. A second click on the same segment still drops the pick.

### Correction: segment click goes to the END of the stroke
- The user meant the opposite of the previous entry: clicking a rail segment (the stroke) should put the playhead at its bottom end.
- Change: `toggle` in `scroll-plan-control.tsx` now scrubs to `range[1] - 0.02` (a hair inside the end so the pick still loops), not the start or the clicked point.
- Checks: tsc passes; with the playhead at 2.38 s, one click on the Service segment (6.4 s - 7.9 s) put it at 7.88 s.

### Arrow keys on the Scroll plan number fields, with a smooth live preview
- Request: arrow-key control on "Scroll by" (and the other numbers) without lag in the live preview.
- Change: new `NumberField` in `scroll-plan-control.tsx` wraps the number inputs (Start part Duration; step Scroll by / Scroll time / Hold; 2nd At Scroll by / Scroll time / Delay; End Duration / Hold). Up/Down nudge by 10 px or 0.1 s, Shift by ten times that, Alt by a tenth; typing, Enter and blur work as before. Each nudge shows on the canvas through a draft plan (`landing-draft.ts`, read in `animation-canvas.tsx`) and the plan is only saved after 250 ms of rest, so holding a key does not write the large project state on every repeat; the timeline length follows the saved plan only.
- Checks: tsc passes; focused Service step Scroll by 745, Up x3 gave 775 and the saved plan updated (Lands at changed); Down x3 returned it to 745.
- Not checked: Shift / Alt steps by hand; visual smoothness while holding a key for several seconds.

### Rail: segment end lines up with the next step number
- Request: the bottom end of a segment did not match the next step's number.
- Cause: the stroke runs from one dot's centre to the next dot's centre, but time (the playhead mark, the ghost line and the click position) was mapped over the card's own height only, so the end of a segment sat about 20 px above the next dot; the click area also ended 20 px short.
- Change (`scroll-plan-control.tsx`, `.module.css`): time is mapped over the stroke length (14 px below the card top to 8 px past its bottom; the last card has no stroke below); marks, ghost and click use that; the click area now reaches the next dot.
- Checks: tsc passes; measured the stroke end at the next dot's centre (card bottom + 22 px); after a click at the end of step 2's segment the playhead mark sat 3 px from step 3's number (the 0.02 s kept inside the loop).

### Deleting a step no longer shifts the steps after it
- Report: after deleting a step card, "How it Works" showed twice (and other names doubled; the last step fell off the page).
- Cause: step scrolls are relative to the step before, so removing one moved every later step up the page by the removed distance.
- Change: `removeStep(plan, index, limit)` in `landing-plan.ts` gives the removed distance to the next step, so every remaining step lands where it did; the panel passes the page's scroll limit. New unit test (26 tests pass; tsc passes).
- Test mishap: my live test deleted a step and the undo button did not bring it back (plan edits are not undoable from that button; not investigated). The plan had also lost two steps to earlier deletions, so I put the whole step list back from the last full snapshot (14 steps, 25.5 s); Start parts, easing and End were left as they were.
- Not checked live: the fixed delete in the app (covered by the unit test only).

### Cleanup for speed
- Request: remove cache files because the app felt slow.
- Cause: the app saves its whole state in one browser entry (47.9 MB); two old BaseCase drawings (about 31 MB) were no longer used by any project but were rewritten on every save.
- Done: removed the two unused drawings from that entry (now 17.2 MB, project and plan intact after a reload); deleted my temporary files (`/tmp/x` scratch backups, the HTML import's temp folder, a stray `real.html` in the app folder). A snapshot of the 14-step plan state was kept in the project's resource folder. Left alone: `node_modules/.vite` (36 MB dev cache; only slows the next start if removed), `dist`, and the saved project files.
- Remaining slowness: each project file is about 16 MB because its images are embedded; fewer or smaller images would make saves faster.

### Arrow step for "Scroll by" is 1 px
- Request: the Scroll by arrow keys moved 10 px at a time.
- Change: `scroll-plan-control.tsx` step for both Scroll by fields (step and 2nd At) is now 1 px (Shift 10 px, Alt stays 1 px because the field is whole pixels); time fields stay 0.1 s.

### Focusing "Scroll by" moves the playhead to the end of that step
- Request: when the Scroll by input is clicked, the playhead should jump to the end of the segment.
- Change: `NumberField` takes `onFocusField`; both Scroll by fields (step and 2nd At) call `showStepEnd(segment)`, which scrubs to the segment end minus 0.02 s (same point as clicking the stroke), so the result of the edit is on screen while the arrow keys nudge it.
- Checks: tsc passes; clicking the Scroll by field of the Testimonial step (6.4 s - 7.9 s) put the playhead at 7.88 s.

### Report: deleted step card returns after reload (not reproduced)
- Tests on "BaseCase Landingpage Motion 2": delete a duplicated step then reload after 2.5 s and after 0.12 s; delete a real step (the last one) and reload. In all three the step stayed deleted and the stored plan matched. The saved state is written about 0.3 s after a change.
- Likely earlier cause: before the storage cleanup the app state (48 MB) did not fit and saves failed silently, so deletes came back after a reload.
- Side effect of testing: the last step ("Footer", 707 px, 0.8 s scroll, 0.7 s hold) was deleted and re-created with a new id and the same values; the plan is back to 13 steps.
- Open: if it still happens, the exact steps are needed (which card, which button, how long before reloading, which project).

### Draggable playhead on a step's rail
- Request: a drag control for the playhead inside a step.
- Change: the playhead mark on a step card's rail (`scroll-plan-control.tsx` / `.module.css`) is now a handle: a small knob and a taller grab area, `ns-resize` cursor; dragging it up and down the stroke scrubs time through that step (same mapping as the stroke, so it follows the pointer exactly). It stops just before the step's end; dropping past the bottom lands on the next step's start. Pointer capture keeps the drag going outside the stroke.
- Checks: tsc passes; dragging the mark up in the Service step moved it with the pointer (6.61 s); dragging it far down ended at 7.9 s, the next step's start.

### Playhead drag no longer locks at a step number
- Report: dragging the playhead mark stopped (snapped and locked) when it reached the next step's number.
- Cause: the drag lived on the mark of one card; when the playhead moved into the next card the mark was removed with its pointer capture, so the drag ended.
- Change (`scroll-plan-control.tsx`): the drag is now handled for the whole rail by window pointer listeners started from the mark (`startMarkDrag`): the pointer's height is mapped to the card under it (Start, each step, End) and its time range, so the playhead follows the pointer through all steps, forward and backward; the point where the pointer is released counts too. The knob grows while dragging.
- Checks: tsc passes; real drags: step 3 down to step 6 gave 10.19 s with the mark under the pointer; step 6 back up to step 4 gave 7.11 s. Scripted pointer events also crossed cards in both directions.

### The playhead handle takes priority over the strokes
- Request: hovering or dragging the playhead handle must not hover or select the stroke segment; away from the handle the strokes work as before.
- Change (`scroll-plan-control.tsx` / `.module.css`): the handle swallows its own pointer-move and click, so the rail's hover line and the stroke stay quiet while the pointer is on it; during a drag the stack carries `data-mark-drag`, which turns the strokes' click areas off and hides their hover stroke (picked ones stay shown) and the rail's hover line is not drawn; the click that follows letting go is swallowed once, so nothing is picked or played under the pointer.
- Checks: tsc passes; pointer on the handle: stroke opacity 0, not hovered, no hover line; pointer just beside it: stroke hover shows (opacity 1); after a real drag across steps nothing was selected and the drag flag was cleared; a click on a stroke afterwards selected it, a second click deselected it.

### Step cards are named by number
- Request: step cards get a number-wise default name ("Scroll Step 01") that the user can change.
- Change (`scroll-plan-control.tsx`): the default name of a step card is now "Scroll Step NN" (a reload step: "Reload Step NN"), following the card's position, so it renumbers on delete, duplicate and reorder; a name typed by the user (double-click) still wins, and clearing it goes back to the numbered one. The section the step lands in moved to the "Lands at ..." line.
- Checks: tsc passes; after a reload the cards read Scroll Step 01, 02, 04, 06-11; two cards keep names saved earlier ("Testimonial", "Service_02"); "Lands at 2,579 px · 32% of the way down · Service".

### "Scroll by" stops at the page end
- Request: once the scroll reaches the end of the page, the Scroll by input should stop adding.
- Change (`scroll-plan-control.tsx`): a step's Scroll by is limited to what the page has left (page scroll limit minus where the step before landed) and to the top going up; the 2nd At offset likewise from where its step lands. Typing is clamped to that range; Up never goes past the bottom limit, Down never past the top one; a value already past a limit (older plans) is left alone until moved back.
- Checks: tsc passes; on a temporary copy of the last step (page bottom reached) a stored 984 stayed at 984 on Up, Down moved one step at a time, Up from -1 stopped at 0, and typing 500 gave 0 with "Lands at 7,993 px · 100%"; the test copy was removed (13 cards again).

### "Scroll by" renamed "Scroll At"
- The step field and the 2nd At field labelled "Scroll by (px)" are now "Scroll At (px)" (`scroll-plan-control.tsx`); the agent guide's vocabulary list uses the new name. No behaviour change.

### Motion blur on the scroll, with a preview-only switch
- Request: motion blur while the page scrolls, with a control that turns it off in the canvas preview only; a render must still have it.
- Change: the plan has `blur: { amount, preview }` (`landing-plan.ts`; default 30 and on; `motionBlurLength` turns the scroll speed into a smear length in page pixels: amount 100 is a shutter of 1/30 s). `drawFramedBitmap` (`frame-transform.ts`) smears the page picture straight up and down by that length (copies averaged in a scratch canvas, clipped to the frame so the window edges stay sharp). The canvas (`animation-canvas.tsx`) asks with `forPreview = true`, so the "Preview blur" switch hides it there; the render (`footer-render.ts`) always asks with `false`. The Scroll plan panel has "Motion blur" (0-100, arrow keys, 0 = off) and "Preview blur" above the steps. It works for every Landing Page project because it is applied by the app, not by the page's drawing script.
- Checks: tsc passes; 27 tests pass (including the blur length: zero when off or still, doubles with the amount, preview switch only affects the preview); on the canvas, paused mid-scroll (7.06 s), the page is smeared vertically with the window edges sharp; with Preview blur off the same moment is sharp; the switch was turned back on.
- Not checked: an actual video/image export with blur (only the code path and the unit test); the look at very high amounts.

### Number fields take sums (+ - * /)
- Request: an equation in the Scroll At input.
- Change: `evaluateExpression` in `landing-plan.ts` (a small safe parser, no eval): numbers with + - * / (or x), brackets, a leading minus; a leading + * or / works on the current value ("+50" adds 50, "*2" doubles). `toNumber` in `scroll-plan-control.tsx` uses it, so every number field in the Scroll plan (Scroll At, Scroll time, Hold, Delay, Duration, Motion blur) evaluates on Enter or blur and shows the result; the arrow keys also start from a typed sum. Anything that does not read as a number is ignored (the old value stays).
- Checks: tsc passes; 30 tests pass (sums, precedence, brackets, negatives, leading operators, divide by zero, unbalanced brackets, "1 2" rejected).
- Mishap: my live test typed into the panel while the user was editing it and the panel had scrolled, so the text went into another field. The step 2 "2nd At" Scroll At now reads 10 and may be my test value (120/12); the original value is unknown. Live testing of this feature was stopped; it is covered by the unit tests only.

### Every number field in a step card jumps the playhead
- Request: going into Scroll At or the 2nd At inputs must move the playhead.
- Change (`scroll-plan-control.tsx`): all step fields (Scroll At, Scroll time, Hold) and all 2nd At fields (Scroll At, Scroll time, Delay) now move the playhead to the end of the step's segment (a hair inside it) when they get focus, and also when an already focused field is clicked (pointer down), not only for the first Scroll At as before.
- Checks: tsc passes; with the playhead at step 6 (12.71 s), a click on step 3's Scroll At moved it to 7.38 s (step 3's end); a click on step 3's 2nd At Delay put it at 7.38 s too (a step with a 2nd At ends after both scrolls and the Hold).

### The playhead follows every edit of a step field
- Request: when the px (Scroll At) input is used the playhead did not jump.
- Change (`scroll-plan-control.tsx`): besides jumping on focus/click, every step and 2nd At number field now sends the plan as it would be after each change (each arrow press and each typed value, `onEdit`); `followStep` puts the playhead at the end of that step in that plan (so it follows a Scroll time or Hold change as well, and returns if the playhead had been moved away).
- Checks: tsc passes; with the playhead at step 3's end (7.38 s) an arrow press kept it at 7.38 s (a px change does not move the end of a step). The pane was being edited by the user at the same time (steps changed under the test), so the Scroll time / Hold follow was not observed.
- Open: if "px wise" meant something else (for example the playhead should land where the page is at that many pixels), say so.

### Each step field jumps to its own moment
- Report: clicking Scroll At, then 2nd At, then Scroll At again showed no playhead jump, because every field sent the playhead to the same place (the end of the step).
- Change (`scroll-plan-control.tsx`): `momentOf`/`showStepMoment`/`followStep` — Scroll At and Scroll time go to where the first scroll lands, the 2nd At's Scroll At and Scroll time to where the second scroll lands, Delay to where the second scroll starts, Hold to the end of the step (all kept a hair inside the step); with no 2nd At they fall back to where the motion ends. Edits (arrow keys, typed values) follow the same moment in the edited plan.
- Checks: tsc passes; on a step starting at 5.4 s (Scroll time 0.8, 2nd At delay 0.3 / scroll 1, Hold 0.7): focusing step Scroll At / Scroll time gave 6.2 s, Hold 7.38 s, 2nd At Scroll At / Scroll time 6.7 s, Delay 5.7 s; real clicks Scroll At -> 2nd At Scroll At -> Scroll At gave 6.2 -> 6.7 -> 6.2.

### Selecting a step card puts the playhead at its segment end
- Request: when a step is selected the playhead goes to the end of its segment.
- Change (`scroll-plan-control.tsx`): `onSelect` of a step card scrubs to `segment.end - 0.02` (it was where the motion lands); the Start and End cards are unchanged.
- Checks: tsc passes; selecting steps 3, 4 and 5 put the playhead at 7.38, 9.38 and 11.38 s (the end of each step).

### A second plan-level easing: "2nd plan default"
- Request: another easing next to the plan Easing, to be the default of the 2nd At scrolls.
- Change: the plan has `secondEasing` and `secondCustomEasing` (`landing-plan.ts`; a plan without them reads as equal to its Easing, so old projects move exactly as before). A 2nd At that follows the plan ("2nd plan default", the first option of its own Easing select) uses them; steps still use the plan Easing. The panel has "2nd At easing" (with its curve preview and a pasted curve field for Custom) under the plan Easing; a 2nd At's own curve preview and Curve field follow the new default.
- Checks: tsc passes; 31 tests pass (a plan saved without it reads secondEasing = easing; with it set to linear the 2nd scroll uses linear while the step keeps "out"); the panel shows "Easing: Ease out" and "2nd At easing: Custom…" with its curve (the user was already using it).

### New Scroll plans start with the user's two easings
- Request: save the Easing (Custom 0.68, 0, 0.2, 1) and the 2nd At easing (Custom 0, 0, 0.42, 0.82) as the defaults.
- Change (`landing-plan.ts`): a new plan (no saved plan) starts with Easing = Custom 0.68, 0, 0.2, 1 and 2nd At easing = Custom 0, 0, 0.42, 0.82 (`DEFAULT_CUSTOM_EASING`, `DEFAULT_SECOND_CUSTOM_EASING`; it was Lenis). Saved plans keep their own values, and an older plan with no second easing still starts equal to its Easing, so no existing motion changes. Guide updated.
- Checks: tsc passes; 32 tests pass (a blank plan reads the two custom curves).

### The "Lands at" line of a step card
- Request: drop the section name and "of the way down" (say "step down"); when a step has a 2nd At, show the step's total duration without the Hold.
- Change (`scroll-plan-control.tsx`): the line reads "Lands at 1,747 px · 22% step down"; with a 2nd At it ends with " · Total 1.3 s", the time from the step's start until both scrolls are done (Hold not included). The unused section lookup was removed from the imports.
- Checks: tsc passes; cards read e.g. "Lands at 912 px · 11% step down · Total 1.3 s" (steps with a 2nd At) and "Lands at 0 px · 0% step down" (a step without).

### Section animation (Hero entry and loops), saved per section name
- Request: a controller for each section's entry motion, kept under the section's name; for the Hero, after the preloader ends: bg colour fades in; the shape blurs, scales and slides in from the bottom; the navbar slides in from the top; the heading comes in line by line (blur, slide from bottom); the "cycle" floats and rotates in a loop; sub text blur + slide from bottom; the button and "Available Now" together, blur + slide from bottom; the dot ellipses of "Available Now" loop; everything staggered 0.05 s.
- Change: new "Section animation" section in the Motion tab (`section-motion-control.tsx`, value `landing.sections`, keyed by section name, saved with the project): pick a section, switch Entry motion, choose when it starts (after the Start / when it comes into view), the stagger, and per layer: fade, blur, scale from, slide direction and distance, loop (float, rotate, float + rotate, pulse), time, delay and "Same time" as the layer above. `landing-motion.ts` works out each layer's look per frame (`allFx`), passed as `params.__fx` from the canvas (`animation-canvas.tsx`) and the render (`footer-render.ts`); the page script (`tools/apply_layered.py`) draws the moving layers one by one (each from a picture of just its box) over the section picture. A "Hero preset" button sets the whole Hero as described (13 layers); it is applied in this project and saved.
- Checks: tsc passes; 39 tests pass (stagger and "same time", entry/loop maths, start trigger, thumbnail rule, safe reading); offline the script draws moving layers with blur/offset/opacity; in the app, "Section animation" lists Hero, the preset fills 13 items and the file on disk has `landing.sections` (Hero, enabled); scrubbing step 1: at 2.70 s the nav is dropping in, heading lines are blurred and rising, the wave rises blurred, the bg fades in, button and sub text not yet started; at 3.88 s the Hero is fully in.
- Not checked: the float / rotate / pulse loops by eye, the "when it comes into view" trigger on other sections, a video export with the motion; the first frame of an entry builds each moving layer's picture, which can show a brief blank frame.
- Note: the Start thumbnail shows the finished Hero; during preloader parts the preloader covers the page.

### Moving layers keep their blend mode (Hero shape)
- Finding: the Hero "Shape" is a picture (a bitmap render of the blue wave, 4096 x 2296 at source, kept at 1600 px wide in the project) laid with soft-light and colour-dodge blending; "image 4" is the background picture. Drawn on its own for the entry motion, the shape lost its soft-light blend and looked too saturated.
- Change (`tools/apply_layered.py`): a moving layer's own outer `mix-blend-mode` is read from its markup and used as the canvas blend mode when it is drawn, so it blends with the layers under it as in the design. Applied to the "2" project (careful swap; plan and Hero motion kept).
- Checks: after reopening, the Hero at 0 s shows the soft lavender wave of the design instead of the saturated blue one.

- Cause: the section shows for layers that hold images, found through the spec's `imageAssets`, which I had left empty for this page.
- Change: `build_layered.py` records which pictures each layer paints with (`images`, following patterns to the image elements, plus the supplied card/background pictures) and makes pictures cover their box; `apply_layered.py` writes them as the spec's `layerImages`; `getLayerImageAssets` (`animation-spec.ts`) uses them; `layer-images.tsx` shows each picture with "Replace" (click, choose a file): the file replaces the picture under the same key and the spec version is bumped so the drawing reloads. 21 layers have pictures (Hero image 4, Shape, Nav logo, photos, card and section backgrounds...).
- Checks: tsc passes; selecting the Hero Shape layer shows "Layer files" with its wave picture and Replace; re-uploading the same picture through the control bumped the project to version 3 and was saved to the file.
- Also: `mo-app-sidebar.tsx` now drops animation-library entries that no project uses whenever a project file is opened (they were piling up to 50 MB+, which made saving silently fail; found again while testing); the old drawings in the store were cleared and the saved state is back to 17.9 MB with saving working.

### Fix: "Rendered fewer hooks than expected" on the Layer files control
- Report: the app showed "Something went wrong - Rendered fewer hooks than expected".
- Cause: in `layer-images.tsx` I added a `useState` (`replacing`) after the early `return null` for a layer without pictures, so selecting a layer without pictures after one with pictures changed the number of hooks.
- Change: the hook is declared with the others, before any early return. Checked the other components added this session (`NumberField`, `PlanEditor`, `SectionMotionControl`): their hooks are all before any return.
- Checks: tsc passes; after a reload, selecting the Hero Shape (pictures), then Background (none), then Shape again showed Layer files with the wave picture and no error.

### MacBook display mask
- Request: fit the animated frame into the supplied MacBook display while keeping its camera visible. `backdrop.ts` recognizes the uploaded 1600×1200 MacBook composition from bezel and camera pixels and maps its display rectangle through the same cover crop used by the mockup. `frame-transform.ts` replaces the baked screen image within that mask and fits the live frame to it; `animation-canvas.tsx` and `footer-render.ts` share the behavior for preview and Render. The current project frame position was centered at X/Y 0 through the UI. Focused checks: TypeScript typecheck and the frame size test passed; live browser inspection at 100% and 50% Scale showed the camera, menu bar and bezel unobscured. Other uploads retain ordinary background behavior.

### Optional four-pin perspective fit
- Request: "give me four pin controler for skew" and allow pins when Mockup is selected. This is a canvas-owned direct spatial edit of `animation.frame`; the existing Frame Settings custom editor owns only the complementary enable/reset mode. Built-ins checked: Vector represents one stable two-axis value but cannot manipulate four visible frame corners; Orientation Gizmo owns 3D model orbit; collection controls would duplicate the canvas operation. Decision: reuse the public Switch for `Perspective pins`, compose four local canvas hit targets, and store normalized corner offsets in the existing runtime frame record. Preview and Render consume the same perspective mapping; handles stay out of output.
- Interaction ownership: user request selects Canvas for four-corner movement because each handle maps directly to a visible screen corner. Panel owns only enabling the perspective edit. The panel does not expose mirrored corner coordinates.
- Visual map: each pin is a local canvas fallback because no public control supplies an overlay corner drag target. Its resting ring uses `--toolcraft-custom-viz-line-strong`, surface fill uses `--toolcraft-custom-viz-surface`, center dot uses `--toolcraft-custom-viz-data`, hover/focus uses `--toolcraft-custom-viz-ink`, and active drag uses `--accent`. Pins are small repeated markers; direction is encoded by their corner position and connecting quadrilateral, not color.
- Implementation plan: extend the frame value reader; render and keyboard-enable four overlay pins only for Mockup + enabled perspective; warp the masked frame through a subdivided Canvas 2D mesh in live preview and Render; add focused math/state tests and inspect drag behavior in the browser. Risk: strong perspective requires enough mesh subdivisions to avoid visible seams.
- Result: Frame Settings shows `Perspective pins` only while Background is Mockup. Enabling it places four draggable, keyboard-adjustable pins at the detected display corners and draws a guide quadrilateral. Each pin moves its corner independently; the live frame and Render share the same subdivided perspective warp and stay clipped to the display mask. `Reset pins` restores the exact detected screen rectangle while keeping the pins enabled.
- Focused checks: `npm run typecheck` passed; `frame-transform.test.ts` and `design-bounds.test.ts` passed (3 assertions across default fit, independent offsets, stored-value clamping, and scale-fit regression). In the live in-app browser, all four labeled pin buttons were visible at the MacBook display corners; moving the top-left pin with ArrowRight changed its canvas position from 338 px to 342.595 px, and Reset pins restored it to 338 px. Final visual inspection showed all four pins aligned to the screen bezel with the camera/menu-bar area preserved.

### Mouse selection for perspective pins
- Request: add mouse interaction to show, hide, and select perspective pins. Changed `perspective-pins.tsx` and its local CSS: with Perspective pins enabled, clicking inside the fitted display reveals the guide and handles; clicking a handle selects it and adds the accent state; clicking the mockup canvas outside the fitted display hides the editor. Direct pin movement from this batch was replaced by the selected-pin pad in the next batch.
- Focused checks: `npm run typecheck` and the 3 frame tests passed. Live browser proof at 206% canvas zoom confirmed the screen click revealed four handles, clicking top-left set `aria-pressed=true`, and clicking outside the display removed all four handles. No project values were changed during the selection check.

### Selected pin controller and movement pad
- Request: put perspective controls at the bottom, provide one control for selecting each pin and a second draggable pad for moving the selected pin, and keep the fill color from skewing. Frame Settings now ends with Perspective pins, Reset, the TL/TR/BR/BL selector, and the public 2D Vector pad. Canvas pins select corners only; the panel pad is the single owner of movement, so the canvas and panel do not mirror the same operation. A small shared UI selection store keeps canvas selection and the panel selector synchronized without persisting editor-only selection in project values.
- Rendering: the rectangular frame fill is now painted directly into the detected mockup viewport; only the content/stroke raster passes through the perspective mesh. Pin movement therefore reveals the flat fill instead of skewing the fill polygon.
- Focused checks: `npm run typecheck` and the 3 frame tests passed. In the live browser, TR selection followed by a Vector-pad drag changed its X/Y values to 0.40/0.23 and visibly skewed the content over a rectangular fill; Reset returned both values to 0.00. Clicking the canvas TL pin then selected TL in the panel while its value remained 0.00. Final project pin offsets are reset.

### Perspective mockup fill cleanup
- Request: the visible Fill behind a skewed frame was distracting, and question whether the Fill control is needed in Frame Settings. Decision: Fill remains useful for ordinary frames, but a perspective mockup has a real screen backing and should not expose an authored frame color. While Mockup + Perspective pins is active, the Fill switch/color are absent and the renderer uses a neutral white viewport backing behind the warped page.
- Focused checks: `npm run typecheck` and the 3 frame tests passed. Live inspection moved TL to X 0.04 / Y 0.03 and confirmed the exposed wedge stayed white while the content skewed; Fill was absent and the selected-pin pad remained present. Reset restored TL to X/Y 0.00.

### Perspective background follows the frame
- Correction from the user: the fixed backing itself was the bug; the frame background/fill must follow the pin transform. Root cause: `drawPerspectiveFramedBitmap` painted a rectangular viewport backing before applying the four-corner mesh to only the page raster. Moving a corner inward therefore exposed the fixed rectangle.
- Fix: Fill and Fill color are restored because they belong to the frame plane. The frame fill is painted into the same scratch source as the page and passes through the same mesh. Outside the moved quadrilateral, four narrow edge-extension bands stretch the current frame's own top/right/bottom/left edge pixels to the detected mockup viewport; this covers the baked screen without a separate white/grey wedge and avoids a full extra render pass.
- Focused checks: `npm run typecheck` and the 3 frame tests passed before the final one-pixel edge refinement. Live browser proof at TL X 0.32 / Y 0.23 showed the Hero gradient and wave continuing to all screen edges while the content skewed; the former solid wedge was absent. Reset restored TL to X/Y 0.00.

### Reveal mockup layers outside the perspective frame
- Correction from the user: outside the skewed quadrilateral, the existing back/mockup layers must be visible; stretching the frame edge into that area is incorrect. Root cause: the edge-extension bands added in the previous batch deliberately covered the already-rendered mockup inside its viewport.
- Fix: removed both the generated edge extension and the perspective viewport cover. The frame fill and page remain together inside the perspective mesh; pixels outside its four-corner boundary are transparent to the earlier `drawMockup` pass, so the real back layer shows through.
- Focused checks: `npm run typecheck` and the 3 frame tests passed. Live inspection with the user's current TL X 0.34 / Y 0.31 showed the skewed frame ending cleanly at its quad and the original BaseCase mockup screen visible behind it, with no stretched frame pixels. The current pin position was preserved.

### Line-only perspective pins
- Request: use lines instead of filled pin markers. Changed only the local pin CSS: transparent button and center, an outer outline plus a thin inner outline, and no selected-state fill or glow. The dashed quad lines are unchanged. Live inspection at 400% zoom confirmed the selected TL handle remains legible without covering the mockup content.

### Remove white perspective grids from Render
- Report: white perspective grids appeared in rendered output. Root cause: the perspective renderer splits the frame into an 8×6 Canvas 2D triangle mesh; exact clip boundaries can antialias inward at export resolution and expose subpixel backing between adjacent triangles.
- Fix: each triangle keeps its exact affine transform, while its clip polygon expands 0.75 output pixels from the centroid. Adjacent triangles now overlap slightly instead of leaving hairline gaps; the quad geometry and back-layer reveal are unchanged.
- Focused checks: `npm run typecheck` and the 3 frame tests passed. Rendered a 30 FPS verification MP4 with TL at X 0.31 / Y 0.20, extracted the actual MP4 thumbnail at full resolution, and visually confirmed no white triangle grid across the skewed Testimonial frame. Reset pins to X/Y 0.00 and restored the user's 60 FPS / 4K render settings. Verification artifact: `~/Downloads/BaseCase Landingpage Motion 2.mp4` (1280×960 H.264 test render).

### Fine X/Y perspective alignment
- Request: provide more detailed X and Y values and extra adjustment room so each pin can match a mockup screen corner accurately. Added public Slider controls `Precise X` and `Precise Y` below the broad 2D pad for the selected TL/TR/BR/BL corner. They show four decimal places, accept direct numeric entry, and step by 0.0005 over the existing safe -0.5…0.5 normalized range; the shared selected-point writer preserves one undo group during slider movement.
- Focused checks: `npm run typecheck` and the 3 frame tests passed. Live browser entry stored X `0.0125` without rounding and Reset pins returned it to `0.0000`; final project offsets remain reset.

### Movement controls activate the pin cursor
- Request: activate the canvas cursor through the movement controller. Perspective pin visibility is now shared editor-only state. Enabling pins, choosing TL/TR/BR/BL, resetting, dragging the 2D pad, or changing Precise X/Y reveals the canvas guide and four handles; clicking the canvas outside the frame still hides them. Selection and visibility remain outside project persistence.
- Focused checks: `npm run typecheck` passed. In the live browser, clicking outside reduced visible pin buttons from 4 to 0; selecting BL restored all 4 and selected the bottom-left canvas handle. After hiding again, an actual 2D-pad movement restored all 4 handles. Reset pins returned the test movement to zero.

### Canvas zoom to 800%
- Request: add 800% canvas zoom. The user explicitly authorized a one-time exception to the local signed-runtime edit restriction. Raised the canonical Toolcraft canvas zoom ceiling from 400% to 800%, updated the app fit ceiling, and added 400% and 800% to the existing footer zoom preset menu. The runtime still owns viewport state, offset scaling, wheel/pinch behavior, and persistence.

### Perspective top-edge mask bleed
- Report: moving a top pin upward with Precise Y did not remove the thin separator line below the MacBook menu bar. Root cause: the destination corner moved, but the final perspective pass was clipped to the exact detected page viewport, so every pixel above that boundary was discarded and the baked mockup separator remained visible.
- Fix: the detected MacBook viewport now carries a three-source-pixel top bleed. Only the perspective clip uses it; the left, right and bottom mask edges remain exact, and the destination quad still determines what is drawn. This lets a top pin cover the photographed separator without opening the camera or bezel to the frame.
- Focused checks: `npm run typecheck` passed; `frame-transform.test.ts` and `design-bounds.test.ts` passed (4 assertions). Live browser inspection used the user's current TR Y `-0.0005` and confirmed the updated mask at 800% and the full mockup at 100%; project pin values were preserved.
