### Preview performance plan completion
- Request: use preview render scale 1 instead of 2 while preserving independent export quality; replace the four-picture section cache with a memory-budgeted current/nearby-section cache and expose cache-miss measurements; reuse drawing buffers and release transferred images.
- Changed owner: `app-schema.ts` now defaults Preview quality to `Smooth · 1×`; `mo-app-sidebar.tsx` makes that preview choice authoritative on first load while continuing to mirror later runtime Resolution scale edits. Both BaseCase saved projects use preview/render scale 1. Their embedded page renderer replaces `KEEP = 4` with a 128 MB priority-aware LRU cache that favors visible and nearby sections and records hits, pending hits, misses, evictions, entries, and bytes. The existing `AnimationSandbox` surface pool was retained after verifying that it resets leased 2D contexts, caps idle surfaces and bytes, releases rejected surfaces, transfers frames as `ImageBitmap`, and the canvas consumer closes each bitmap after drawing.
- Result: editing uses one-quarter of the former 2× preview pixel count on the same display, Render keeps its own format/FPS/resolution controls, current and nearby section pictures remain cached within a fixed memory budget, and frame buffers are recycled without retaining transferred bitmaps.
- Focused checks: both saved animation scripts parse with `new Function`; the stored values are Preview quality `1` and Resolution scale `1`; both scripts contain the memory budget and cache counters and no longer contain `KEEP = 4`. Live inspection loaded BaseCase Landingpage Motion 2 with `Smooth · 1×`, runtime Resolution scale `1`, independent 4K Render selection, and three recent saved projects. `npm run typecheck` passed. The legacy `src/app/app-schema.test.ts` was also run; four starter-template assertions fail because this existing product schema, timeline, and controls no longer match that unchanged starter test, including the already-known missing product acceptance inventory.
- Risks: cache counters are diagnostic state inside the saved renderer; this focused edit did not run the protected measured performance suite or claim latency/throughput improvements beyond the deterministic pixel-count reduction and bounded cache/buffer policies.

### Preview performance plan: offscreen elements and next-section warming
- Request: extend section culling to individual offscreen elements with effect-safe margins and correct re-entry state; prepare the next section early without competing with visible drawing or causing a memory spike.
- Changed owner: both BaseCase saved renderers now cull independently drawn moving, grouped, and custom-styled layers from their transformed alpha bounds plus authored shadows, current motion/rotation/scale, three blur radii, and 48 px overscan. Timeline-derived effects continue to evaluate every frame, so re-entry uses current animation state. Visible sections render first; afterward one direction-aware adjacent section warms sequentially after 48 ms, yields between cache runs, and stops during visible work or above 90% of the 128 MB cache budget.
- User-visible result: offscreen animated elements avoid high-resolution picture creation and draw calls, while approaching sections can reuse prepared pictures without allowing unbounded background work.
- Verification: both saved scripts parse with `new Function`; structural checks confirm element culling, visible-only foreground jobs, adjacent-section scheduling, cache-pressure guards, and the new diagnostics. Live project reload and playback were checked after writing the saved files.
- Risks: static layers remain intentionally batched into section pictures, so individual culling applies to the layers already separated for motion, groups, blend, or opacity. No protected measured performance run was requested or executed.

### Preview performance plan: static effect cache
- Request: cache unchanged blur and shadows while keeping animated elements separate and preserving clipping, blend modes, and layer overlap order.
- Changed owner: both BaseCase saved renderers now store independently rasterized layers and motion groups in a 64 MB LRU effect cache keyed by membership, detail scale, and edited text. Static SVG filters, masks, authored blur, and shadows stay in the cached pixels. Animated transforms, opacity, and timeline blur remain draw-time state. Text-aware bounds prevent edited labels from reusing stale effect extents; evicted canvases release their backing pixels.
- User-visible result: unchanged expensive effects are rasterized once and reused without flattening animated elements or changing their compositing order.
- Verification: both saved scripts parse with `new Function`; structural checks confirm the byte budget, LRU hit path, eviction and canvas release, text-sensitive keys, and effect diagnostics. Live project reload and playback were checked after updating the saved renderer.
- Risks: the effect cache retains at least one entry even if a single unusually large layer exceeds its 64 MB budget, preventing immediate rebuild thrashing. No protected measured performance run was requested or executed.

### Preview performance plan: lighter blur and display-sized images
- Request: calculate changing layer blur at lower preview resolution with a high-quality inspection mode, keep scroll blur separate, and use display-sized preview copies of oversized images while retaining originals for export.
- Changed owner: preview frames now carry explicit preview/quality markers; Render carries an explicit export marker. Preview qualities below 2× blur animated layers through one reusable lower-resolution padded surface, while Sharp 2× and export retain the full-resolution blur path. Four measured 2× raster sources received compressed half-dimension preview copies. The saved renderer selects them only in low-quality preview, validates each against its source signature, and keeps preview/original section, bounds, and effect caches separate.
- User-visible result: ordinary editing performs fewer blur pixels and decodes smaller copies of the confirmed oversized card/testimonial assets; Sharp 2× remains available for close inspection and exports continue using the original sources.
- Verification: `npm run typecheck` passed; both saved scripts parse with `new Function`; structural checks confirm preview/export markers, the 2× high-quality boundary, reusable preview blur, four guarded preview copies, and preview/original cache separation. Live Smooth 1× and Sharp 2× playback were checked after reloading the saved project.
- Risks: only assets with an unambiguous 2× source-to-display relationship were resized. Other images remain on their originals until their transformed display bounds can be established without risking visible quality loss. No protected measured performance run was requested or executed.

### Preview performance plan: adaptive playback and current-time delivery
- Request: lower preview resolution when frame time rises, offer 30 FPS playback, restore sharpness when paused without quality oscillation, keep scroll synchronized to elapsed time, and verify the existing latest-pending-frame behavior under load without adding a queue.
- Changed owner: `preview-playback-quality.ts` applies a playback-only, one-way quality policy: sustained pressure reaching six slow or superseded samples steps internal rendering to 75% at a 30 FPS submission rate, isolated fast samples reduce that pressure score gradually, continued pressure can step to 50%, and pause restores the selected full resolution and 60 FPS. `animation-canvas.tsx` keeps the visible canvas at the runtime-selected backing scale while passing the lower effective scale only to the sandbox; scroll and motion remain evaluated from runtime `timeSeconds`. `latest-frame-scheduler.ts` formalizes the existing one-active/one-replaceable-pending lifecycle and releases an active result instead of displaying it when a newer timeline frame is waiting.
- User-visible result: playback can trade internal sharpness for responsiveness under sustained pressure, stays at the chosen lower level for the remainder of that playback run, and returns to full selected sharpness when paused. Slow rendering skips obsolete visual samples while landing-page scroll remains aligned with elapsed timeline time.
- Focused checks: `npm run typecheck`, `npm run build`, and seven Vitest checks across the new policy/scheduler plus existing frame geometry passed. The simulated-load test submitted frames 1, 2, and 3 while frame 1 was blocked; frame 2 was replaced before rendering, completed frame 1 was released as stale, frame 3 alone was delivered, and recorded maximum pending depth stayed at one. Live playback kept the visible backing at 1600×1200 for Smooth 1× and 3200×2400 for Sharp 2×, reported maximum pending depth one, advanced the delivered frame's timeline time, and returned to factor one/60 FPS with Smooth 1× after pause. `npm run ai:check` did not reach an app verdict because the supplied `toolcraft-execution-observer.mjs` crashed inside TypeScript's `isSourceFile` with an undefined node under the installed Node 26/TypeScript 6 toolchain.
- Risks: the 34 ms pressure threshold and six-sample window are deterministic policy defaults rather than device benchmark results. The protected measured performance suite was not requested or run.

### Preview performance plan: session memory and rendered video preview
- Request: measure memory across playback, scrubbing, and project reopen; evict distant cached pictures and release unused resources at the budget; treat slowdown as a measurement signal rather than proof of a leak; and offer a temporary rendered preview for a full timeline or selected section that refreshes after relevant edits.
- Changed owner: both BaseCase saved renderers now enforce a 160 MB combined section/effect/blur budget in addition to their existing local budgets, release evicted canvas backing stores, expose cache memory diagnostics, and clear their resources on sandbox disposal. `AnimationCanvas` samples those diagnostics, browser heap when available, and scheduler render timing into a bounded 120-sample session history. The Preview section exposes that telemetry plus full/selected rendered range and Build/Remove actions. `renderTemporaryPreview` reuses the final export frame painter at 0.5K/30 FPS without downloading. `rendered-preview.ts` owns its temporary object URL and content signature; the canvas uses decoded video frames while the timeline remains the time authority, then revokes the video on relevant edits or manual removal.
- User-visible result: a heavy project can be rendered once for smooth effect-complete playback, including scrubbing and loop timing, without redrawing the design for each preview frame. Memory use, peak, evictions, average render time, and optional JS heap remain visible through the session. Reopening a project releases the prior sandbox resources; composition edits mark the rendered preview stale and return to live drawing.
- Focused checks: both saved scripts parse through `new Function`; TypeScript and the production build passed; ten Vitest checks passed across scheduler, adaptive quality, memory-history bounds, full/selected preview range, preview invalidation, and frame geometry. Live telemetry classified scrubbing and playing samples. A full 29.5-second rendered preview completed at 1280×960/30 FPS. During 2.5 seconds of playback its canvas time advanced from 21.5735 to 24.0803 while sandbox-delivered frames stayed at 396. Changing the canvas ratio removed the video immediately and showed the refresh message; Undo restored the prior ratio.
- Risks: browser JS heap is shown only where `performance.memory` is available and includes more than product caches. The session measurements establish trends and eviction behavior, not a memory-leak diagnosis. Temporary preview creation is intentional offline work and remains interruptible only through the existing render abort lifecycle.

### Figma plugin + paste import: multi-paint and picture fills become an image
- Request: when a fill has several colors or a picture, import that fill as an image.
- Plugin (`Figma plugin/Copy Layer JSON`): a layer whose visible fills are more than one, or include a picture, gets `fillImage`: a 2x PNG of only that fill (a temporary copy with children hidden and strokes, effects, opacity and rotation cleared; removed again). Checkbox "Several colors or a picture in a fill -> import as a picture" (on). Version 3.
- Paste import: such a layer is drawn from its picture (clipped to its rounded shape), stored as an asset `fill-<id>`; its colors are not painted. Checked live with a rounded frame carrying a gradient picture, a white stroke and a text: picture, stroke and text correct; test project deleted. A shadow on a picture-fill layer is not drawn. tsc clean; plugin scripts pass a syntax check but are not run inside Figma.

### Figma effects: layer blur, background blur, inner shadow, drop shadow, glass
- Request: the import must bring layer blur, background blur, inner shadow, drop shadow and glass effects.
- Plugin (version 4): `effect()` now keeps every number of an effect (color and alpha, offset, radius, spread, and glass values such as light angle and intensity, refraction, depth, dispersion).
- Import (`figma-paste-import.ts` script): shadows use device pixels so they match at any zoom or render scale; LAYER_BLUR blurs the layer; BACKGROUND_BLUR copies what is painted underneath, blurs it and shows it inside the layer's shape; INNER_SHADOW is cast from a frame with the shape cut out; GLASS is approximated (frosted backdrop, faint tint, a light edge along the light angle). Drawing order per shape: backdrop blur, drop shadow with fills, glass, inner shadows, strokes. Checked live with a sample (blurred circle, glass card with background blur + inner and drop shadow, glass pill): all visible; test project deleted. Not covered: spread, progressive blur, refraction/dispersion/depth of glass, effects on rotated layers (backdrop blur skipped). tsc clean; plugin not run inside Figma.

### Deleted layers of a pasted design still drew
- Report: layers deleted in the Layers panel, but the artboard still showed the design. Cause: `getLayerStack` draws every layer of the animation when none of its layers are in the project (meant for a new project whose layers do not exist yet), so an emptied pasted design came back whole, including the hidden Original picture. Fix: `hasOwnLayerList(spec)` (ids starting `figma-design-`) skips that fallback, so a pasted design with no layers draws nothing. Checked live on "Research Signals Card" (layers all deleted): the artboard is now empty. tsc clean.

### Rotated and flipped layers: arrows were shifted and double-turned
- Report: the dropdown arrows of an imported card sat below-left of the headings and pointed up. Cause: Figma's x/y of a rotated layer is its pivot corner, not the corner of its box, so a 180-degree arrow was drawn one box off; and an icon exported as SVG already carries its own rotation, which was applied a second time.
- Plugin (version 6): every layer carries `abs` (its box on the page) and, when it is rotated or flipped, `transform` (Figma's relativeTransform).
- Import: each layer's place is a matrix multiplied down the tree; an upright layer is drawn as before, a rotated or flipped one is drawn in its own box through its matrix (text, shapes), and an exported icon is only placed in its bounds (its picture is already turned). Roots are placed by their page boxes. Clips of rotated containers are skipped; backdrop blur on rotated layers is still skipped. Checked live: a 180-degree chevron points down beside the title, a 45-degree bar turns about its corner, mirrored text is mirrored. Test project deleted. Existing imports (made with plugin 5 or older) keep their old placement until the design is copied again with the new plugin. tsc clean.

### Imported text sat too high
- Report: text layers of the imported card were a little above where Figma has them ("Overview" not level with "All accounts"). Cause: the baseline was placed at the middle of the line box, which puts the letters above the middle. Fix (`figma-paste-import.ts` script): the font's ascent plus descent (`fontBoundingBoxAscent/Descent`) is centered in the line height, and the baseline is that ascent below the top, as Figma lays text out. Patched into the saved project "Research Signals Card 2" (script only; id suffix -b; backup in the scratchpad) and checked: the title and Overview line up, pill labels are centered. Older imports keep the old script until patched or copied again; arrow icons in them still use the old rotation placement. tsc clean.

### Matching the Figma render of the card by measurement
- Request: improve the imported card by hand until it matches the supplied 1600x1200 reference.
- Method: a test page ran the project's drawing script and compared it pixel by pixel with the reference (mean difference over the whole picture, and per layer). Findings and fixes: (1) the glass tint made every glass pill about 25 levels too bright - removed (whole-picture difference 4.1 -> 1.4); the light edge is softer (alpha 0.35 and 0.2 of the light intensity, 2 px) - also changed in the importer; (2) Figma's real glass values arrived from the plugin (radius 100, refraction 0.8, depth 22, light angle -45, intensity 0.8), so the guessed property names were right; (3) text positions were already exact (every shift made it worse); (4) text used a fallback font - the project now carries Instrument Sans 400/500/600 (copied from the Hover Cards project) and its text is drawn at 0.9 opacity, which matches Figma's thinner rendering.
- Applied to the saved project "Research Signals Card 3" (script, fonts and assets only; layers and values untouched; id suffix -b; backup in the scratchpad). Final difference to the reference: about 1.4 on a 0-255 scale. Left as is: the thin rim lines of the pills and the sliders' inner glow, which differ by a few levels. tsc clean.

### Figma plugin moved into the app
- The Copy Layer JSON plugin now lives in `figma-plugin/` at the app root (was `~/Documents/Mo_App/Figma plugin/Copy Layer JSON`): `manifest.json`, `code.js`, `ui.html`, `README.md`. In Figma: Plugins > Development > Import plugin from manifest... and pick `figma-plugin/manifest.json`. It is plain files outside `src`, so the app build and tests do not read it. `files:check` passes, tsc clean.

### Research Signals Card 3: four-segment card motion (Animation) + cursor behaviour (Reactor)
- Request: from the marked screenshot: 001 the main card zooms in and focuses the Portfolio slider (mark 2) while the cursor enters; 002 the cursor drags the black line back (counts down) and forward (counts up), then the line returns (counting); 003 a click on the +12.8% pill (mark 3) opens it to 383 px while mark 2 shrinks to 160 px, it takes the Investments slider look (mark 4) and the cursor drags its white line up and down; 004 leaving it closes mark 3, the cursor drags mark 4's white line up and down, the bar returns, the cursor leaves the screen. Motion settings go in Motion > Animation, interaction behaviour in Reactor.
- Changed (project file only, edited through the switch-away / delete in-app copy / reopen route; backup in the scratchpad): the drawing script gained a camera (card zoom and focus, background zoom share), per-layer changes (mark 2 width, line and counting value; mark 3 width, white block, white line, icon carried into the block and darkened, value moved right and counting; mark 4 white block, line and counting %), a screen-space "Cursor" layer whose tip is the point it acts on, and a camera-aware background blur. 19 Animation controls in four categories (001 Focus, 002 Black line, 003 Mark 3 opens, 004 Mark 4): zoom, times, drag distances, open width, cursor entry/exit. Reactor is on with nine items (Cursor, Drag, Cursor, Tap, Cursor, Drag, Cursor, Drag, Cursor) timed to the default motion, size 44. Timeline 14 s, the loop ends where it starts. New animation id `figma-design-muz6ytkf-motion1`.
- Changed (app): `interactions.ts` an item that hands over to another at the same moment does not fade there (no blink between consecutive Reactor items); `animation-canvas.tsx` and `footer/footer-render.ts` draw no Reactor items while the animation's cursor layer is off the picture (before it enters / after it leaves), instead of falling back to the item's own position.
- Checked live: scrubbed 0-14 s; zoom and focus, value counting (down to $54,849 and up past $180,330, back to $128,450), mark 3 open/morph/drag (+14.9% / +8.7%), close, mark 4 drag (75% / 39% / 60%), cursor exit and zoom out. Drag defaults lowered so the lines never touch the value text. tsc clean.

### Research Signals Card 3: rolling counters, hand cursor while dragging, holds, full segment controls
- Request: counting values roll with a 10 radius blur (up when rising, down when falling); at 4.08 s the cursor did not follow the black line; a different cursor while dragging; hold 0.8 s at the end of every segment; controls for every motion segment and the counter in the Animation section.

When it follows an animation's cursor it is placed by the hand's painted top-left, so it lands exactly on a hand the animation draws with the same shape; without a cursor it is centred on its point.

### Reactor: Hold slider and items that follow a longer Duration
- Request: increasing a Reactor item's duration moves the next items automatically; a Hold slider in the editor under Duration.
- Changed: `interactions.ts` items carry `hold` (seconds the item stays on its last moment after its duration; older items read as 0), drawing shows an item until duration + hold and hand-overs use that end, `interactionEnd` and `patchInteraction` (a change of Duration or Hold moves every item starting at or after the old end by the same amount). `interaction-list.tsx` edits go through `patchInteraction`; new Hold slider (0-5 s, 0.1) between Duration and Size. New `interactions.test.ts` (4 tests).
- Checked live on Research Signals Card 3 (Drag at 11.9 s): Duration 2.0 -> 2.1 moved the next Cursor 14.0 -> 14.1; Hold 0 -> 0.1 moved it to 14.2; both undone, list back to the user's values and selection. tsc clean, animation tests 67/67.
- Not done: Start is still independent (moving an item's Start does not move the others); the timeline length is not extended when items run past it.

### Reactor: link toggle per row with a node line
- Request: a link icon beside each Reactor row's ×, with a small node line between linked rows; unlinked rows are not affected when another item changes or is deleted.
- Changed: `interactions.ts` items carry `linked` (linked to the row above; on by default, older items read as linked). Duration/Hold changes move only the chain of linked rows below the item and stop at the first unlinked row (`patchInteraction`, `shiftChain`); `removeInteraction` pulls the linked rows below a removed item up so the first starts where it started, unlinked rows stay. `interaction-list.tsx`: link / unlink button (LinkSimple / LinkBreak, not on the first row) between the row and its ×; delete uses `removeInteraction`. `interaction-list.module.css`: `.link` column and `.chained` node line with a small dot up to the row above; `.unlinked` dims the broken-link icon. Tests 7/7 in `interactions.test.ts`.
- Checked live on Research Signals Card 3: eight linked rows with the node line; Tap unlinked, first Drag 3.1 -> 3.2 s moved only the linked Cursor (5.5 -> 5.6), Tap 6.8 and later rows stayed; all undone (links, times and selection back as they were). tsc clean, animation tests pass.

### Reactor: overlapping items drew two cursors; items playing together share one row
- Report: cursor icons faded in and out on top of each other. Cause: in the open project several items overlap the next one (Drag 2.6 + 3.1 runs to 5.7 while the next Cursor starts at 5.5; Cursor 5.5 + 1.4 to 6.9 vs Tap 6.8; Tap to 7.6 vs Cursor 7.5), so both pointers were drawn, each fading.
- Changed (`interactions.ts`): a pointer item (Cursor, Tap, Drag) is shown only until a later pointer item starts (`shownEnd`, exclusive at the take-over moment), and that hand-over does not fade; pointer items starting together (within 0.06 s) draw only the strongest (Drag > Tap > Cursor).
- Request: when several items play at the same time on the same point, show them as one row, e.g. "Cursor & Tap  6.8s · 0.8s". Changed: `sameTiming` (start, duration and hold as the list shows them, to 0.1 s), `gatherTogether` (an item that comes to match an earlier row moves up beside it, linked), `patchInteraction` takes one id or a row's ids. `interaction-list.tsx` lists runs of neighbours with the same timing as one row (labels joined with " & "); the editor edits every item of the selected row; × removes the row; the row's link is its first item's.
- Checked live: frames at 5.5 s and 7.5 s show one cursor. tsc clean, animation tests 72/72 (two new for rows). Not checked live: merging rows in the panel (the user was working in the app; covered by unit tests). There is no way to split a merged row except changing its items back through another time.

### Research Signals Card 3: smooth focus out at the end
- Report: the return to the main position at the end was rough.
- Causes: (1) the zoom out eased the focus point and the zoom separately, so the card swung sideways while shrinking; (2) the user's later Animation edits (Open time, Move to line, Drag down + time, Back time, Cursor exit) made the motion 17.45 s while the timeline stayed 16.9 s, so the loop cut the last 0.55 s of the zoom out and jumped to the full view.
- Changed (project script, id `figma-design-muz6ytkf-motion4`): the focus out zooms about the one point that stays put between the zoomed and the full view (no sideways swing), with the zoom changing evenly in log scale on a smootherstep curve; Zoom out time default 1.6 s (the project's 1.2 s, still the old default, became 1.6). The rebuild keeps the user's own Animation settings and Reactor list; the timeline is set to the motion's length, 17.85 s.
- Checked: camera probe (card corners move monotonically to rest); live playback frames 16.06-17.75 s glide in and settle, 0.08 s matches the end.
- Note: when Animation times change, the timeline Dur still has to be set to the motion's length by hand (otherwise the end is cut).

### Research Signals Card 3: stronger card background blur that holds when zoomed in
- Report: when the card focuses in, the MainControllerCard background's blur looks weaker (the background picture shows clearly); add a little more blur.
- Cause: the glass blur copies the picture under the card and blurs it; a zoomed-in card covers most of the frame, so that copy was cut at the canvas edge and the blur faded to transparent there, letting the sharp background through.
- Changed (project script, id `figma-design-muz6ytkf-motion7`): when the copy reaches past the canvas edge, its edge pixels are stretched outwards on a reused work canvas before blurring, so the blur keeps its strength to the edge. New layer control "Background blur" (id `fg-119.backdropBlur`, default 130, was 100 in the design), shown in the Modifier tab for the MainControllerCard background layer. The user's other settings and Reactor list were kept.
- Checked live: at 8.0 s (zoomed) the card's right side, where the waves showed sharply before, is evenly frosted; Modifier shows Background blur 130px for the layer. Not measured: render cost of the larger blur at Sharp 2x.

### Research Signals Card 3: lines trail the hand while dragging
- Request: when dragging, the black or white line follows with a little delay, about 0.4 s.
- Changed (project script, id `figma-design-muz6ytkf-motion8`): the hand drives each drag and the line (with the white block it ends) trails it: the hand's path averaged over the last 2 x "Line follow" seconds with a soft Hann weighting (about 0.4 s behind, starts gently, catches up after the hand lets go). New control Line follow (Drag category, default 0.4 s). The counters are read from the hand's path, still "Count delay" (0.4 s) after it, which keeps the number in step with the trailing line. The user's other settings and Reactor list were kept.
- Checked live playback: 3.16/3.50 s hand ahead of the black line going back, 4.52 s going forward, 5.51 s hand released while the line glides home; 9.34 s (mark 3) and 12.80 s (mark 4) the white line trails the hand.
- Follow-up: Line follow set to 0.2 s in the project (Animation > Drag), through the panel; default in the build script also 0.2. Count delay left at 0.4 s.
- Follow-up: Line follow 0.15 s and Count delay 0.18 s (user wrote "1.5 ms" / "1.8 ms"; read as 0.15 / 0.18 s from their earlier ".2 ms" = 0.2 s), set in the panel; the file holds 0.15 and 0.18 exactly. The Count delay slider (step 0.05) displays 0.2; the build script now uses a 0.01 step for both and these defaults, applied on the next rebuild.

### Reactor: press feedback on drag and tap
- Request: when a drag starts (the cursor turns into the drag hand) the icon scales down a very little and back up; a tap scales down and up a little more.
- Changed (`interactions.ts`): Drag on desktop: the hand dips 7% about the point it holds over its first 0.3 s and springs back (`DRAG_PRESS`); Tap on desktop: the arrow dips up to 16% about its tip and back as a smooth pulse over the press, in time with the ripple (`TAP_PRESS`; was a jump to 0.88 and back). `arrowCursor` takes a scale, `pointer` an optional scale, `dragHand` a press factor. Touch devices unchanged. The animation's own cursor (shown only with the Reactor off) is unchanged so the Reactor's tracking of it stays exact.
- Checked in the page with the live module: hand 51x40 -> 47x38 -> 51x40 px over 0.3 s (handing over from a Cursor item, no fade); tap arrow shrinks before the ripple starts. tsc clean, animation tests 72/72.
- Note: the project had a rendered preview active (video built before this change), so the canvas shows the old icons until the preview is rebuilt or removed; not touched.

### Sound: interaction sounds and minimal background music
- Request: add interaction SFX and, based on the visual and motion, minimal background music.
- Changed: `animation/sound.ts` synthesises the soundtrack with Web Audio (no sound files): interaction sounds from the Reactor items (Tap: a click as the arrow presses and a soft tick as it lifts; Drag: a soft grab as the hand takes hold and a lighter release), and a music bed (warm detuned pad through four chords, quiet sub, sparse triangle pluck; Calm = Fmaj7 Am7 Dm9 Cmaj7, Bright = Cmaj7 Am7 Fmaj7 G7) whose chord length fits the timeline (about 4 s each). The music is rendered twice and the second pass kept, so the loop has no seam; a compressor glues the mix. One built buffer is cached by a key of everything it depends on. `animation/sound-player.ts` plays it in step with the timeline (starts at the playhead, loops, restarts after a real jump such as a scrub or loop segment, stops on pause); used in `animation-canvas.tsx`. `footer-video-encoder.ts` takes an optional soundtrack and adds an audio track (AAC in MP4, Opus when AAC is missing; Opus in WebM), fed in one-second pieces alongside the frames; `footer-render.ts` passes it for the final MP4/WebM render only (not the temporary preview, not GIF). New Motion-tab section "Sound" (`app-schema.ts`): Interaction sounds, Sounds volume (60%), Background music, Mood (Calm/Bright), Music volume (35%), Play in preview; per-project (`sound.` added to the project targets in `footer-projects.ts`). New `sound.test.ts`.
- Turned on for Research Signals Card 3 (Interaction sounds + Background music, Calm).
- Checked in the page: soundtrack for the project's timeline builds in ~0.4 s (16.7 s, peak 0.36, louder at the tap); a 4 s test encode in memory gave MP4 (AAC) and WebM (Opus) files whose audio decodes back at the right length and level; preview starts one looping source at the playhead (5.63 s), stops on pause, restarts at 0.00 after a jump while playing. A page reload was needed for the new section (it dropped the session-only rendered preview). tsc clean, animation tests pass.
- Not checked: how it sounds (no listening possible here); a full Render with download (not run).

### Sound: two more interaction sound styles to choose from
- Request: two variations of the SFX to choose between.
- Changed: `sound.ts` `SfxStyle` with per-style sounds for a Tap's press/lift and a Drag's grab/release: Soft (the first set, default), Crisp (short bright square-wave ticks with a strong click transient, softened by a 6 kHz low-pass) and Bubble (rounded sine pops: rising for the tap and grab, falling for the release; no noise). The style is part of the soundtrack key. `app-schema.ts`: "Sound style" segmented (Soft / Crisp / Bubble, target `sound.sfxStyle`) under Interaction sounds. Test added (sound.test.ts 4/4).
- Checked in the page: all three render with similar peaks (press 0.23-0.34, grab 0.23-0.30) and no clipping; after a reload the Sound section shows the choice. Not checked: how they sound (no listening possible here).

### Sound: pitch control for the interaction sounds

- Changed: `sound.ts` `sfxPitch` (semitones, -12..+12, default 0, part of the soundtrack key): every interaction sound's tones and its click colour move by 2^(pitch/12). `app-schema.ts`: "Pitch" slider (st) under Sound style. Test added (animation tests 77/77).

### Sound: minimal drag sound with controls
- Request: a minimal dragging sound, with controls.
- Changed: `sound.ts` `scheduleDrag` sounds for the whole length of each Reactor Drag: Swish (looping noise through a band-pass at 1.1 kHz and a 280 Hz high-pass, swelling in over 0.12 s, breathing slowly, fading out over 0.18 s) or Ticks (quiet sine detent clicks, about 12 a second, slightly uneven). Follows Pitch; own gain; included in the loop-tail wrap with music; part of the soundtrack key. `app-schema.ts`: Drag sound switch, Drag style (Swish / Ticks), Drag volume (50%), shown while Interaction sounds is on. Test added (animation tests 78/78).

New Drag fade (0-1 s, default 0.25) fades every drag style in after the grab and out before the release (`fadeAt`, `heldNoise`). ### Sound: drag sound follows the real drag movement
- Report: the reel sound did not match the slider's backward and forward drag timing. Cause: the reel used a fixed spin-up / wind-down over the Reactor Drag's length, unrelated to the motion (back, turnaround, forward, turnaround, return).
- Changed: new `animation/drag-motion.ts` samples the animation's cursor layer (30 per second, half scale) through each Reactor Drag and gives its speed (smoothed over five samples) and direction; a change of the cursor's shape (arrow to hand) counts as no movement. `sound.ts`: drag sounds follow it: Reel clicks at up to 36/s scaled by speed, none at the turnarounds, two semitones lower while moving back, the whir follows speed; Ticks rate follows speed; Swish loudness follows speed. Without a sampled path the old fixed curve is used. The movement's fingerprint is part of the soundtrack key. `sound-player.ts` samples with the probe sandbox once per change of the motion (cached); `footer-render.ts` samples with the render's sandbox for the final MP4/WebM. New `drag-motion.test.ts`.
- Checked in the page on Research Signals Card 3: sampling the three drags takes about 4 s (once); speeds follow the phases (back peak about 3.2 s, turnaround 3.4-3.6, forward peak 4.0-4.2, turnaround 4.6, return 5.0); reel clicks/s: back 27, turnaround 5, forward 40, turnaround 0, return 20, after the release 0 (the hand-to-arrow spike removed). Animation tests 82/82, tsc clean. Note: the sound follows the cursor (hand); the slider line trails it by Line follow (0.15 s).

### Research Signals Card 3: minimal hover glow on the sliders
- Request: when the cursor hovers a slider, its stroke glows a little, minimal, not rough.
- Changed (project script, id `figma-design-muz6ytkf-motion10`): for mark 2 (gradient slider, its current width), mark 3 (pill, its current width) and mark 4 (bar), the cursor's distance to the slider's box gives a hover amount (smoothstep from Reach to on it), times Strength and the cursor's own fade; drawn right after the slider as a thin 1.25 px white edge (alpha 0.45 x amount) with a soft white shadow (alpha 0.55 x amount, blur Glow size), clipped only by the card's root clip. New Animation category "Hover glow": Hover glow switch (on), Strength 40%, Glow size 10 px, Reach 40 px. The user's other settings, sound values and Reactor list were kept.
- Checked: state probe (glow on mark 2 during its work 1.5-6 s, mark 3 6.9-11 s fading at 11.8 as the cursor leaves, mark 4 13-15 s; a brief glow on mark 4 at 1.0 s as the entering cursor passes over it); offline frame at 2.0 s with and without: a thin soft light rim around the slider, inside unchanged (mean 0), edge up to +21 levels.

### Research Signals Card 3: cinematic focus blur when the card focuses in
- Request: a cinematic focusing blur when the card focuses in.
- Changed (project script, id `figma-design-muz6ytkf-motion11`): after the picture is drawn (before the cursor, which stays sharp), `applyFocus` lays a blurred copy over it through an elliptical radial mask centred on the frame (the camera always centres the focused slider): clear in the middle, opaque outside. Depth of field = Depth blur x how far the card is zoomed in (so it fades with the focus out); focus pull = Focus pull x sin(pi x zoom-in progress), applied everywhere (mask floor) so the picture passes through soft focus and lands sharp. The copy's edges are stretched before blurring (no dark frame edge); two work canvases are reused. New Animation category "Focus blur": Focus blur switch (on), Depth blur 8 px, Focus size 40%, Focus pull 6 px. The user's other settings, sound values and Reactor list were kept.
- Checked: state probe (pull 3 -> 6 -> 3 px over the zoom in, depth 8 px while zoomed, 6.35 / 1.0 px during the focus out); offline frames: 0.8 s whole frame soft; 1.6 s Portfolio slider sharp, corners/Scenes/background soft; 13.0 s mark 4 sharp, Portfolio and Scenes soft; no edge darkening. Not measured: the added per-frame cost (two full-frame copies and one blur while zoomed).

### Sound check: one late release, fixed; playback allows for speaker delay
- Request: check whether the SFX mismatch the motion.
- Found: every sound matched its moment except the first Drag's release: its Reactor row ran 2.6 + 3.1 = 5.7 s while the hand lets go of the black line at 5.35 s (the motion's release), so the release sound came 0.35 s late (and the Reactor hand stayed until the next Cursor row at 5.5 s). Tap click 6.95 / lift 7.19 vs click 6.95-7.15, mark 3 8.30 / 10.55, mark 4 12.25 / 14.30 all matched; the drag hold follows the sampled cursor movement.
- Changed: the project's first Drag row 2.6 / 2.75 s (ends at the release, 5.35) and the next Cursor row 5.35 / 1.4 s (to the Tap at 6.75), through the build (other rows, settings and sound values kept). `sound-player.ts`: playback starts `baseLatency + outputLatency` further into the sound so what is heard stays in step with what is seen (6 ms here; more over Bluetooth).
- Checked after reopening: sound onsets 2.61, 5.36, 6.96, 7.20, 8.31, 10.56, 12.26, 14.31 s against the motion's 2.60, 5.35, 6.95, ~7.15, 8.30, 10.55, 12.25, 14.30. tsc clean.

- Request: add Lottie Animation to Motion Type; export must follow what Lottie supports; when it is selected, agents build only with Lottie-supported features; anything the export cannot carry shows in red with why and what to do.

New `src/app/lottie/`: `lottie-data.ts` (recognise a Bodymovin file, info, top-level layers as `lottie-<ind>`, `prepareLottie` writes hidden layers, Layer-section blend/opacity and an optional Background solid layer), `lottie-project.ts` (a Lottie `.json` opens as a Lottie Animation project at its size and length, frame fill off; wired into `parseProjectFile`, so Browse files and the Open list open Lottie files), `lottie-runtime.ts` (lottie-web 5.13.0 light canvas player, no expressions, loaded as source and started inside the existing sandbox; the same `prepareLottie` runs there), `lottie-support.ts` (`checkLottieExport`: expressions, effects, layer styles, 3D, camera, audio, merge/offset paths, pucker/bloat, twist, zig zag, blend modes, mask modes and expansion, motion blur, linked images, live text without glyphs; plus app parts not in the file: Reactor, Sound, Frame Settings, mockup/stickies, timeline length; a project with no Lottie data blocks the export), `lottie-export.ts` (Lottie JSON or dotLottie v1 zip, file named after the project), `lottie-check-control.tsx` (red list: title, why, "What to do", layers; the export control adds the button, disabled while a blocking item is listed, and the last export's result). `app-schema.ts`: Project tab section "Lottie export" (Format JSON / dotLottie, Background layer, the check with an Export Lottie button inside the section: the sticky footer merges every section's actions and would show it on every project type) and the same check under Motion Type, both only for Lottie Animation. `animation-sandbox.ts` sends the player and the Lottie script to the sandbox for a spec with `lottie`; `rendered-preview.ts` signs a Lottie spec by its data. `docs/mo-app/agent-motion-guide.md` section 2b: allowed features, never-use list, file rules, how to start and check. New dependency `lottie-web@5.13.0` (exact).

Not checked: a downloaded Export Lottie file in an external player, and a full MP4 render of a Lottie project. `npm run ai:check` crashes inside the checker on `main` as well, so it was not usable.

- Request: import MP4, WebM and GIF files outside the Lottie motion type; asked: both as a new project and as a layer in the open project.

Guide section 2c. Tests `media-layer.test.ts` (5).

- Request: under Resolution, a choice to keep the video under a number of MB, for uploading to Dribbble and other sites.

### Loop Wheel animation: pill blur also works where ctx.filter is ignored
- Report (screenshot): the dashes still show crisp across the specialty pills and labels. In the in-app browser the version-3 render already hid them, so the likely cause is a browser that ignores `ctx.filter` (Safari): the blur call is then a no-op and the dashes stay sharp behind the translucent pill.
- Changed (project script, version 4, Recovered and Projects): `blurBehind()` feature-detects `ctx.filter` once (`CAN_FILTER`); without it the area behind the pill is blurred by repeated halving through two scratch canvases and drawn back up (steps = log2(blur × scale / 2) + 3), clipped to the pill. With the filter the code path is unchanged.
- Checked (offline frames, specialties blur 10, right end of the Integrative Hormonal pill, base pill colour 129): no blur 98-106; filter path 128-129 at scale 0.5 / 1 / 2; fallback forced on 128-129 at the same scales (it was 114-125 with fewer steps). Reopened from the file: version 4, fallback present, controls unchanged. Not checked in Safari itself.

### Loop Wheel animation: white fill layers under Specialties and Doctors
- Report: the dashes still show through the pills in the in-app browser; the pills and labels are below 100% opacity (0.7 / 0.3) by design, so leave them and add a white fill layer under Specialties and under Doctors, blurred to blend with the background.
- Changed (project script, version 5, Recovered and Projects): two new layers, Specialties fill and Doctors fill (Layers panel: Doctors, Doctors fill, Specialties, Specialties fill, Wheel; draw order wheel, specialties fill, specialties, doctors fill, doctors). Each draws a rounded backing under every pill of its layer, grown by its blur, as a feathered shadow (`shadowBlur` with an off-canvas offset: works in every browser, no `ctx.filter`). Modifier controls per fill layer: Fill color (#FFFFFF), Blur (0-40 px, default 8), Opacity (0-100%, default 100). The pills' own opacity and shapes are untouched; their earlier Background blur sliders stay (Specialties / Doctors layers) but now default to 0.
- Checked (offline frames at scale 0.5 / 1 / 2, `Integrative Hormonal` pill's right end): darkest value 98-116 without the fill (dashes) against 135 with it (the pill's own colour over white); the strip just outside the pill 124-157 without and 203 with (white halo that fades, dashes covered); a red Fill color reaches the pill region. In the live canvas after reopening from the file (version 5, layers listed, 8 controls): no dash crosses a pill or a label; a soft white halo surrounds each pill.

### Loop Wheel animation: interaction motion v_01 (segments, list loop, wheel, button hover, Reactor, sound)
- Request: Interaction Animation for Loop Wheel, version v_01. Segments: 01 cursor comes in, pause 0.5 s; 02 hover Schedule Now twice, then unhover; 03 cursor changes icon and scrolls forward and back, the wheel turning clockwise and back; 04 pause 0.5 s, cursor out. Animations: Doctors loop one by one around the middle row, top fades out and bottom fades in, following the scroll; add doctors if needed so the loop starts and ends on the same doctor; Specialties the same, same direction; Schedule Now hover changes colour, label rolls out of the top while a copy rolls in from the bottom (hover out the reverse); a line under "Womanhood" reveals from the centre on hover. Reactor per segment; SFX reel while scrolling (hover sounds optional, not used); minimal UI-style BGM.
- Changed (project script, version 6, Recovered and Projects; previous files in /tmp/lw_before_motion.json and /tmp/lw_projects_before_motion.json):
  - `schedule(P)` / `stateAt`: 01 0-1.8 s, 02 1.8-5.95 s (hover in 2.6, out 3.3, in 4.35, out 5.05), 03 5.95-9.6 s (hand at 6.75, scroll forward 7.0-8.2 by 2 rows, back 8.2-9.4), 04 9.6-11.2 s; timeline 11.2 s; `getSegments` reports the four segments and fx spans for List loop, Wheel spin, Button hover, Womanhood line.
  - Lists: 7 items each (added Fertility Care, Menopause Care; Dr. Maya Chen, Dr. Elena Ruiz with initials avatars until photos are added as doctor-6 / doctor-7 in Layer files) so the item leaving at the top is never the one entering at the bottom. Rows and opacity interpolate along 0.3 / 0.7 / 1 / 0.7 / 0.3 with hidden rows above and below; forward = up. Seven loop steps per loop, split between before the grab (5) and after the release (2), so none is caught mid-way; the scroll adds the hand's drag in rows. The fill backings follow each pill and fade with it.
  - Wheel: turns 360/7° per row (clockwise forward), one full turn per loop, so the ring's brightness gradient returns to where it started.
  - Schedule Now: colour mixes to Hover color (#111111), label rolls by Text roll (34 px) clipped to the button. Womanhood: line (2.5 px, 30 px under the word's middle, orange) opens from the word's centre by the hover amount.
  - New screen-space Cursor layer (arrow / grabbing hand) for the Reactor to follow.
  - Controls: segment groups "01 · Cursor in" ... "04 · Cursor out" (times, holds, Scroll rows) and effects List loop, Wheel spin, Button hover, Womanhood line (with on/off eyes); the fill and pill-blur controls stay in Modifier.
  - Reactor on: Cursor 0.3-1.8, Cursor 1.8-5.95, Cursor 5.95-6.75, Drag 6.75-9.4, Cursor 9.4-10.9. Sound: Interaction sounds on (soft), Drag sound on, style Reel; Background music on, Calm, 30%.
  - Version "v_01" saved in Versions.
- Checked: Node run of the schedule (segments as above; list steps 0 -> 7 and wheel 0 -> 360° over the loop, lists on whole rows through the scroll); offline frames (hover: dark button, label in place, line under Womanhood; mid-roll both copies; scroll: hand on Doctors, both lists up, wheel turned); after reopening from the file: version 6, Interaction plan shows the four segment cards with times, playback shows the Reactor cursor on the button with the line, and the grab hand on the Doctors list mid-scroll. Not checked: how the reel and music sound.

### Agent channel: change the open project in place, with progress on the artboard
- Request: stop going to the Open page, deleting and re-creating the project for every motion or design change; while the agent works show a 0-100% loading animation / micro-interaction on the artboard with what it is doing, and end with "saved as a new version".
- Cause of the old behaviour: autosave writes the open project over its file every 10 s, so the agent switched away, deleted the in-app copy and reopened the edited file.
- Changed: `scripts/project-autosave.mjs` serves `~/.mo-app/agent/` (`GET /agent`, `GET /agent/update`, `POST /agent/done?id=`). New `scripts/mo-agent.mjs` (status / apply / clear). `mo-project-autosave.ts`: channel client. New `mo-agent-overlay.tsx` + CSS module: a card centred on the artboard (glass, progress ring with %, breathing orange halo and orbiting dot while working, a check that draws itself when done, the message under it; reduced-motion respected; pointer-transparent; outside the product canvas). `mo-app-sidebar.tsx`: polls every 0.8 s while the helper is on; shows a status for the open project (ignored after 10 minutes); applies an update for the open project in place (animation into the library, params, allowed values, timeline length, Layers list) in one history step and saves a version (given name, else next v_NN) as the active one, then clears the channel. Guide section 5b; the old "do not edit a file while open" trap now points to it.

### Loop Wheel animation: fills cover the whole list column (v_03, via the agent channel)
- Report (screenshot): dashes still cross the faint top and bottom rows; increase the Specialties and Doctors fill height.
- Cause: each fill was a backing per pill that faded with its pill (below 30% opacity) and left the 16 px gaps between rows, so the rows fading in and out showed the dashes.
- Changed (project script version 9; applied in place through the agent channel with progress, saved as v_03): each fill layer draws one column behind its list, from the row fading in above it to the row fading out below it (y 369-801), as wide as its widest item so it never jumps, feathered by its Blur. New Modifier control on Specialties fill and Doctors fill: Height (0-200 px, default 0) stretches the column above and below.
- Checked: Node schedule unchanged (loop 0 -> 7 steps, wheel 0° at the ends); in the app: version 9, both Height controls, Versions v_01, v_02, v_03 (active); the live canvas shows each list on one soft white column with no dash crossing any row, faint ones included.

### Loop Wheel animation: each pill masks the wheel (v_05, via the agent channel)
- Report: the columns look bad; every list item should mask the wheel, and the mask must not depend on the item's opacity.

The fill layers became "Specialties mask" and "Doctors mask": hiding one turns its list's mask off; their controls are now Padding (0-40 px, grows the cut) and Softness (0-30 px, feathered edge via a shadow); the old fill colour / blur / opacity / height / width controls and the column backing were removed.

### Loop Wheel animation: lists follow the wheel's circle, scale 100 -> 70% out and 70 -> 100% in (v_06, agent channel)
- Request: as the lists move in and out, follow the wheel's circle path; when an item goes out it scales 100 -> 70%, when it comes in 70 -> 100%.
- Changed (project script version 12; applied in place with progress, saved as v_06): `rowPlace()` puts each item's edge nearest the wheel on a circle around the wheel's centre (Specialties radius 301, Doctors 331, so the middle row stays where the design has it and the rows above and below curve toward the wheel: right edge 499 / 506 / 528 / 568 for rows 2 / 1&3 / 0&4 / -1&5); the scale is 100% on the five rows on show and eases to Out scale beyond them, anchored at that edge. Pills, labels, avatars and the wheel masks all use the same place and scale. New List loop controls: Follow wheel (on) and Out scale (40-100%, default 70).

### Loop Wheel animation: masks fade in and out with the items (v_07, agent channel)
- Report: apply the same in / out behaviour to the mask; it seems delayed when items come in and go out.
- Cause: the masks already followed the circle and the scale, but cut at full strength for any item on the canvas, so a cut showed while an entering item was still invisible and stayed while a leaving one had faded.

- Report: on the first hover the button's text motion starts late.

### Render: no black at the end of videos with sound
- Report: a rendered video ends on a black screen instead of the last frame.
- Cause: the frames were fine to the end, but the AAC sound track came out 0.064 s longer than the video (11.264 s vs 11.2 s: the encoder pads its last block), so the file ran past the last frame and players showed black for that tail.
- Changed: `mo-video-encoder.ts` `fitAudioToVideo()` cuts the soundtrack 0.1 s before the video's end with a 30 ms fade (no click); `createMoVideoEncoder` takes the video's length (`durationSeconds`), passed by `renderVideo` (frames / fps).
- Checked by rendering Loop Wheel animation in the app (0.5K / 30 FPS, the download caught in the page, no file saved): before, total 11.264 s, video 11.2 s, audio 11.264 s; after, total 11.2 s = video, audio 11.179 s (AAC), and the frame at the very end is the last frame (brightness 232, not black). The user's render settings (MP4, 60 FPS, 2K) were put back and the test's status line cleared. tsc clean.

### Loop Wheel animation: background music not heard in the render (v_09, agent channel)
- Report: the BGM is not in the rendered video.
- Found: it is. A render of the project (2K / 60 FPS, download caught in the page, no file saved) has an AAC track whose level from 0.3 to 6.5 s is -48.9 dB (peak 0.0084), exactly the soundtrack the preview plays; the project's Background music volume was 4%, which is almost silent. At 35% the same music is -30.1 dB.
- Changed (asked: 35%): `sound.musicVolume` 35 through the agent channel (saved as v_09; versions keep animation and render settings, sound settings belong to the project). No code change.
- Checked: the app's soundtrack now measures -30.1 dB over 0.3-6.5 s; Versions up to v_09 (active); the channel is empty and the test render's status line was cleared.

### Versions bring back their own motion (script, controls, layers), all in the project file
- Report: cannot jump to another version. Asked to store it in one file.

Everything stays inside the one project file. Guide 5b updated.
- Backfill for Loop Wheel animation (through the agent channel, no new version): v_01 = spec 6, v_02 = spec 8, v_03 = spec 9 (rebuilt from spec 10 without its card-colour / width change: white fill, no width), v_04 = spec 10, v_05 = spec 11 (masks; controls without Follow wheel / Out scale), v_06 = 12, v_07 = 13, v_08 and v_09 = 14; each with its controls and Layers list (fill vs mask names).
- Checked in the app: every version shows a script; Load v_03 -> script equal to v_03's, white fill columns, layers "Doctors fill" / "Specialties fill"; Load v_09 -> script equal to v_09's, curved lists with per-pill masks, layers "... mask", music 35%. Node run of the old scripts: v_01's wheel turns one full turn with the lists, v_03's only with the scroll. tsc clean.

### Versions: Duplicate instead of Load (2026-10-11)

The version on show now takes any missing version settings at their current values (once), after which edits mark it changed. Checked on v_07: Reactor off → dot + overwrite icon; back on → dot gone.

Cmd/Ctrl+click Duplicate while the dot shows saves the change as the next v_NN instead and leaves the version as saved. Jumping away saves a waiting change first. The overwrite icon is removed. - Follow-up: deleting the version on show now opens the next one (the one below; the one above when it was last). Deleting another version leaves the one on show. ### Figma plugin and paste: import into the open project, fonts, pictures, Paste to replace (2026-10-12)
- Request: pasted designs go into the open project (no new project); a default-named project takes the frame name, and the main layer is named after the frame; plugin sends full font settings; Layer section gets a font picker; a text whose font is missing shows as its picture, is locked, and asks for the font file, which is saved in the app for every project; picture layers carry their file and fit with an uploader; Layer files move into the Layer section; right-click a layer ▸ Paste to replace.
- Plugin (`figma-plugin/code.js`, `ui.html`, v7): text `fonts` (all styled segments), auto-resize, paragraph spacing, `textPng` + `textPngBox`; `image` = original picture file (≤ 8 MB), scale mode, transform; new "Texts as picture too" switch.
- App: `src/app/mo/figma-paste-import.ts` now keeps the design in `spec.design` as blocks drawn by one design script (`DESIGN_CODE`) in the Layers order; a project with its own script gets the blocks drawn over it (composite). Older pasted projects keep working and are converted when changed. `mo-paste-design.ts` plans the in-place paste/replace; `mo-fonts.ts` + helper `/fonts` endpoints keep saved fonts in `~/Documents/Mo_App/Fonts`; sandbox gains a `fonts` check (families the drawing can use). `figma-layer-settings.tsx` adds the text/picture editors to `LayerEditor`; `mo-paste-menu.tsx` is the right-click menu (clipboard, or Cmd+V when the browser refuses). Schema: `layerImages` moved into the Layer section; the Layer files section is gone. Text edits live in params `__text`, picture fit in `__picture` (kept by `resolveParams`, so versions keep them).
- Checks: `figma-paste-import.test.ts` (4 tests); typecheck; in-app browser with a v7-shaped sample: new project → "Card A" renamed and drawn (picture from file, missing font as picture); second paste merged into the same project with "Card B" main layer; missing-font warning + locked controls; font upload unlocked editing and saved `~/Documents/Mo_App/Fonts`; text edit and font picker (Georgia) redraw; picture replace + Fit; right-click Photo ▸ Paste to replace (via Cmd+V) put Card B in Photo's box; composite over a script layer drew both. Test project and test font removed.
- Follow-up (2026-10-12): Layer section controls match the Toolcraft ones the user showed: one-line `TextInput` for labels (Figma auto-resize WIDTH_AND_HEIGHT), a multiline field (library `Field` + `ControlFieldLabel` + `Textarea`; `CodeTextarea` is monospace) for paragraphs, then Font | Weight, Size | Case, Color (`ColorOpacity`), Line height | Letter spacing, Align — composed from public controls because the library `FontPicker` only lists its Google catalog. Picture layers use `FileDrop` (image grid) and take a picture, GIF or MP4/WebM (played through `assetMedia`). Plugin: VIDEO fills are detected (sent as their picture, flagged `video`), GIF fills play after paste, and circles/boxes filled with a photo or video are picture layers instead of SVG icons. Checked in the in-app browser with a sample (title, paragraph, video fill): weight 700, Uppercase and #FF5500 redraw; a recorded WebM dropped on the uploader plays on the timeline; test project removed.
- Fix (2026-10-12, PropVera): the Occupancy ring and its track are outlined-stroke VECTORs larger than the 96 px icon limit, so they arrived as plain shapes and drew as rectangles. Plugin v8 sends every drawn path (vector, boolean, star, polygon, line, ring/arc ellipse) as SVG, or as an exact 2x picture (`vectorPng` + render box) when it carries blur or shadows; the app draws those as picture items. Maintenance bars used a linear gradient whose Figma matrix cannot be inverted (second row 0, 0, 0.5); the design script now places linear gradients from the first matrix row (t = a u + c v + e) and guards the radial inverse. Stored pasted designs now run the app's current design script (`currentDesignCode` in the sandbox), so fixes reach older pastes. Checked: PropVera Maintenance bars redraw green → yellow → faded like the original; the ring needs a fresh copy with plugin v8.
- Fix (2026-10-12): the blurred ring's picture landed ~28 px left / 130 px up: Figma's render bounds of the plugin's scratch copy are stale. The app now reads the picture's size from the PNG header and centers it on the shape's own box (`abs`); text pictures are exported with `useAbsoluteBounds` and placed on the text's box. Test added (5 pass).
- Correction (2026-10-12): the real cause of the broken gauge was Figma groups, not stale bounds. A GROUP (or boolean shape) has no coordinate space: its children's positions are in the group's parent space, and the import multiplied in the group's offset again, pushing everything inside groups by the group's x/y (the gauge track and its label moved +24, +126.5). `walk` now gives group children the parent's matrix (`abs × local⁻¹`). Vector and text pictures are centered on the layer's box from that matrix, at the PNG's real size. Test added (6 pass). PropVera's newest Occupancy card was repaired in place through the agent channel (saveVersion false): gauge contents moved back; the ring, track and "202 / 214" label now match the original. Designs pasted before this fix keep the old group offsets until pasted again.
- Change (2026-10-12): picture layers hold several files (`pic.files`, asset keys in order): the Layer section uses FileDrop's multi-image grid — + adds pictures/GIFs/videos next to the existing ones (the newest is shown), click a thumbnail to show it (params `__picture[id].show`), × removes one, drag reorders. The design script loads every file and draws the shown one. PropVera's "Imge-154" got its building picture back (from Figma's rendered fill) as file 1, with the uploaded video as file 2; switching checked in the browser.
- Change (2026-10-12): video and GIF thumbnails in a picture layer's uploader show a small play icon (drawn onto the preview copy only; files unchanged). Checked on PropVera Imge-154.
- Fonts (2026-10-12): Urbanist Thin, Light, Regular, Medium, SemiBold, Bold and Black saved to the app's font library (~/Documents/Mo_App/Fonts, through the helper). PropVera now carries Regular, Medium and SemiBold (the styles it uses); its 182 texts that showed as pictures are live and editable. Applied in place via the agent channel, no new version.
- Change (2026-10-12): video/GIF settings in the Layer section (`mo/clip-settings.tsx`), for video layers and for the video or GIF a picture layer shows: info (kind, duration, size, the part that plays), Start, End, Reverse and (videos) Sound, saved in params `__video[clipKey]` so they undo, go into versions and never reload the drawing. `media-layer.ts` `clipTime` maps timeline time to clip time (the Start–End part loops, forwards or backwards); the sandbox uses it for every video frame. `media/media-sound.ts` decodes the sound of videos with Sound on, lays it out the same way and mixes it with BGM/SFX in the preview (`useSoundtrackPlayback`, now given the layer stack) and the render (`buildRenderSound`). Checked on PropVera Imge-154: controls shown, info updates (2.9–7.0 s backwards), clip time runs 7 → 6 → 5 → 3 then loops, sound decodes (10 s stereo), settings saved; test values reset afterwards.
- Change (2026-10-12): video settings add "Match project length" (sets the project duration to the playing part; turns Fit off) and "Fit to project" (params __video[key].fit: the part is stretched to one project loop, frames and sound alike; clipTime takes the project length). The info box says whether the video matches, loops inside, is cut at the end, or is fitted (with its speed). Checked on Imge-154: messages for 0–10 s, 4–10 s and Fit (0.6×); fitted clip time runs 4 → 10 over the 10 s loop and restarts at the loop; reset afterwards.
- Change (2026-10-12): Frame Settings can use a pasted design's own layer as the frame ("Frame layer": groups near the top that have a background layer). The choice and its settings live in params `__frame` (layer, its background item, block, box, plus fill/fillColor/stroke/strokeColor/strokeWidth/radius/clip once changed). Fill, Stroke, Corner radius and Clip Content then read the layer (`frameLayerStyle`) and edit it: the design script restyles the background item and gives the layers it clips the new corner radius (or no clip). The app draws no card of its own while a frame layer is set (`readFrameStyle`). PropVera_Header: Frame layer = Container (Clip on, Fill #EFEFEF, radius 0 as pasted); radius 27 px rounded the container and everything it clips, then set back to 0.
- Motion (2026-10-12, PropVera_Header v_01, Interaction Animation): the shared design script now runs an optional `design.motion` script (stateAt per-layer changes, drawLayer for a Cursor layer, getSegments for the Interaction plan); documented in the motion guide. The occupancy gauge is now a stroke along the pill's centre line (radius 66, 16 px band), both arms from the left end, whose trim end moves with the percentage (210 / 249 / 171 px for 94.4 / 97.2 / 91.6 %), coloured green → yellow → clear over its reach; maintenance bars are trimmed round strokes whose width follows the count (150 px = 7), on the numbers' easing so shape and number land together; dot-chart dots pop in or out one by one (dot stagger 0.022 s, pop 0.12 s, column stagger 0.015 s), with more lit dots on page 02. It is now one continuous stroke (no seam at the left) trimmed by dashes: on each change the arms erase outward from the left end (trim start, first 42%), then draw in again from the left to the new occupancy (trim end), in step with the count. Checked frames 3.9–4.8 s.
- Change (2026-10-13): deleted layers leave no data in project files. `createProjectFile` saves the animation through `pruneFigmaDesign` (pasted layers not in the Layers panel lose their items, pictures and spec layers; empty pastes go; a picture drawn from its own file drops Figma's rendered copy) and `pruneDeletedMedia` (deleted video/GIF layers lose their clip). Tests: 26 pass in src/app/mo + src/app/media (2 new).
- Perf (2026-10-13, live preview of PropVera_Header): the live preview showed no motion on the first play pass (frames replaced/stale, quality dropped to 0.5). Cause: the 1080p video layer was read with a seek (`CanvasSink.getCanvas`) for every preview frame, about 55 ms each (decode from the nearest key frame), plus a copy per frame. `media/media-clip.ts` now keeps one in-order reader (`CanvasSink.canvases`) that moves forward (about 1 ms per picture), reopens it only for a jump back (loop) or far ahead (scrub), and reuses a decoded picture for as long as its source frame lasts. Measured in the browser: delivery gaps 120–270 ms (first gap 2 s) → about 40 ms; the picture follows the clock from the first second; cursor and data change show while playing. Checked: existing src/app/media tests; playback on PropVera_Header. Renders (video export) read the same clip, so they get the same speed-up. Risk: preview still drops to 0.75 quality / 30 fps on this project (frames take about 38 ms against a 34 ms limit).
- Fix (2026-10-13): hovering the timeline bar no longer pauses playback. `timeline-panel.tsx` passed its hover state to the clock (`useTimelineClock`), which stopped the playhead while the pointer was over the bar; hover now only shows the scrub handle (`isHoverPaused: false` for the clock). Checked in the app on PropVera_Header: with the pointer over the bar (hover state true) the time ran 3.49 → 4.70 s; type check passes.

A scene owns the animation's own controls and the hand-built plan (`animation.params` keys without `__`), `interactions.*` and `sound.*`; the live values are the scene on show and the other scenes keep theirs in `slice` (`scenes.ts`, 12 unit tests). The canvas, the render and the sound map timeline time to a scene and the scene's own time (`resolveProjectScenes`, `sceneFrame`; sounds built per scene and laid end to end in `scene-sound.ts`); the timeline is the sum of the scenes (`scene-duration.ts`), the plan rail adds the scene's start to its times. A new scene has no motion (`Scene.motion` false → `__noMotion`) and uses the hand-built Interaction plan; the design script (`figma-paste-import.ts`) now draws that plan (`planState`: fade, move, scale, blur per animation) and, for "Previous end", the previous scene's last motion state under its own (`__base`). Versions keep all scenes (`mo-versions.ts`) so switching scenes is not a version change; projects store `scenes.*` (`mo-projects.ts`). Not looked at: every scene's frames one by one, the clicks' sound, the render.
- Preview fix for scenes (2026-10-13; request: fix preview): after the split into eight scenes the live preview froze (frames stopped after a second or two, quality fell to 0.5). Cause: videos and GIFs were timed by the scene's own time and length, and PropVera's video is set to Fit to project, so every scene fast-forwarded the whole 10 s clip in 1-2 s and every frame seeked (0.6-1.3 s each). `SandboxFrame` now carries `mediaTimeSeconds` / `mediaDurationSeconds` (the whole timeline's time and length; canvas and render set them) and `withMedia` plays clips by them, so a video runs once across all scenes, read in order. Checked on PropVera_Header: playing the 14.3 s loop twice, frames keep arriving (about 48 ms apart on average, 20 fps), no seeks; quality still steps down to 0.5 on this heavy project (spikes of 130-300 ms when a scene is shown).
- Scene selection at the stop (2026-10-13; request: do not move the selected scene while it plays; where play stops, that scene is selected): `useSceneFollow` (`scene-duration.ts`) no longer switches scenes while the timeline plays; when playback stops (and the segment wise preview is off) the scene the playhead stopped in becomes the scene on show, as if clicked. Moving a paused playhead changes nothing. Checked on PropVera_Header: Start stayed selected through playback and, stopped at 7.05 s, Scene_05 was selected; type check passes.
- Preview, all in all (2026-10-13; request: stop having to ask for preview fixes, give the complete solution): found and fixed the causes that made the preview freeze or crawl. (1) Videos and GIFs were timed by the scene's own time and length, so every scene fast-forwarded the whole Fit-to-project clip and seeked on every frame; they now use the whole timeline's time and length (`mediaTimeSeconds`, `mediaDurationSeconds`). (2) A picture that never came back blocked every picture after it and the failure was swallowed: `LatestFrameScheduler` now has a 6 s watchdog, counts failures (`failed`, `lastError`, shown as `data-preview-failed` / `data-preview-last-error`), drops late pictures and calls `AnimationSandbox.repair` (reopens the video readers, logs `[preview]` once); sandbox render requests time out as well. (3) `media-clip.ts`: a decode that does not answer is dropped after 4 s (single read, then the last picture); the preview gets a video picture no bigger than it draws, a render the full one; clips open and decode their first picture when the project opens (`warmUp`). (4) Scene selection no longer changes during playback (earlier change), which removed the 130-300 ms spikes that dropped quality. Checked on PropVera_Header (14.3 s, eight scenes): playing 31 s, about 24 pictures a second, quality held at 0.75 (before: 0.5 after a few seconds, then frozen), 0 failures, no console warnings; tests: scheduler watchdog (2 new) and all of src/app/animation, media and mo pass (207). Not covered: a scrub storm, a render of all scenes.

- Cursor no longer blinks between scenes (2026-10-13; request: the cursor fades out and in): every scene draws its own Reactor items, so a cursor that began at the start of a scene faded in (and one that reached the end of a scene faded out) at every scene change. `SceneFrame.carriesOn` (the scene before ends with a pointer on screen) and `leadsOn` (the next scene starts with one) tell `drawInteractions` (new `options`: `carriesOn`, `leadsOnAt`; `isPointerShownAt`) to skip the fade there; canvas and render both pass them. Fades inside a scene and at the first and last scene (the cursor comes from and goes off screen) are unchanged. Unit tests added (3, interactions.test.ts). - Rolling digits never overlap the text (2026-10-13, PropVera_Header v_16; request: make sure the number illusion does not overlay the text letters): a rolling digit was drawn at the left edge of its place with its own width, so a wider digit (or the old one, laid out for the old text) could reach the letter or sign beside it. `drawLetters` now measures every place of the new text (`letterPlaces`: left edge and width) and draws every shown digit, old, rolled or new, centered in its own place and squeezed horizontally when it is wider (`digit`); the fixed letters stay at their places in the new text. - Scene_05 gap on the right (2026-10-13, PropVera_Header v_27 and a no-version update; request: the Rent collection card touched the frame's right edge (red mark), want a gap): the visible width was 758 design px, so the card (right edge at 744 px inside the frame) had 14 px; zoom 1.9 → 1.75 (about 823 px visible, ~65 design px of gap, the left gap stays 104 px, the bottom edge stays at the frame's bottom), so the card has a clear gap and the next card (Maintenance) starts to show at the edge. Tried 1.68 first (the next card showed too much). Scene_06 starts from the new framing. Checked in the app at 11.6 s.
- Scene_06 framing (2026-10-13, PropVera_Header v_28; request: readjust Scene_06 like the picture: the pager at the top right, the Maintenance and Net operating income cards, a sliver of the card before at the left): measured against the design (the Maintenance card 320 design px = 290 px in the picture, the Net operating income card's right edge 24 design px from the frame's right, its bottom 30 px from the frame's bottom): the camera shows the frame's bottom right, zoom 2.05 about the frame's bottom right corner (focus 95 %, 93.333 %), so 702 design px are visible and 6 px of the Rent collection card show at the left. Scene_06's camera now pans from Scene_05's framing to that (the angle gives way to 0 at a corner) and Scene_07 zooms out from it; the data still starts after the move. Checked in the app at 13.0 s and 14.43 s: the framing matches the picture, Maintenance and Net operating income rolling from 12 / $4.6M to 23 / $3.8M.
- Scenes 05 and 06 camera jump (2026-10-13; request: the cameras of Scenes 05 and 06 do not match, a little shake): Scene_05 ended at angle 3 / tilt 1.5 while Scene_06 started at angle -6 / tilt 3 (same zoom and focus), so the picture turned 9° and 1.5° in one frame at 12.16 s. Cause: one of my updates was built from the autosave file, which was still the older state (it lags by up to 10 s), and wrote Scene_05's old end angle back. Fixed Scene_05's end pose to Scene_06's start (zoom 1.75, angle -6, tilt 3, focus 5 %, 93.333 %); checked every scene change from Scene_04 to Scene_07 (end pose = next start pose in the saved file). Also made `changeScene` keep neighbours matched: changing a camera's end pose changes the next scene's start pose when they were the same (and the earlier scene's end when a start changes). 3 new tests (scenes 16). Checked in the app: frame-to-frame change of the picture is 0 through 11.7-12.25 s (Scene_05's end and Scene_06's first frames) and rises smoothly from 12.28 s as the move starts, no spike at 12.16 s.
- Camera angle and tilt in the moves (2026-10-13, PropVera_Header v_29; request: angle and tilt do not match the transitions, the delay or easing is off): (1) the pans of Scenes 05 and 06 swung the angle (3 → -6, then -6 → -6) while the pivot ran along the frame's edge, so the tilt looked as if it swayed against the pan; all poses of Scenes 04 to 07 now carry the same angle -4 and tilt 2, so the angle only appears with the zoom in (Scene_04: 0 → -4 / 2 together with the zoom) and goes with the zoom out (Scene_07), and the pans keep it still; (2) the zoom is now mixed in log space (`poseAt`), so zoom, angle, tilt and focus, which share one ease (cubic in and out, same delay and duration), feel as one move; the test checks the middle of a 1 → 2.2 push at the geometric mean. Saved camera chain checked: each scene's end pose = the next scene's start pose. Checked in the app (Scene_05 pan, 9.35 to 10.31 s): the same slight tilt throughout the move.

## Camera angle/tilt same in every scene (Scenes 04-07)
- Cause: angle and tilt were rotated around the zoom pivot (the focus corner), so the tilt was almost invisible near the top-left (Scene_04) and strong at the bottom corners (Scenes 05/06).
- Fix: `camera.ts` zooms about the focus pivot, then applies angle/tilt with perspective about the window centre; `coverShift` moves the picture a few pixels so it still covers the window (instead of reducing angle or zoom). Pose data unchanged.
- Tests: `camera.test.ts` (`coveringShift`), `src/app/animation` 196 pass.

The feather (90 px) shrinks to 0 as the draw-in finishes, so the final gauge is identical to the design. Template and PropVera_Header script updated through the agent channel (v_30).

## Beat grid, click sounds and music (v_43)
- Timing on a 120 BPM grid (0.5 s beats, 4 s chords): Start 1.0 · Scene_01 1.5 · Scene_02 0.5 · Scene_03 3.5 · Scene_04/05/06 3.0 · Scene_07 5.5 · Scene_08 1.5 · End 1.5 = 24.0 s (was 22.46 s), so every scene cut falls on a beat and the music has six whole bars. Parameters changed in every scene's slice: `s3.moveTime` 0.5, `click.delay` 0.4, `d3.hold` 0.68, `d4/d5/d6.hold` 0.78, `s7.moveTime` 0.9, `s7.gap` 0.4, `d7.hold` 0.28, `s10.pause` 1.5. Taps (press sound 75 ms after the tap starts): 3.4 s, 5.9 s, 16.4 s, 18.9 s ≈ beats 3.5, 6.0, 16.5, 19.0. Reactor cursor/tap items re-timed to match.
