- App fix, `scene-sound.ts`: (1) the scenes soundtrack was assembled at 44.1 kHz from 48 kHz sounds, which stretched every sound 9 % late; now 48 kHz. (2) The music is built once for the whole timeline from the first scene that asks for it (no seam at each scene join); the interaction sounds are built per scene and mixed on top.
- Checked in the app: the soundtrack is 24.000 s at 48 kHz, music runs through, clicks stand out at the tap times, a full preview pass runs in real time with no stall.

## BGM only from Scene_01 to Scene_08 (no music in Start and End)
- `scene-sound.ts`: the music bed is built for the span from the first to the last scene that asks for music (here 1.0 s – 22.5 s, 21.5 s) with a 0.4 s fade in and a 0.8 s fade out at those ends; scenes outside the span (Start, End) stay without music. Checked: 0 s – 1.0 s and 22.6 s – 24 s are silent, the middle sits at the same level, taps unchanged. Bars of the bed are 4.3 s (21.5 s / 5) instead of 4.0 s, not noticeable for a slow chord bed.

## BGM fade in / fade out controller (v_44 – v_46)
- New controls in Motion › BGM: `sound.musicFadeIn` and `sound.musicFadeOut` (0 – 5 s, step 0.1, default 0 = no fade; shown while Background music is on). `sound.ts` shapes the music's gain on the kept pass of the loop (so single-scene projects work too), the fades are part of the soundtrack key, and `scene-sound.ts` builds the one bed across the music scenes with those fades. PropVera_Header: 0.4 s in, 0.8 s out in every scene's slice. Checked on the built soundtrack: ramp 1.0 s → 1.4 s and 21.7 s → 22.5 s. Test added in `sound.test.ts`.
- Trap: the scene that is shown (here Start) rewrites its slice from the live values, so a value set only in other slices can be overwritten; set it in every slice and in the live values.

## BGM fades at the start and the end of the whole video
- `scene-sound.ts`: the music bed spans the whole timeline (Start and End included), from the first scene that asks for music (its mood and volume). Fade in begins at 0 s, fade out ends at the last frame, by `sound.musicFadeIn/Out`. Checked on the soundtrack: ramps 0 – 0.4 s and 23.2 – 24 s. This replaces the Scene_01 – Scene_08 span.

## BGM was too quiet to hear (MUSIC_MAKEUP)
- The music bed measured RMS 0.03 at 44 % volume (about -30 dB), and the tap clicks were 2 – 4 times louder, so it was easily lost. `sound.ts`: `MUSIC_MAKEUP = 3.2` scales the music gain; now RMS ≈ 0.10 at 44 %, peak 0.51 with the clicks, no clipping. The Music volume slider still goes 0 – 100 %. Checked: the AudioContext is running and the soundtrack buffer is played by the preview.

## Layer video (Imge-154) played in parts: forward, slow, then reversed; fade in per part (v_53)
- The clip `pic-fg2-772` (10 s) used "Fit to project" (10 s stretched over 24 s, slow motion all the way). Now `__video["pic-fg2-772"].segments` plays it in parts: Start → Scene_03 (0 – 6.5 s) clip 0 → 6.5 at its own speed; Scene_04 – Scene_06 (6.5 – 15.5 s) clip 6.5 → 10 stretched over 9 s (slow motion, chosen); Scene_07 → End (15.5 – 24 s) the whole clip backwards 10 → 0 (1.18×), so the last frame is the first one and the loop is seamless. The parts are tied to the timeline seconds (0 / 6.5 / 15.5 / 24); if the scene lengths change, change `from/to` too.
- `media-layer.ts`: `VideoSettings.segments` (`VideoSegment {from,to,start,end}`, `end < start` = backwards) in `clipTime`; tests added. `media-clip.ts`: backwards requests (falling times within 1 s) decode the half second before the asked time once, in order, and hand the pictures out backwards, instead of seeking from a key frame for every picture (which froze the preview). Checked: a full pass runs in real time (reverse part 16.75 s → 22.75 s at 1×), no failures.
- Motion script: `video.fade` (0.6 s): in Scene_04 and Scene_07 the layer `fg2-772` fades in as the part starts.

## Video parts dissolve into each other, smoothly (v_54, v_55)
- The dip to white is gone: the layer video no longer fades through the page background. `segments[n].fade` (1.6 s on parts 2 and 3) dissolves the new part in over the picture the part before it ended on (held still), as one blended picture made by the sandbox (`animation-sandbox.ts`, `planBlend` in `media-layer.ts`, eased with smoothstep). The script's own `video.fade` is off (default 0).
- Reading the clip: the part that plays backwards has its own reader (a second decoder, opened and warmed at load) and the backwards pictures are decoded one second at a time, the next second in the background while the current one is handed out (`media-clip.ts`, `prepareBackwards`), because seeking per picture cost 0.5 – 1 s and froze the preview. Checked a full pass: 446 sampled pictures, longest identical run 4 (0.2 s), no failed pictures; frames at 6.7 – 7.9 s and 15.5 – 17 s show the video behind the page throughout.

## Number layers fade with their card (v_57)
- Cause of "not all layers follow the card": the number layers drawn by the script (the counting texts, e.g. 94.4%, $578.1k) slid with the card but stayed fully visible, because the design's drawing sets its own alpha (`ctx.globalAlpha = it.alpha`) and ignored the fade put on the canvas; so they stood out ahead of the card. The wrapper now also passes the fade in `it.alpha`; gauge, bars and dot charts (direct canvas drawing) keep the canvas fade. All layers of a card (backgrounds, titles, numbers, badges, gauge, list rows, charts, legends) share one `dy`, blur and fade (checked with the script's own state at four times: identical values for card, text, number, gauge).

## Clip content kept while a layer slides in (figma-paste-import.ts)
- Cause of "clip content not working": a layer that slides in (`dy`) moved together with all of its clips, so the container frame's clip (Clip content on) moved down with it and the card showed below the frame's bottom edge. `drawPatched` now keeps the big clips (the page and container, larger than 60 % of the design in both directions) in place and only moves the small ones (the layer's own card). Shared design script, so every project gets it. Checked: the strip below the frame (94.5 % – 99.5 % of the canvas height) stays the plain background (brightness 66, max 69) during all of Scenes 05 and 06; 16 `src/app/mo` tests pass.

One hitch of about 0.1 s at 16.07 – 16.15 s (three repeated pictures), probably the background decode of the next second of the reversed video.

## Camera easing option; Scene_07 zoom out linear (v_59)
- `SceneCamera.ease` (`"smooth"` default, `"linear"`): a Linear speed switch in the Camera editor (Scenes Segment); `camera.ts` `poseAt` + `readCamera`, test added. Scene_07's camera is linear (1.6 s). Checked the frame change: constant ≈ 2300 from 15.62 s to 16.44 s.
- Linear showed a stall of about 0.5 s at 16.5 – 17.0 s: the next second of the reversed video was only requested late. `media-clip.ts` now starts decoding the next second as soon as the current one is handed out. Re-checked a whole pass: the only stills longer than 0.2 s are the planned wait before the second click (18.45 s).

## No-interaction version (PropVera_Header_v2, v_64)
- Built in the open project `PropVera_Header_v2` (a copy of PropVera_Header the user made; the interaction version PropVera_Header is untouched). Scenes: Start 1.0 · Scene_01 1.5 (intro, page 01 data) · Scene_02 3.5 (zoom in, role 04) · Scene_03 3.0 (role 05) · Scene_04 3.0 (role 06) · Scene_05 3.0 (zoom out, role 07, linear 0.95 s) · End 1.5 = 16.5 s. No cursor, no taps (Reactor lists empty), no click sound, music + fades stay.
- Script: `flow.direct` (params, default off) in `propvera-header-motion.template.js`: `changeAt` brings every area to page 02 at the zoom in (`z.delay` 0.3 s, with the `d3.*` count settings), then each area counts to page 03 in its own scene; the zoom out counts every area straight back to page 01 (`d7.delay` 0.3 s); the pager stays 01 / 24 (`pagerPlan`); the arrow highlights are off; `lengths` gives scene 07 `d7.delay + count + d7.hold`. Checked with the script's own state (page 01 → 02 → 03 → 01 values at the expected times) and frames of a full pass.
- Video parts for 16.5 s: clip 0 → 2.5 forward (0 – 2.5 s), 2.5 → 6 slow (2.5 – 12 s), 6 → 0 reversed (12 – 16.5 s), 1.6 s dissolves.
- Note: an update sent to `PropVera_Header` first would have waited for that project to open; it was cleared and sent to the open `PropVera_Header_v2` instead.

## PropVera_Header_v2: the pager pill and plus button do not animate (v_65)
- With `flow.direct` the pager strip (`fg2-693/695/696/697/698`) is left out of the Scene_04 (role 06) slide-in; the cards still slide in. Checked with the script's state: pager and plus carry no slide or fade at 0.4 s, 0.9 s and 1.2 s.

## No-interaction version really switched on (v_66)
- Cause of the pager showing "03 / 24" (and the two-click zoom-out in PropVera_Header_v2): the script only receives the params declared in the animation's `controls` (plus keys starting with `__`, `resolveParams`), so `flow.direct` and `z.delay` set in the scenes never reached it and the version ran as the interaction flow. Both are declared now (switch "Without interaction (no clicks)", slider "First data change delay"); they are also in `propvera-header-scenes.build.py`.
- Trap for every new script setting: add it to `controls`, or the saved value is silently dropped. Checked: pager "01" in every part (script state, direct on) and in the frames of Scene_04; the counts at the zoom in and the straight count back at the zoom out run as designed.

## Scene_04 (PropVera_Header_v2): data change no longer starts behind the card; video stalls (v_67)
- What was seen as a delay: in Scene_04 the count started at 0.9 s while the card was still fading in (0.8 – 1.5 s), so the first part of the count was invisible; and in the looped/segment preview the video asked for a clip time that looked "falling" (one request a little earlier than the last) and decoded a whole second backwards (0.8 – 1.8 s stalls inside the scene). Fixes: `d5.delay` 1.2 s / `d6.delay` 1.3 s (holds 0.48 / 0.39, lengths unchanged at 3.0 s) so the count starts as the card lands; `media-clip.ts`: backwards = three falling requests in a row (kept until a forward move), a ring of the last 40 shown pictures (≤ 1280 px) so a short jump back (looping a scene) needs no new decode, and the backwards chunks are decoded two ahead. Checked in Scene_04: no media stall above 80 ms between 9 and 12 s.
- Still open: the reversed video part (12 – 16.5 s in v2) stalls 0.3 – 1 s about every second in the preview, because decoding the clip runs at only about the clip's own frame rate (1 s of video takes about 1 s to decode) while the part plays it 1.33× faster; the render has no such limit. A slower or shorter reverse, or a lower-resolution clip, would remove it.

## Zoom out data change starts 0.5 s in (PropVera_Header_v2, v_68)
- Scene_05 (role 07): `d7.delay` 0.3 → 0.5 s, `d7.hold` 1.38 → 1.18 so the scene stays 3.0 s.

## Undo / redo / zoom bar moved to the top right (mo-viewport.ts)
- The toolbar panel (undo, redo, zoom %, two icons) sits on the top edge, right-aligned just left of the inspector (it was at the bottom right). `getPanelPlacement` (toolbar) uses `PANEL_EDGE_MARGIN - defaultTop` for the y offset; the first fit places the panels again at 0.25 / 0.7 / 1.2 / 2 / 3.2 s because the toolbar is measured before the layout has settled (the first placement left its y offset at 0). Checked on reload: top 10 px, right edge 1205 px at a 902 px high window; Center canvas puts it back there too.

## Scenes strip at the bottom (scenes-strip.tsx)
- A strip of scene blocks (as long as the scenes, name + length, the active one lighter, the playhead running across) sits above the playback bar, between the project sidebar and the inspector; shown when the project has several scenes (Interaction Animation). Clicking a block shows that scene (`selectScene`) and puts the playhead at its start. `mo-viewport.ts` leaves room for it when fitting the scene (when the strip is there at the time of the fit). The compact playback bar and the Timeline switch are unchanged; Toolcraft's keyframe timeline (mode "keyframes") was not turned on.

## Toolcraft keyframe timeline turned on (app-schema.ts)
- `timelineModule({ defaultDurationSeconds: 8, mode: "keyframes" })` (was "playback"): the bottom bar gets an "Expand timeline panel" button; expanded it shows the ruler, the playhead and one keyframe row per keyframeable control that has keyframes ("Add your first keyframe from the properties panel."). Tried with Music volume (`keyframeable: true`): the row and a keyframe appeared and could be deleted again; removed afterwards, so no control is keyframeable yet.
- Not wired, on purpose: the keyframes of a control change `state.values[target]` over time. Music volume / fades are baked into a soundtrack built once per settings change (a keyframed value would rebuild it every frame); the camera lives in `scenes.items`, the frame in `animation.frame` and the layer opacity / blur in params `__layerStyle` (objects, not scalar controls), so they have no keyframe rows until scalar controls (and drawing / render code reading them over time) are added.

## Expanded timeline as wide as the scenes strip
- The expanded keyframe timeline was a fixed 688 px (`timelinePanelExpandedWidthPx`, Toolcraft). `scenes-strip.tsx` writes the strip's width to `--mo-strip-width` on the document and `styles.css` sets the expanded timeline panel (`[data-slot="timeline-panel"]` that contains `timeline-expanded`) to it with `!important` (the panel's inline width is set by React). Checked: panel 1017 px = strip 1017 px, both starting at x = 268. The compact play bar keeps its own width.

## Toolbar above the inspector in one right column (mo-viewport.ts, styles.css)
- The toolbar (undo, redo, zoom %) sits on the top edge, right-aligned with the inspector's right edge; the inspector is placed below it (`controls` panel offset y = 46 px = toolbar height + gap) and shortened by the same amount through `--mo-toolbar-band` (`.toolcraft-panel-surface` max-height in `styles.css`), so it still ends 10 px above the bottom. When the inspector is on the left half (user dragged it) there is no band and the toolbar stays just left of it. Checked at 1604 × 902: toolbar 10 – 46 px, right edge 1594; inspector 56 – 892 px, right edge 1594, height 836.

## Toolbar as wide as the inspector
- Over the inspector the toolbar takes the inspector's width and left edge (`--mo-column-width` set in `mo-viewport.ts`, rule in `styles.css`, buttons `space-between`). Checked: toolbar and inspector both x 1294 – 1594 (300 px).

## All tab and the motion timeline (scenes-strip.tsx, motion-timeline.tsx, timeline-view.ts)
- The scenes strip starts with an "All" tab: it shows the whole motion and turns Segment wise preview off, so the play bar plays everything. A scene block shows that scene (as before).
- The expanded bottom timeline now draws this motion's rows instead of Toolcraft's keyframe rows (styles.css hides those; `motion-timeline.tsx` adds its own container inside `timeline-expanded` and the panel takes `--mo-tracks-height`):
  - All: Scenes (click a block to open the scene), Camera, Layer effects (the plan's Animations of every scene), Reactor, Video (parts of the clip), Music, over 0 → full length with a playhead; clicking a row or the ruler moves the playhead.
  - One scene: a row per plan animation (layer · effect), the camera, and each Reactor item, at their real times; bars are draggable: the body retimes (plan `delay`, camera `delay`, Reactor `start`), the right edge changes the length (`duration`). The plan edit writes `animation.params.plan`, the camera edit goes through `changeScene`, the Reactor edit through `patchInteraction`.
- Checked in the app: All rows, a scene's camera row, dragging the Scene_03 camera bar by 1.04 s (delay 0 → 1.04) and back. Not tried: plan animation bars (this project has none), Reactor bars, the resize edge. Script-driven effects (slide ins, data counts) are not rows yet.
- Note: the video plan in PropVera_Header_v2 still ends at 16.5 s while the timeline is 15.5 s.

## Timeline centred, height grip, more editable bars
- Position: the bottom timeline (play bar and the expanded timeline) is centred in the free area between the project sidebar and the inspector (`getPanelPlacement` for `timeline` in `mo-viewport.ts`; the 250 ms keeper now also fixes x). Expanded it is as wide as that area, so it lines up with the scenes strip (checked: both 268 – 1285 px at 1604 px wide).
- Height: a grip at the top of the expanded timeline (`role="separator"`, `motion-timeline.tsx`): drag up / down to change the height (min = header + ruler + 2 rows, max 60 % of the window), double click for the automatic height; the height is kept in localStorage `mo.timeline.height` and drives `--mo-tracks-height`. Checked: dragging 30 px (frame) up made the panel 212 → 272 px, remembered.
- Dragging / resizing: All view: the Scenes row's right edges change a scene's length (`changeScene` + `timeline.setDuration`; checked: Scene_04 3 → 3.52 s and the later scenes moved), the Camera bars move / resize, the Video part edges move the part boundaries (`__video[clip].segments`). Scene view: plan animation, camera and Reactor bars move and resize as before. A drag does not count as a click (the click after a drag no longer opens the scene). Not tried: video part edges, Reactor and plan bars.
- Test data restored: Scene_04 back to 3 s (15.5 s total) through the agent channel (v_69).

## Height grip on the top edge of the timeline panel
- The grip moved from under the header to the top edge of the whole expanded timeline panel (a host div appended to `[data-slot="timeline-panel"]`, `data-mo-tracks-grip`), so dragging it moves the panel's top edge and the bottom stays on the bottom edge. Checked: panel top 642 → 602 px after a 20 px drag (frame), bottom 892 px; the grip's top equals the panel's top. Double click returns to the automatic height (localStorage cleared).

## Drag icons in the timeline
- Cause of no drag icon: the theme sets `cursor: default !important` on every element (`src/toolcraft/runtime/styles.css`), so the cursors in the module CSS never showed. `styles.css` now overrides it for `data-drag` parts of the timeline with a more specific rule: `move` (bars that can be moved) = grab, `resize` (right edge handle, now 10 px wide with a grip line seen on hover) = ew-resize, `height` (the grip on the top edge) = ns-resize; while a drag goes on `html[data-mo-dragging]` sets grabbing / ew-resize / ns-resize over the whole app. Checked with computed cursors: grab, ew-resize, ns-resize; grabbing while dragging.

## Timeline resize keeps the bottom fixed; the strip follows at once; scenes follow a resized scene
- Cause of the bottom moving while the height was dragged: only the CSS height changed and the bottom-edge keeper (250 ms) moved the panel afterwards, so the panel first grew downwards. `startResize` (`motion-timeline.tsx`) now sets the height variable, the panel's transform (`startOffsetY - growth`) and the stored offset in the same step, and the keeper skips while `html[data-mo-dragging="height"]`. The scenes strip re-measures on `mo-timeline-layout` and writes its position straight onto its element, so it moves with the panel (8 px gap) instead of 300 ms later.
- Checked with synthetic drag steps (-10, -30, -60 px): host bottom stayed 892 px at every step, host top moved by exactly the drag, strip bottom stayed 8 px above it.
- Resizing a scene in the Scenes row now moves the scenes after it with it while dragging (they used to jump when released).

## Timeline anchored bottom-middle by CSS; strip placed from the same variable
- The timeline panel's position is no longer worked out in JS: `styles.css` gives the panel host `transform: translateX(var(--mo-timeline-x)) translateY(calc(100dvh - 20px - 100%)) !important`, i.e. its bottom edge is always 10 px above the window's bottom, whatever its height. `mo-viewport.ts` only keeps `--mo-timeline-x` (the middle of the free area between the project sidebar and the inspector) up to date every 250 ms. The old height keeper and the per-move position dispatches are gone.
- The scenes strip's `bottom` is `calc(10px + var(--mo-tracks-height, 36px) + 8px)`: the same variable that sets the panel's height, so panel and strip change in the same frame (no measuring delay). The height drag now only sets that variable.
- Checked with drag steps (-10, -40, -80, +20 px): bottom 892 px at every step, top moved by the drag, strip 8 px above, both 268 – 1285 px across.

## Same timeline height for every scene
- The automatic height used the number of rows of the view (a scene with a camera row only was shorter than All). It is now always 6 rows (`AUTO_ROWS` in `motion-timeline.tsx`; more rows scroll). Checked: 216 px for All and for Start, Scene_02 – Scene_05 and End.

## Toolbar no longer jumps to the bottom on a window resize
- Cause: the toolbar's default place is the bottom middle and its top-right place was only a stored offset, so every resize put it back at the bottom until the debounced refit (120 ms) moved it up again. Like the timeline it is now placed by CSS (`styles.css`: `translateY(calc(20px - 100dvh + 100%))`, always 10 px from the top) and only its place across is a variable (`--mo-toolbar-x`, set by `mo-viewport.ts` on the window's resize event and every 250 ms: over the inspector, or just left of it when the inspector is on the left half); `--mo-column-width` is set there too.
- Checked by sampling every 16 ms while the window went from 1583 × 902 to 1300 × 760: the toolbar's top was 10 px in all 291 samples; it moved straight from x 1274 to 990 with the inspector.

## Inspector stays on the right edge during a window resize
- The inspector's place was a stored offset on top of its default (top right), which the framework re-worked on a resize and which overshot the right edge before settling. `styles.css` now fixes its transform to `translateY(var(--mo-toolbar-band))` (!important), so it keeps its default place, 10 px from the right and top edges, 46 px lower for the toolbar; `mo-viewport.ts` sets `--mo-toolbar-band` with the toolbar's values. Dragging the inspector to another place no longer works.
- Checked by sampling every 16 ms while the window went from 1549 × 902 to 1300 × 760: the inspector's right gap was 10 px in all 308 samples (never past the edge), top 56 px, bottom 892 → 750 px; the toolbar's top 10 px throughout.

## No shaking while the chat area / window is resized
- The scene was fitted 120 ms after the last resize event and the panels were re-placed (extra renders) every time, so while the chat area was dragged the scene stood still (or lagged) and jumped. `useMoViewport` (`mo-viewport.ts`) now fits the scene on every animation frame of a resize (one per frame, `requestAnimationFrame`) and once more 300 ms after it stops; the panel placements were taken out of that path (the panels are placed by CSS).
- Checked by sampling every frame while the window went 1549 → 1300 px: the artboard's centre was 0 px off the middle of the free area between the sidebar and the inspector in all 355 frames (it changed from 688 to 496 px wide in one frame).

## Inspector stays visible when the chat area is dragged wider (pane narrower than 1024 px)
- Cause: the framework gives the app a fixed inline `min-width: 1024px`, so with a browser pane narrower than that the right side (toolbar + inspector, at x 714 – 1014 in a 700 px pane) sat outside the pane. `styles.css` sets `[data-slot="toolcraft-runtime-app"] { min-width: 0 !important }`. Checked in a 700 px emulated pane: app width 700, inspector right edge 690 (10 px gap), toolbar the same; the sidebar and the inspector then overlap the middle (free area under 160 px, so the scene uses the whole width), and the strip hides itself under 120 px.

## Clicking a scene opens the timeline on that scene's own duration
- Clicking a scene (strip block or Scenes bar) now opens (expands) the timeline when it is closed (`timeline.setExpanded`) and, in the scene view, the ruler is that scene's own time: 0 → its length (`rangeStart` / `rangeLength` in `motion-timeline.tsx`), ticks every 0.5 – 4 s depending on the length, the playhead, bar times and the drag scale are all scene-local, bars outside the scene are not drawn, the left label reads "Scene_03 · 3s". All keeps the full motion. Checked: collapsed timeline (36 px) → click Scene_03 → 216 px, ticks 0 – 3 s, camera bar 0s → 0.8s. Toolcraft's own header (Duration, time) still shows the whole motion.

## Scene view keeps whole-motion time
- In the scene view the ruler still shows only that scene's part (zoomed), but its ticks, the label ("Scene_03 · 5s → 8s"), the bar tooltips and the playhead use whole-motion time, in step with Toolcraft's header (5.75 / 15.5s). Checked: ticks 5s … 8s, camera bar "5s → 5.8s", playhead at 25 % of the ruler at 5.75 s.

## Draggable playhead with time and frame
- The playhead (`motion-timeline.tsx`) has a knob and a 14 px wide grab area (`data-drag="playhead"`, ew-resize cursor); pressing the ruler, an empty place of a row or the playhead moves it there and dragging keeps moving it (playback stops while it is dragged). A label beside the knob shows the time and the frame: "6.54s · frame 392" (frame = time × the render's frame rate: `render.videoFps`, or `render.gifFps` for GIF; 60 by default); it flips to the left near the right edge. While dragging, the ruler keeps the range it had (the scene that follows the playhead does not change what is under the pointer). Checked in Scene_03 (5 s → 8 s): pointer steps gave 5.38s/323, 5.96s/358, 6.54s/392, 7.69s/461 and clamped at 5s (frame 300) to the left.

## Playhead label: seconds:frame, top middle, flips at the ends
- The label now reads "9:50 sec:frame": whole seconds, then a frame counter that runs 0 – 99 and starts again every second (frame = floor(fraction of the second × 100)); it replaces "time · frame N". It sits at the top, centred on the playhead; within 52 px of the start it moves to the right of the playhead and within 52 px of the end to the left (`stampStart` / `stampEnd` in `motion-timeline.module.css`). Checked in All: 9.5 s → "9:50", 7.75 s → "7:75", centred (0 px off) in the middle, +42 px at the start, −45 px at the end, fully inside the ruler (0 px to its edge at 0:00 and 15:50).
- Fix on the way: the CSS edit dropped `.empty` and `.grip` for a moment; both restored.

## Playhead label without the "sec:frame" text
- The label shows only "9:50" (seconds:frame); the unit text and its style were removed.

## Scenes strip: all scene blocks the same width
- The blocks of the scenes strip are equal in width (`flex: 1 1 0`, was proportional to the scene length); the length is still written in each block. The strip's playhead runs through the block of the scene it is in by that scene's own part of the time, so it is on a block's left edge when a scene starts. Checked: six blocks of 137 px each (All tab 64 px); playhead x at 1.5 s / 5 s / 8 s / 11 s / 14 s / 15.5 s = 138 / 277 / 416 / 554 / 693 / 832 px of 833.

## Scenes strip: the scene blocks start where the timeline's rows start
- The All tab is now 119 px wide (the timeline's label column of 124 px plus its border, less the strip's own border, padding and gap) and the strip has no padding on the right, so the six scene blocks run exactly over the timeline's ruler: both start at x 393 and end at x 1174 px (checked), each block 129 px wide.

## A line where the rows start
- A 1 px vertical line (foreground at 45 %) marks where the timeline's rows start: the right border of the label column (`.label` in `motion-timeline.module.css`, on every row and on the ruler's label cell) and, over the scene blocks above, a line at their left edge (`.blocks::before` in `scenes-strip.module.css`). Both are at x = 393 px (checked), so they read as one line.

## The start line runs to the bottom of the timeline
- The line where the rows start was a border of the label cells, so it stopped under the last row when the timeline was tall. It is now one element (`.startLine` in `motion-timeline.module.css`, `top: 0; bottom: 0` of the panel's content, at the label column's right edge). Checked with a 400 px tall timeline: line from y 529 to y 891 (panel bottom 892), x 392, the same x as the scene blocks' line above.

## One line at the start, not two
- It was doubled in the ruler: the tick at 0 s (1 px, `.tick`) sat next to the start line (1 px) so two lines showed 1 px apart. The first tick of the range is now transparent (`.tickFirst`), its label stays. Checked: one visible line at the start of the timeline (x 391.5); the scenes strip above has its own single line.

## Strip and timeline playheads at the same place (real time for both)
- The scenes strip's blocks are as wide as the scenes are long again (not equal widths) and run over the timeline's rows, so both playheads are at the same x. The blocks had extra width from their padding / border (a flex base size cannot be below them), which shifted their edges by up to 8 px; padding moved to the texts, the block has no border, and the space between blocks is cut with `clip-path: inset(0 1px round 5px)`. Checked in All at 0.5 – 14 s: the two playheads differ by 0.5 px (their different widths) at every time; the strip's block edges (393, 469, 645, 796, 947, 1098) match the Scenes row's bars (393, 468, 644, 796, 947, 1098) within 1 px. In a scene view the timeline's ruler is zoomed to the scene, so the strip (always the whole motion) shows the playhead at its real place in the whole motion.

## Scenes strip: drag icon, duplicate icon, Delete key
- Each scene block (`scenes-strip.tsx`) has a grip (drag to move the scene to the block it is dropped on, `moveScene`, a bar marks the drop place) and a copy button (`addScene`: a copy after it, shown) at its top right, seen over the block the pointer is on and over the scene that is shown. The Delete (or Backspace) key removes the scene that is shown (`removeScene`) when the scene view is on and no text field has the focus; the last scene cannot be removed.
- Checked: 6 grips and 6 copy buttons; duplicate Scene_04 → 7 scenes, 18.5 s; Delete → 6 scenes, 15.5 s; dragging Scene_05 onto Scene_03 → Start, Scene_02, Scene_05, Scene_03, Scene_04, End (drop bar on the 3rd block), dragged back → original order, 15.5 s.

## Scenes strip: add (+) and rename (double click)
- `scenes-strip.tsx`: a plus button next to "All" adds a new scene (`addScene`) and shows it; double click on a scene block opens an inline name field (Enter/blur saves via `changeScene {name}`, Escape cancels, 40 characters, empty names ignored). Keys typed in the field do not reach the Delete shortcut.
- "All" tab narrowed to 91 px so the scene blocks still start where the timeline rows start.
- Verified: add (6 → 7), rename to "Outro". My test Delete presses removed two scenes; End was restored from the saved project through the agent channel.

## Scenes strip: round hover "+" between scenes
- Replaced the fixed add button with mini round "+" buttons at the start, between every two scenes and at the end; they show on hover and insert a new scene right there (`insertAt`: `addScene` then `moveScene`). "All" tab is 119 px again.
- Verified: insert in the middle and at the start; test scenes deleted again (6 scenes).
- "All" tab got the same 5 px corner radius as the scene blocks.

## Scenes Segment panel: scene list removed
- `scenes-control.tsx`: removed the list of scenes (rename, move, duplicate, delete buttons) and "Add scene"; the scenes strip below the artboard does all of that now. The Segment wise preview switch, Length and the other settings stay.

## Interaction plan: time rail hidden
- `scroll-plan-control.module.css`: the dot/line/playhead rail beside the Interaction Segment and Animations cards is hidden (the scroll plan of landing pages keeps it); cards use the full width.

## Animations tab by layer + effect, keyframes in the timeline
- `animation-cards.ts` (new): `useAnimationCardTimes()` gives each Animations-tab card (layer · effect) with the time its effects work, as the script reports it.
- `motion-timeline.tsx`: scene view gets one row per card (label = layer, bar = effect) with a keyframe diamond at each end (`keys`, `.keyed` in the CSS); plan animations (hand-built plans) are grouped by layer and have the same diamonds.
- `animation-controls.tsx`: the cards no longer show the "0.0s → 8.0s" time line; `interaction-plan-editor.tsx` (hand-built plan): animations grouped under layer headings, Delay/Duration sliders removed (drag the bars in the timeline).
- Limit: the design's own effects have no time controls (the script decides), so their bars are not draggable; hand-built plan bars are.

## All selected: Interaction Segment lists every segment
- `animation-controls.tsx`: with "All" chosen in the scenes strip (several scenes) the plan is not filtered to the shown scene's role, so Interaction Segment lists all segments; `segment-schedule.ts` then measures from the motion's start (offset 0).
- Verified: Scene_04 shows its one segment; All shows the 10 (Start … End).

## Camera moved into the Animations tab
- `scenes-control.tsx`: the Camera editor is no longer in the Scenes Segment panel; `SceneCameraCard` (same controls, without Move starts / Move takes: those are the camera bar in the timeline) is the first card of Interaction plan › Animations (scene view), in the design-motion plan and the hand-built plan editor.

- Camera card is now a layer-style card ("Camera · Zoom, angle, tilt"): folded by default, an eye turns the camera on/off, clicking the card opens its settings (`SceneCameraCard` in `scenes-control.tsx`, built on `RailCard`).

- Camera bars in the timeline (scene view and All) now carry keyframe diamonds at the start pose and the end pose, like the layer effects (`keys: true` in `motion-timeline.tsx`).

- All view of the timeline no longer has a Camera row; the camera's keyframes show only in the scene's own view.

## Scenes Segment section hidden
- `app-schema.ts`: the inspector's Scenes Segment section never shows (applicability that cannot match); the control stays registered so the scenes keep their saved value. Scenes are edited in the strip and the timeline.

## Animations tab filtered per scene
- `animation-cards.ts` `effectsForRole`: in a scene's view the Animations tab (and the timeline's layer rows) list only the effects on layers that scene's segment names (Scene_03: Occupancy, Net operating income · Rent collection; Scene_04: Maintenance, …; Scene_05: Numbers). A layer no segment names (Dot charts) stays in every scene with a segment. All view and scenes without a role are not filtered.

## Data change keyframes, layer by layer, in each scene
- `animation-cards.ts`: `SegmentTiming` (from the scene's segment: its Delay and each layer's Time control); the timeline's scene view gets a row per layer with a keyframe bar (`kind: "timing"`): drag moves it (segment Delay), the right edge changes the layer's Time. Verified on Scene_03 (Occupancy · Rent collection 6.2 → 7.2 s; dragged and put back). The whole-scene effect bars now start at the scene start.

## Choosing a scene loops it
- `scenes.ts` `loopScene`: choosing a scene (strip or a scene bar in the timeline) turns segment wise preview on and starts playing, so the scene's section animation plays on a loop; All turns it off again (full motion).

## Timeline: select, unselect and keyboard on bars
- `motion-timeline.tsx`: a click picks a bar (outline); Esc or a click on empty track lets it go; Left/Right nudge a picked editable bar 0.05 s (Shift 0.5 s); Delete/Backspace removes a plan animation or Reactor item, or turns the camera off. While a bar is picked the strip's Delete-key scene removal is off (`data-mo-timeline-selection` on the html element). Verified: pick, nudge ±0.2 s (put back), Esc.

## Scene select keeps play/pause; the loop only runs in a scene's view
- `scenes-strip.tsx` / `motion-timeline.tsx`: choosing a scene no longer starts playback; it stays paused or playing as it was (the scene's loop is on, so Play loops it).
- `scene-duration.ts`: the loop (and the follow-the-playhead rule) only applies in a scene's view; with All chosen, also right after a reload with the saved flag on, the full motion plays. Verified: select while paused stays paused, Play loops the scene, jump while playing keeps playing, All plays through the end.

- All view: the scene bars all have the same color (the shown scene is no longer lighter there; it was a leftover highlight).

- Clicking a scene bar in the All timeline opens the scene again (a real mouse press was swallowed by the playhead scrub; scene bars now stop it). Verified: click Scene_02 bar, Play loops Scene_02 (playhead 11–32%).

## Timeline All view: click picks a scene, Play loops it, double click opens it
- `motion-timeline.tsx`: one click on a scene bar picks it (outline, playhead to its start; the view stays All); with it picked, Play loops that scene (an effect keeps the playhead inside it); Esc / a click on empty track lets it go and Play runs the whole motion. Double click opens the scene (scene view). Verified with real clicks.

## Space plays/pauses wherever the focus is
- `mo-app-sidebar.tsx`: the Space shortcut no longer skips buttons, links, sliders, switches and tabs (a clicked scene block kept the key before); only text entry (text fields, textarea, select, menus, dialogs) keeps Space. On a button/switch the key goes to playback only (its own press is cancelled). Verified: click a scene block, Space plays, Space pauses.

## Keyframes are real, pickable points that come before the playhead
- `motion-timeline.tsx` / CSS: the diamonds on keyed bars are elements (`data-key` start/end). A press on one picks it (highlight) and moves the playhead there; where the playhead sits on a keyframe, the playhead's press is forwarded to the keyframe (`scrub` checks `elementsFromPoint`). On editable bars the start diamond drags the bar, the end diamond changes its length. Esc or empty track lets it go.

## Timeline scroll bar in its own column
- `motion-timeline.tsx/.css`: the rows' scroll bar has a 10 px column of its own (always shown, styled); the ruler (margin), the playhead overlay and the scenes strip (right padding) leave it free, so keyframes at the end of a row are not under the bar and the end of the scene blocks lines up with the end of the time (measured: ruler 1212.5, blocks 1213 px).

- Last bar's end is no longer cut off: bars are laid out over the narrower of the ruler and a row's track (it was 1 px wider), and the strip's right padding is 11 px so its blocks end with the track.

- Timeline scroll bar thinned: column 6 px (was 10), thumb 4 px wide; strip right padding 7 px.

- Timeline scroll bar: the native bar is hidden (it was always the browser's 11 px) and a thin 3 px indicator of our own sits in a 6 px column (drag it or use the wheel); ruler, playhead and strip leave that column free.

## Layers panel scrolls horizontally
- `src/styles.css`: `layers-panel-content` has overflow-x auto and its list is as wide as the widest row (min 100%), so long names and deep nesting can be scrolled to; the thin global scroll bar is used.

## Left sidebar fills the height
- `src/styles.css`: the sidebar dock is anchored to the bottom (10 px margin) and its panel stretches, so Projects and Layers end at the bottom of the viewport like the inspector.
- Layers list now fills the panel to its bottom edge, so its sideways scroll bar shows at the bottom of the panel (`styles.css`).

## Versions: reorder arrows
- `mo-versions-control.tsx`: instead of arrows each version row has a drag grip that shows on hover; drag the row to another place (a bar shows where it lands); the version on show stays on show. Verified with a real drag (last → first).

## Zoom in / out of the keyframe area and the scenes strip
- `timeline-view.ts`: shared zoom (`zoomTimelineAt`, `panTimeline`, `useTimelineZoom`, `handleTimelineWheel`): Ctrl/Cmd + wheel (a trackpad pinch) zooms around the pointer, a sideways wheel (or Shift + wheel) slides the window; zoom resets when the view or the scene changes.
- `motion-timeline.tsx`: the ruler and rows show only the window; `scenes-strip.tsx`: the blocks slide inside a window (`.zoomed`) so the strip shows the same time as the keyframe area.
- The wheel listeners sit on the window in the capture phase: the app's browser-zoom guard (`toolcraft-browser-zoom-boundary.tsx`) stopped Cmd/Ctrl + wheel before it reached the panels. Verified with Cmd + wheel events: the timeline zoomed to 7–8 s, the strip slid with it, zooming out over the strip reset both.
- Zoomed in, a thin sideways scroll bar runs along the bottom of the keyframe area (its thumb = the time shown; drag it to slide; the scenes strip follows). A sideways wheel or Shift + wheel slides too.

## Ruler with frame counts
- `motion-timeline.tsx`: the ruler's step follows the zoom (about 70 px between marks, from 10 s down to one frame, 0.01 s); marks under a second are labelled seconds:frame (frame 00–99 each second, like the playhead's label), e.g. 7:38 7:39 7:40; whole-second steps stay `4s`.

- The inspector section "Interaction plan" is now "Motion Plan" (schema title, descriptions and the timeline hint).

## Slide-in keyframes per layer, grouped, with their controls in the Animations tab
- Motion script (`design.motion`, also `docs/mo-app/propvera-header-motion.template.js`): `describe()` returns `slides` (layer id, step, start, end in the part's own time) for the zoom-in parts 04–06 (`slidesOf`). Applied to PropVera_Header_v2 through the agent channel (animation v91; versions v_72 and v_73 were saved: the first update was picked up before it could be replaced by the one with the controls).
- Controls: 12 `enter.*` controls (switch, step delay, slide time, slide by, blur, fade-out wait/time, cards wait, scene-06 time/slide, inside wait/blur) are declared as the card "Cards · Slide in" in Motion Plan › Animations (they were only defaults inside the script before).
- Timeline (scene view): a row per slide-in step ("Slide in 1" … with its layer count and keyframe diamonds); click its label to open the layers: one row per layer with its own keyframes. Not draggable (the times come from the controls above). `animation-cards.ts` `slides`, `motion-timeline.tsx` groups.
- Not done: `propvera-header-scenes.build.py` does not yet add the `enter.*` controls when it builds a project from the template.

## Slide in card simplified (applied: animation v92, version v_74)
- Controls of "Cards · Slide in" are now: Slide by (slider), Slide from (arrow segment: top, bottom, left, right, top-right, top-left, bottom-right, bottom-left), Duration (slider), Blur (switch + amount slider shown when on), Fade (switch), Easing (select: smooth out, linear, ease in, ease in-out, out cubic/quart/expo, back). The other enter.* values (step delay, fade-out wait/time, cards wait, inside layers wait/blur) stay as defaults inside the script.
- Script (`design.motion` and the template): slide direction (dx/dy), easing presets (`easeBy`), fade and blur switches; Scene_06's own slide (60 px, 0.7 s, out quart) is now stored in that scene's own settings instead of special-casing the scene in the script.
- Timeline: resizing the end of a slide-in bar (group or layer) changes Duration for all (tested 0.4 → 0.52 s, put back). The start of those bars is not draggable.
- Not done: the build script `propvera-header-scenes.build.py` still does not add these controls; versions v_72, v_73, v_74 were saved.

## Each slide-in step has its own card and settings (applied: animation v93, version v_75)
- Motion Plan › Animations shows a card per step ("Step 1 · Slide in" … "Step 6"), each with its own Slide by, Slide from (arrows), Duration, Blur (switch + amount), Fade (switch) and Easing; only the steps the scene has are listed (Scene_04: 1–6, Scene_05: 1–4, Scene_06: 1, 3, 4). The one shared card is gone.
- Script (`design.motion`, template): the slide-in reads `enter.<n>.slide/from/time/blurOn/blur/fade/easing` per step (defaults for steps 1–8 in the script); Scene_06's slower slide is stored in that scene's own settings for all its steps.
- Timeline: resizing the end of a step's bars changes that step's Duration only (tested: step 2 of Scene_06, 0.7 → 0.8 s, put back); the whole-scene "Cards" row is gone.
- Still not done: the build script does not add these controls; versions v_72–v_75 were saved.

## Versions: only the latest two listed, older ones in a fold
- `mo-versions-control.tsx`: the list shows the last two versions; the rest are behind "▸ Older versions (n)" (folded on load). Open it and drag a version's grip out of the fold into the list (or the other way) to move it; the order is the same list, so a version dragged into the last two pushes another into the fold. Arrow-key jumping opens the fold when it reaches an older version. Verified with real drags (v_58 → end, then back to the start).
- The fold of older versions is a filled block (a soft fill behind the toggle and its versions), so they read as a group apart from the two latest.
- The older-versions fill shows only while the pointer is over the block or it is expanded (transparent when folded and idle).

## Picking a bar opens its card in the Animations tab
- `timeline-view.ts` `setPickedCard` / `usePickedCard`: a bar picked in the timeline (slide-in step or layer, effect row, camera) tells the Animations tab which card goes with it; the tab switches to Animations, that card opens (and scrolls into view), and when the bar is let go (Esc, click on empty track, Delete) the cards fold. Verified with real clicks: Slide in 2 bar → "Step 2 · Slide in" opened; click on empty track → folded. Bars of the Data change rows (Interaction Segment tab) have no card.

## Choosing a layer opens its card, in whichever tab has it
- `motion-timeline.tsx`: when a layer is chosen (Layers panel or canvas) its card is picked the same way a timeline bar picks one: a slide-in layer → "Step n · Slide in", else the effect card of that layer (e.g. "Numbers · Shapes"), else the Interaction Segment card whose controls name that layer (`SegmentTiming.group`). `animation-controls.tsx` switches the Motion Plan tab (segment cards are the dated groups, the rest Animations) and opens the card; nothing chosen folds the cards. Verified: Rail bottom (fg2-713) → Animations tab, Step 2 open. Segment path and deselect not tested.

## Timeline pick ↔ Layers panel, and a scene picked on All filters the Motion Plan
- `motion-timeline.tsx`: picking a bar with a layer (a slide-in layer, an effect row, a Data change row) selects that layer (`layers.select`) and the left sidebar opens its Layers tab (`mo-show-layers` event, `mo-app-sidebar.tsx`); `notePickedLayer()` in `mo-layer-selection.ts` keeps the row highlighted (a layer chosen by the app was otherwise treated as automatic and its highlight removed). Verified: Rail bottom bar → Layers tab, fg2-713 selected.
- `timeline-view.ts` `setSceneFilter`/`useSceneFilter`: with All shown, one click on a scene bar picks it (the scene on show is that scene, the view stays All) and Motion Plan lists only that scene's segments and effects (Interaction Segment: 10 → 1 for Scene_05; the camera card shows); Esc or a click on empty track lets it go. Verified.
- The Layers panel is 248 px wide like the Projects panel (it was 240 px), `src/styles.css`.

- All view of the timeline no longer has a Reactor row (each scene's view has its Reactor items).

- Ruler: mini lines between the marks (4 or 5 parts of the step, none when a mark is a single frame).

- Ruler: a mark's label that would run past the right edge (the last one) sits on the left of its line, so it is no longer cut off.

## Cursor (Reactor) controls in Motion Plan › Interaction Segment (applied: animation v94, version v_76)
- The cursor's way is in the motion script (the Reactor cursor items follow it), so the controls feed the script: `cur.01` Cursor in (Comes in from: 8 sides; Position X / Y; Path; Easing), `cur.02` Move for Arrows (Position X / Y of the right arrow, Path, Easing), `cur.07` Back twice (left arrow position, Path, Easing), `cur.08` Cursor out (Leaves to: 8 sides, Path, Easing). Paths: straight, curve left/right, big curve left/right, S wave (`curvePoint`); easing presets share the slide-in's (`easeBy`) plus ease in-out. Defaults reproduce the old motion exactly (checked numerically).
- Script fix (`val`): string settings were ignored before (they fell back to the defaults), so the slide-in's Slide from and Easing selections did nothing; strings now pass through, and Scene_06's out-quart easing now applies.
- Template (`propvera-header-motion.template.js`) has the same changes. Versions v_72–v_76 were saved. The page lost the project name after a reload (the update waited); opening PropVera_Header_v2 from the Recent list fixed it.

- Reactor editor: the Start, Duration and Hold sliders are removed (the timing is on the timeline bars).
- Motion Plan: a scene with no segment or no animation still shows the Interaction Segment and Animations tabs, with a short "This scene has no …" line in the empty one (`animation-controls.tsx`).

- Interaction Segment cards no longer list the seconds sliders (delay before click, click time, hold, count time, stagger …): the times are set on the timeline's bars. Cards keep their description and the non-time controls (cursor position, path, easing, entry side). `animation-controls.tsx`.

## Video plan restored
- The Video row of the All timeline was empty: the project's live settings had lost the video plan (`animation.params.__video`, three parts 0–6.5 s ▶, 6.5–15.5 s ▶, 15.5–24 s ◀ reverse); version v_76 (saved by the cursor-controls update, after the page lost its project name and the project was re-opened from Recovered) did not carry it, v_75 and earlier did. Put back from v_75 through the agent channel (`params`, saveVersion false), nothing else changed.

## Video preview toggle: Static / Playable
- `video-preview-toggle.tsx` (new), shown at the top of Motion Plan (above Interaction Segment | Animations; also in the hand-built plan editor) when the project has video or GIF layers. Static holds the videos on the first picture of their plan in the preview (`mediaTimeSeconds: 0`, so nothing is decoded while playing or scrubbing); Playable plays them. Preview only, Render is not affected; the choice is kept in this browser (`mo.preview.videoStill`, `timeline-view.ts`). Verified: the artboard changes between the two.

- Picking a video part in the timeline selects its video layer (Layers tab) and opens the Modifier tab with the layer's properties (Start, End, Fit, Reverse, Sound; `ClipSettings`). `motion-timeline.tsx` (`properties` on video bars). Verified: second video part → Imge-154 selected, Modifier shows the Video section.

## Static = the picture the design carries for the video (nothing sent per frame)
- Static no longer sends a picture for the video with each frame (`animation-sandbox.ts`, `__videoStill`): the design then draws the still picture it carries for that layer (its poster), so nothing is decoded or copied per frame and the rest of the motion keeps full speed (the per-frame copy of a decoded picture could slow the motion down). Layers the runtime draws itself (video or GIF layers placed by the app) still use one decoded picture (`stillOf`). Preview only; Render is not affected. Verified: Static shows the poster with the slide-ins running over it.
## Frozen motion in All (investigation, no code change)
- Reproduced: during playback the preview delivers 0–4 pictures per second in this project (the canvas reports 'A picture took longer than 6000 ms'; delivered/skipped-stale counters in canvas.dataset). It is not specific to Static (same with Playable) or to All: each frame draws ~250 layers (the Data change scenes also blur/fade/slide up to 327 layers), and the adaptive quality (down to 0.5 resolution) cannot make that cheap. A test with the blur of all slide-in steps off did not change the rate (2.9 pictures/s), so the cost is the number of layers, not the blur. The project's Rendered preview (pre-rendered video) is the existing way to play it smoothly. The blur test was put back (enter.n.blurOn true).

## Pager: "02 / 09 / 24", the pill grows to the left (applied: animation v97, versions v_77, v_78)
- Pager pages are "NN / 09 / 24" (`PAGER.pages` in the motion script). The pill (Pager background), the left chevron and the text moved/grew 23 px to the left (right edge, right chevron and the plus button stay): design items fg2-698 (x 1294, w 142), fg2-697 (x 1309), fg2-696 (x 1335, w 60) and their clip rectangles (the pager group's and the pill's clips were still the old size, which cut the left chevron off until they were widened).
- Reactor cursor: the left arrow's place in the script (`ARROWS.left` x 1317) and the 'Back twice' Position X default (1318) follow the chevron, so the cursor tip lands on it. Verified at 4.9 s (page 02, right arrow) and 17.0 s (left arrow circle under the cursor). The 23 px is an estimate of the extra text width; if the text looks off-centre it is a one-number change.

## The Reactor cursor follows the motion's cursor (the Position / Path / Easing sliders now move it) (applied: animation v99, versions v_79, v_80)
- Cause: the project had no cursor layer (older versions had `motion-cursor`), so the Reactor items drew the cursor at their own stored positions (the old arrow place, 83.8 %) and ignored the script's cursor: the cursor sat beside the new left arrow and the Interaction Segment sliders (script settings) did nothing to it.
- Fix: the layer `motion-cursor` (Cursor) is back in the animation's layers and the Layers panel (top), and the motion script has `drawLayer` (a small mark at the cursor's tip, nothing while the cursor is off the picture) so the kit tracks it; the Reactor icons now follow the script's cursor in the preview and the render. `propvera-header-motion.template.js` has the same `drawLayer`.
- Verified: at 17.8 s the pointer is on the left arrow (new place); Position X dragged left moved the pointer left, put back to 1318 and it returned. A `layers` update made the video plan disappear once (it is saved only in the live params); the plan was put back from version v_78 (3 parts).

- Interaction Segment lists only the cursor's moves (the Reactor cursor cards with Position / Path / Easing / entry or exit side): Cursor in (Scene_01), Move for Arrows (Scene_02), Back twice (Scene_07), Cursor out (Scene_08); All shows the four. A scene where the cursor does not move (Start, Scene_03–06, End) shows "The cursor does not move in this scene." instead of a card. `animation-controls.tsx` (segments with `cur.*` controls).

The pill, left arrow, text and the clips grew 29 px to the left in total (text x 1329, left arrow x 1303, pill x 1288 / w 148; `ARROWS.left.x` 1311, 'Back twice' Position X default 1312). ## Right arrow 'selected' in the data scenes and the little square beside it (applied: animation v102, versions v_82, v_83)
- The data scenes (04–06) have no click, but the cursor rests at the right arrow there, so the arrow was lit as if hovered: the script now skips the arrow highlight in those parts (`if (DATA[stage]) break`).
- The little square was the tracking mark the new `drawLayer` draws at the cursor's tip (6×6 px): in scenes with no Reactor items the Cursor layer is not removed from the picture, so the mark showed. `drawLayer` now draws it only in the tracking pass (the layer drawn alone, `frame.layerStack.length === 1`), never in the picture. Template has both changes. Verified in Scene_07: the pointer sits on the left arrow with no mark; Scene_06 itself could not be framed (its camera moves the pager out of view in my crops).
