## Sticky presets (any project can use them)
- `Modifier › Sticky › Presets` (new control `stickyPresets`, `src/app/animation/sticky-presets.tsx`): the + button saves all the sticky settings of the project (ratios, top and bottom item with their pictures, text, size, align, offset) under a name; click a preset to use it in the open project (it shows as active while the settings match); its x (on hover) deletes it. A preset made from the Loop Wheel animation project ("Loop Wheel") is saved.
- Kept by the auto-save helper in `~/Documents/Mo_App/Presets/sticky/<name>.json` (new `/presets`, `/presets/save`, `/presets/delete` in `scripts/project-autosave.mjs`; client `src/app/mo/mo-presets.ts`), so every project reads the same list. The helper had to be restarted (npm run dev) to get the new routes. `mo pr` does not copy the Presets folder yet.
- Sticky preset chips: double click the name to rename it (Enter saves, Esc cancels; a taken name is refused), a duplicate icon makes "<name> copy", and a delete icon removes it (the icons show on hover). Verified: duplicate, rename and delete on a test copy; only "Loop Wheel" is left.

## Design background as the artboard's background
- New choice in Modifier › BG Setting › Background: **Design background** (`bg.mode = design`). The artboard is filled with the design's own background (its lowest layer that covers the whole picture; here PropVera background, the grey gradient #8798a6 → #384047, top to bottom) stretched over the whole artboard, so there is no white around the design on other ratios (`readDesignBackground` / `drawMockup` in `backdrop.ts`, used by the preview and the render). Set for PropVera_Header_v2 (applied through the agent channel, no new version). Verified in the preview on the 2:3 artboard: the gradient fills it behind the sticky items and the design.

## Motion type Animation_v2
- New Motion Type **Animation_v2** (`motion.type = animation2`, `motion-type.ts`): all the settings of the Interaction Animation as they are now (scenes strip, Motion Plan with cursor and slide-in cards, Reactor, keyframe timeline, sticky presets, design background) under their own name; the app treats it like Interaction Animation (`isSceneMotionType`, schema applicability `oneOf`). PropVera_Header_v2 uses it now. The Video preview Static / Playable toggle shows in Motion Plan only for a project that has a video or GIF layer (it is hidden otherwise).

## Timeline opens with the project and keeps its width when folded
- `scenes-strip.tsx`: the timeline is opened (`timeline.setExpanded`) when a project is opened and when the page is reloaded; folding it afterwards stays until the next open. `styles.css`: the timeline panel is as wide as the scenes strip (956 px here) whether it is open or folded (a folded timeline used to shrink to 256 px). Verified: after opening the project the timeline is open; folded, the bar keeps the same width and position under the strip.

- Timeline open on project open, second try (`scenes-strip.tsx`): the project sets the timeline up a little after it opens and left it folded after a reload (the first version of the fix only worked when the timeline was already open in the saved state). The strip now keeps it open for 20 s after a project opens or the page loads, unless a press on the timeline itself came first. Verified by reloading: open all 14 seconds; folding it with a click stays folded. Pressing Play on the folded timeline plays (button turns to Pause, 6.04 → 7.58 s).

- Folded timeline: its play line (`timeline-playback-line`) now starts under the first scene block and ends under the last (126 px from the panel's left to 8 px from its right), so it is as long as the strip's blocks and its end sits under the strip's playhead (`src/styles.css`).

  Measured: line 393 → 1216 px = the scene blocks (393 → 1216); at 7.22 s its blue progress ends at 640.7 px, the strip's playhead is at 639.7–641.7.
## The timeline follows the inspector's tab (corrects the earlier reading: it is the Motion tab, not the motion type)
- `scenes-strip.tsx`: the timeline opens when the inspector is on **Motion** and folds on **Project info** and **Modifier**; a project that opens or a page that reloads follows the tab it shows (kept for 20 s unless the timeline itself is pressed). Opening or folding it by hand holds until the tab changes. Verified by switching tabs: Project info c, Motion E, Project info c, Motion E, Modifier c, Motion E.

- The timeline opens and folds instantly (no height/fade animation on the timeline panel, `src/styles.css`), and the scenes strip rides on the timeline's top edge frame by frame while it changes (`scenes-strip.tsx`), so the two never show a gap or an overlap.

- Folding and opening the timeline is instant now: the tab press tells the page first (`html[data-mo-timeline=open|folded]`, `scenes-strip.tsx`), the panel's own script-driven height/width animation is overridden by CSS (`src/styles.css`), and the scenes strip is on the timeline's top edge in the same frame. Measured: folding goes 204 → 36 px in one frame with no gap between strip and timeline.

- The "All" tab of the scenes strip is now **Full motion** (it plays every scene one after another); the timeline's first row label in that view is "Full motion" too (`scenes-strip.tsx`, `motion-timeline.tsx`). The text fits the 119 px tab without clipping.

## Empty canvas + Animation_v2 starts with Start, Scene_01 and End
- When a project with no animation (an empty canvas) and no scenes yet is given the **Animation_v2** type, the scenes strip gets three scenes: `Start`, `Scene_01`, `End`; Start and End are a seventh of the timeline each (0.3 s – 1.5 s, in 0.05 s steps) and Scene_01 is the rest, so together they are the timeline's length (`defaultSceneSet` in `scenes.ts`, effect in `scenes-strip.tsx`). It happens once: after that the scenes are the project's own (add, delete, resize). Verified on a new empty project with an 8 s timeline: Start 1.15 s, Scene_01 5.7 s, End 1.15 s (test project deleted again).

- The strip's first tab and the timeline's first row label in that view are now **Overview** (was Full motion, before that All).

## 2026-10-15 — Scenes row as a sequence, draggable
- `src/app/animation/motion-timeline.tsx`: scene bars are numbered ("01 · Start"); dragging a scene bar sideways reorders it (drop line shows where it lands, `moveScene` + `controls.apply`); click/double-click/right-edge resize still work.
- `motion-timeline.module.css`: `.lifted`, `.dropLine`.
- Follow-up: the Scenes row is now a collapsible "Scenes" group (spans "N scenes") with one indented row per scene, stepping down in order; dragging still reorders; resizing a scene ripples the later rows.
- Scenes group bar shows a line and the number at each scene start (`.sceneMark`).
- Scene names that do not fit their bar are written beside it (right, or left near the end) with a short line (`.outLabel`).
- Scenes and the scenes strip now work for every motion type (`resolveProjectScenes` no longer gated; the strip shows even for one scene so scenes can be added); the Overview/scene timeline was already shared. Animation_v2 keeps its extras (Motion Plan cards, default scenes on an empty canvas). "1 scene" singular label.

## 2026-10-15 — Artboard setting
- Canvas width/height moved from Project info (hidden by CSS on the framework's fields) to Modifier › "Artboard setting" as "Artboard width" / "Artboard height" (`src/app/animation/artboard-size.tsx`, sets `canvas.size.width/height`, aspect ratio rules unchanged).
- "BG Setting" renamed "Artboard setting"; it holds the size, Background mode and "Background color" (now shown in every mode).
- Artboard setting: Background color is shown only when Background = Fill, with a Fill type (Solid / Gradient); Gradient adds "Gradient to" and "Gradient angle" (`bg.fillType`, `bg.fillTo`, `bg.fillAngle`, kept in versions). `drawMockup` paints the gradient for the live canvas and in Render; the solid color is still painted first.
- PropVera_Header_v2: the design's own background is a linear gradient (#8798a6 → #384047, top to bottom). The artboard now uses Fill › Gradient with the same two colors and 180° (applied through the agent channel, values only, no new version); the video plan was checked and is intact.
- Background: the "Design background" option is removed (old projects with it use Fill).

## 2026-10-15 — Tool rail
- New slim rail on the inspector's left edge (`src/app/mo/mo-tool-rail.tsx` + css, mounted in `animation-canvas.tsx`): a three-dot circle button at the top opens a menu with Pages, Filter effects and Tools (all "Soon", no behaviour yet). It follows the inspector's position; Esc or a click outside closes the menu.
- Tool rail: clicking the three-dot circle now shows the tools as a row of icon buttons (Pages, Filter effects, Tools) to its left instead of a list.
- Tool rail taller: 48px bar (was ~32px), 38px buttons, larger icons.
- Tool rail: width back to 40px (original icon size); only the height is taller (56px bar).
- Tool rail: the bar is 520px tall (RAIL_HEIGHT, or the inspector height if shorter), following the marked height; it does not run to the bottom of the window.

## 2026-10-15 — Perspective on any layer
- Tool rail: the Filter effects (wand) button now opens a "Perspective" section in the Modifier tab (hidden `tool.perspective` switch); click it again to close.
- Perspective works on any layer or group selected in the Layers tab (`src/app/animation/layer-perspective.ts`, `layer-perspective-control.tsx`): four corner X/Y offsets (±600 design px) kept in `animation.params.__layerPerspective`; Reset perspective clears one layer.
- Drawing: `sceneParams` adds `__perspectives` (the drawn layers each entry holds, groups expanded); the sandbox (`animation-sandbox.ts`, `drawLayers`) draws those layers on their own surface and warps them to the moved corners (projective mesh, 14×14 cells), at the place of the first layer in the stack. Used by the live canvas and Render. Not yet for video/GIF layers inside a perspective group.
- Tool rail flow: the three-dot circle opens the tools bar; the wand (Filter effects) adds the Perspective icon to the same bar (after a divider); Perspective's icon opens the section in Modifier (unfolded) for the layer selected in the Layers tab, and a second press closes it. The section is off when a project opens (`tool.perspective` reset on load). The rail counts as a panel in `mo-layer-selection.ts`, so clicking it no longer deselects the layer.
- Tool rail reorganised: the tall rail (green) lists all toolbars vertically under the three-dot circle (Pages, Filter effects, Tools); the tool picked there opens its own bar (red) beside the rail: Filter effects → Perspective icon, the others "coming soon".
- Tool rail directions swapped (confirmed): the three-dot circle opens a horizontal bar of pages (Pages, Filter effects, Tools); the page picked there lists its tools down the tall rail (Filter effects → Perspective cube; Pages and Tools "Soon").
- Rule: the Perspective icon is disabled ("select a layer first") while no layer or group is selected in the Layers tab; once on, it can still be switched off.
- Perspective tool now uses the frame's existing perspective settings, not new ones: Perspective pins switch, Reset pins, Select pin (TL/TR/BR/BL), Move selected pin (pad) and Precise X / Y (-0.5…0.5), same as Frame Settings shows with a Mockup background. For the selected layer or group each pin moves a corner of the layer's box by that fraction of its width/height (`__layerPerspective[id] = { enabled, points }`, same shape as the frame's); the sandbox warps to those corners. Not done: draggable pins on the canvas for layers (the frame's pins are tied to the mockup area).

## 2026-10-15 — Playhead alignment
- Folded timeline: the blue playback dot is moved onto the playback line (it was placed over the whole bar width while the line is inset under the scene cells), so it sits under the scenes strip's white playhead (`scenes-strip.tsx`, rAF effect).
- Opened timeline: the framework's centring shift (`translateX(-88px)` on `[data-slot="timeline-panel"]`) is switched off, so the open timeline lines up with the scenes strip at any window width (`styles.css`).
- Artboard centring: the tool rail takes 46px left of the inspector, so the canvas world is shifted left by 23px (`[data-toolcraft-canvas-world]` in styles.css); the artboard is centred between the project sidebar and the rail (18px each side at 56%).
- Folded timeline scrubbing: pressing/dragging on the playback bar now sets the time from the pointer's place on the blue line (the framework used the whole bar width, so the dot ran ahead of the cursor); transport buttons and the expand toggle are untouched (`scenes-strip.tsx`).
- Frame Settings: the Perspective pins block (switch, Reset pins, Select pin, Move pad, Precise X/Y, shown with a Mockup background) is removed, since Perspective now lives in the tool rail (Filter effects). The frame perspective that a project already has saved is still drawn; the code that draws it is untouched.

## 2026-10-15 — Group layers: Clip content and corner radius
- Selecting a group in the Layers panel now shows the Modifier Layer section (`useLayerActiveFlag` no longer skips groups; `layer-editor.tsx`): the group's visibility switch, **Clip content**, and **Corner radius** with an icon row (All corners, top left, top right, bottom right, bottom left) and a slider; "All" sets the four radii together, a corner icon edits that corner (`group-style-editor.tsx`, stored in `__groupStyle[groupId] = { clip, radius: [tl, tr, br, bl] }`).
- Drawing: a group that clips has its layers drawn together and cut to a rounded box around what they draw (sandbox `drawWarped` + `roundedBox`; shared with Perspective through `layerEffects` in `layer-perspective.ts`, which now returns `{ ids, points, clip }`). A layer that sits in two effects (a clipping group and a perspective layer inside it) is drawn by the first one only.
- Group clip: the clipping box is now pixel-exact (found on thin full-size strips after the coarse scan), so the rounded corner is a true quarter circle of the set radius and does not shift between pictures.
- Layer alignment row (Align left / top / Center / bottom / right, same icons as Frame Settings) in the Modifier Layer section for a selected layer or group: the drawing script moves the box around what the layer draws to that edge or the centre of the design (`layer-align.ts`, `layer-align-row.tsx`, `__layerAlign`, sandbox `drawWarped`). Pressing an icon again lets go of it; Center sets both axes. It is a standing rule, so a layer that moves in the animation stays on the edge.
- Group size: selecting a group shows Width, Height (px), Lock ratio and Scale (%) in the Modifier Layer section (`layer-size.ts`, `layer-size-editor.tsx`, stored in `__layerSize[groupId] = { lock, sx, sy }`). The natural size is the box around the design's own boxes of the layers the group holds; Width/Height set the scale across/down (together while Lock ratio is on), Scale sets both. The sandbox scales the group's drawn box from its top-left corner and then aligns the scaled box (`layerEffects().size`, `drawWarped`). Scale 100% with Lock ratio on removes the setting.
- Group transform also has Position X / Y (px) and Rotation (degrees, about the middle of the scaled box) next to Width, Height, Lock ratio and Scale (same `__layerSize` record: `x`, `y`, `rot`); the sandbox applies move, then rotation, then the scale from the top-left corner after alignment.
- Change (2026-10-15): the Layer section (`layerEditor`) is always shown on the Modifier tab; with no layer selected it shows "Select a layer in the Layers tab to edit it. Click outside to deselect it." Checked on PropVera_Header_v2: shown with nothing selected, and filled (Container: visibility, size, position) once a layer is picked. The 4 app-schema starter-contract tests fail the same way on main.
- Fix (2026-10-15): a group's Position X / Y (Layer section) showed only the move applied on top (0 by default). They now show where the group sits inside its parent group (or the design for a top-level group): its own place minus the parent's top-left, plus the move; editing writes the move as before. Block offsets and scale are included, which also fixes Width/Height when pasted blocks hold layers of the group (Container read 1520 × 1120 instead of 1440 × 1040). Checked on PropVera_Header_v2: Container = 1440 × 1040 at X 80, Y 80 in PropVera (1600 × 1200); +20 moved it 20 px and was set back.
- Change (2026-10-15): Artboard setting gets a Name input (`artboard.name`, single-line text, default "Artboard", first control of the section). It is a per-project modifier target (isModifierTarget), so it is saved with the project and a new project starts at the default. It is only stored for now (no canvas label or file name uses it yet). Checked on PropVera_Header_v2: typed value saved to the project file, other projects unaffected, set back to the default.
- Change (2026-10-15): the Frame Settings section is hidden in the Modifier tab (its control's applicability never matches, like the hidden flag controls). The control, its code and the stored `animation.frame` / `__frame` settings are unchanged, so projects that use them still draw as before; the Layer section covers size, position, clip, corner radius and align. Modifier now lists Layer, Artboard setting, Sticky.
- Change (2026-10-15): frame settings are off and not kept, for current and future projects. `FRAME_SETTINGS_ACTIVE = false` (frame-transform.ts): `readFrameTransform`, `readFrameStyle` and `readFramePerspective` return neutral values whatever a project stored (no card fill, stroke, radius, clip, position, scale, rotation, opacity or perspective), `resolveParams` drops `__frame` (the Frame layer choice), and Original size writes nothing for the frame. Saving a project (`createProjectFile`) no longer writes `animation.frame`, `__frame`, or the frame values inside saved versions; `animation.frame` is no longer a version target and old versions' copy never counts as a change or is applied on load. One-time sweep of ~/Documents/Mo_App/Recovered and Projects removed the stored values (backup copies of the changed files are in the session scratchpad: frame-backup/). Effect: designs that used the frame as their card (Loop Wheel, Hover Cards, Hover wise Card Reveal, Footer Text Hover, Step 03) lose that card background; BaseCase pages lose the frame's clip and corner radius. Checked: Loop Wheel and Hover Cards open and draw with no console errors; PropVera_Header_v2 file and its 4 versions hold no frame values and no false change dot. Tests: 18 pass in src/app/mo (2 new).
- Fix (2026-10-15, PropVera_Header_v2): only the picture (Imge-154) showed and the dashboard layers did not. Cause: layers were grouped into "Group 1" (Camera) and the runtime moved only the group's direct children, leaving their sub-layers further down the flat list; drawing order followed the flat list, so the picture was drawn above the whole dashboard. `getLayerStack` now orders layers by the tree the Layers panel shows (a group, then what it holds, top first), with a guard for cycles; a normal contiguous list gives the same order. Test file: src/app/animation/layer-stack.test.ts (2 tests). Checked in the app: Scene_07 camera view shows the Maintenance and Net operating income cards over the picture. A stray click on the timeline added a 0.1 s scene "Scene_11" (24.1 s); it was removed through the agent channel (10 scenes, 24 s again).
- Change (2026-10-15): the scene camera works on the design's layer group named "Camera". `animation/camera-group.ts` (+ `group-box.ts`, the group's box from its pasted layers with block offsets and the Layer section's size/position) turns that group's box into the camera's frame (percent of the picture) and reads the focus points as percent of the group (0 left/top, 100 right/bottom); preview (`animation-canvas.tsx`) and render (`mo-render.ts`) both apply it, so the frame follows the group when it is moved or scaled. Without a Camera group the camera keeps its own frame and focus in percent of the picture. The camera card hides Frame X / Y / width / height when the group exists (note shown); Frame corners stays. PropVera_Header_v2: scene cameras 04–07 converted from picture percent to group percent through the agent channel (same view as before: Scene_07 at 15.5 s zooms onto the Maintenance and Net operating income cards). Tests: camera-group.test.ts (3).
- Fix (2026-10-15): the timeline's Scenes row looked messy at the start (the "10 scenes" label sat under the numbered scene markers of the first, short scenes). The count moved into the row's name ("Scenes · 10"), the bar carries only the scene lines, and a number is written only where there is at least 20 px for it (closer scenes keep their line and a tooltip with the number and name). Checked on PropVera_Header_v2.
- Change (2026-10-15): the Scenes row's scene lines carry no numbers now. Resting the pointer on a scene of the row for half a second shows its name above the pointer (moving to another scene restarts the wait; leaving the row hides it). Checked in the app: no tooltip at 0.2 s, name at 0.7 s, new scene waits again, tooltip hides on leave.
- Change (2026-10-15): the Scenes row's name tooltip is per mark: the pointer must be on a scene mark (the nearest within 8 px) and rest 0.5 s; it then shows that scene's name above the mark. Between marks nothing shows. Checked: Start, Scene_04, Scene_05 and End marks.
- Fix (2026-10-15): the scene-name label on the Scenes row was drawn but placed off screen (the timeline panel is transformed, so a fixed label inside it was offset to y 1300 and clipped). It is now rendered into document.body (portal) directly above the hovered mark. Checked: Scene_04 label shown above its mark after 0.5 s; hides on leave.
- Fix (2026-10-15): the browser's own tooltip on the Scenes bar ("10 scenes · 0s → 24s") covered the scene-name label; the Scenes bar has no title attribute now, so only the per-mark scene name shows. Checked: no title on the bar or marks; marks 1, 3, 6 show Scene_01, Scene_03, Scene_06.
- Change (2026-10-15): the scene-name label waits 0.2 s (was 0.5 s) before showing (SCENE_TIP_DELAY_MS in motion-timeline.tsx). Checked: not shown at 80 ms, shown at 300 ms.
- Change (2026-10-15): the scene-name label on the Scenes row is faster and smooth: it waits 0.08 s (SCENE_TIP_DELAY_MS), fades and rises in (0.14 s), glides from mark to mark (the label stays 0.14 s while the pointer crosses between marks) and fades out. The app switches every CSS transition and animation off (mo/mo-instant-ui.ts), so this label sets its durations with !important to win over that rule; reduced-motion keeps only a short fade. Checked in the app: label appears at about 80 ms and fades in over ~100 ms; moving to another mark glides over several frames (464 → 896 px); it fades and is removed after leaving.
- Fix (2026-10-15): Clip content on a group did nothing visible: it cut to a rounded box around what the group draws, and the Container's picture (1520 × 1120) overhangs its own frame (1440 × 1040), so that box was the picture's. A clipping group now cuts to the box of its own background layer (the layer named "<group> background" inside it, as pasted from Figma; `clip.box` in the effect, `placedItemBox`), with its corner radius; aligning, scaling and turning work on that frame. A group without such a layer keeps the old box around what it draws. PropVera_Header_v2: Container (clip, radius 8): the picture no longer shows outside the frame. Tests: layer-clip.test.ts (2).
- Fix (2026-10-15): at a playhead where a scene camera is on, the Container's rounded corner was not visible and grey edges appeared: the camera works in a square window and adds a dark vignette towards its corners. The camera window now takes the corner radius of the Camera group or of the group that clips around it (cameraGroupArea `radius`, from __groupStyle), and the camera has a `vignette` setting ("Dark edges" switch on the camera card; on unless false, so older scenes are unchanged). PropVera_Header_v2: scenes 04–07 set to no dark edges. Checked at 8.08 s: the top-left corner is rounded and the pixels beside it are no longer darkened (207,236,253 inside, no 103,116,127 corner). Test frame-transform.test.ts updated for the frame settings being off; tests: 241 pass in src/app/animation, mo, media.
- Fix (2026-10-15): with the camera on, the Container's top-left corner was rounder (about twice) than the other three: the camera zoomed the picture's own rounded corner about the focus point at that corner. Now a scene whose camera works on the Camera group draws the picture with square corners (sceneParams zeroes the radius of the clipping group that supplies the window radius), the camera window rounds all four corners equally (a camera with rounded corners keeps drawing through its window even when it looks straight at the picture), and the window's four corner cut-outs are repainted with the artboard's background (drawCameraView `behind`, same painter as the plain artboard in preview and render). Camera vignette is skipped when the pose is flat. Checked at 8.08 s (camera on) and 23.75 s: corner depth TL 2.25, TR 2.5, BL 2.5, BR 3 px (before: TL 4.25 vs 2.5–3). Tests: 249 pass.

## 2026-10-20 — Edit the design directly on the artboard
- Request: click a layer to select it; drag to move and resize with corner handles; Shift-click multi-select and group resize; snap guides and distance labels; Delete/Backspace removes the selection; double-click a text to edit it.
- `src/app/animation/canvas-edit.tsx` (+ `.module.css`, mounted in `animation-canvas.tsx` next to the perspective pins): an overlay over the artboard. Click picks the topmost layer that paints something and is not the background (a layer covering 90% of the artboard); empty space (or Escape) lets go; Shift-click adds or removes; a drag moves the selection (Shift = one axis, Alt = no snapping); the four corner handles resize it (Shift = keep proportions) and a group of layers scales together; blue outlines and handles keep a constant size at any zoom; red snap lines and gap labels show while dragging; Delete/Backspace runs `layers.delete` for the selection (not when focus is in a field, a panel or an open menu); double-click on a text opens an in-place editor (Enter or click away saves, Escape cancels; a text whose font is missing stays locked, as in the Layer section).
- `src/app/animation/layer-geometry.ts` (+ test): layer boxes in design pixels, hit test, move/resize maths, snapping and gaps. It writes the same data the Layer section does: `params.__pos` (move), `params.__text` (text) and a new `params.__grow` (stretch from the layer's top-left corner). One drag is one undo step (history group).
- `src/app/mo/figma-paste-import.ts` (design script): reads `params.__grow` (scale from the layer's top-left corner on top of any motion scale). Designs always run the current script, so existing projects get it.
- `src/app/mo/mo-layer-selection.ts`: `dismissLayerSelection`, and the artboard overlay counts as part of the editor (a click on it is not a click "outside").
- On while the artboard shows a plain, still view of the scene being edited: off while playing, with a camera, perspective, a mockup, a Landing Page, a rendered preview, or when the playhead is in a scene that is not the active one.
- Known limits: layers move by their pasted place (a Reactor or motion offset is not followed); resizing stretches the drawn layer (text glyphs and stroke widths stretch too); Delete of several layers is one undo step each.
- Checked in the browser (Cargora and PropVera test copies, deleted afterwards): select, hover, move, resize, multi-select + group resize, guides and gap labels, Delete, undo, text edit and Escape; `tsc` and `vitest run src/app/animation src/app/mo` pass (26 new geometry tests).
- Dragging on the artboard (follow-up): a fast drag used to draw the whole design again for every move, so the picture lagged behind the pointer. Now `src/app/animation/drag-preview.ts` draws the design in three pieces once when a drag starts (everything below the moved layers, the moved layers alone, everything above, at 60% of the preview resolution); every move only slides or stretches the middle piece over the others (a few image copies, drawn through the same `drawFramedBitmap`, which now takes a canvas as well as a bitmap), the outline follows the pointer from live boxes, and the values are written once when the pointer is let go (one undo step), after which the design is drawn in full. `animation-canvas.tsx` registers the host that draws the pieces and repaints in full when a drag ends (also when nothing changed); a design that cannot be split falls back to writing every move. Measured on the Cargora design with 60 fast moves: about 2 ms per move, no sandbox frames during the drag (before: about 25 ms per move and a redraw for most moves), the layer lands where it was let go and the inspector shows the same position. While a layer is dragged, effects that read what is behind a layer (blend modes, background blur) are not shown for it; the full picture is right again on release.

## 2026-10-20 — Editable vector fill/stroke, side-stroke lines, both blurs (app + Figma plugin)
- Request: vectors and shapes carry Figma fill and stroke (gradients too); a frame/auto layout with a stroke on one side becomes a line shape with the same stroke; a layer with background blur and layer blur imports both.
- Plugin `code.js`: a single vector, boolean shape, star, polygon, line, ring/arc with solid/gradient paints (no dashes, arrow heads, inner shadow or glass) sends its outline (`paths`: `fillGeometry` data + winding rule) instead of an SVG or picture, plus stroke cap, join, miter and align; a frame, auto layout or rectangle with strokes on unequal sides sends `sideStrokes { sides, paints }` and no all-round stroke. (Effects such as shadow and both blurs travel as before; the plugin already sent every effect.)
- App `figma-paste-import.ts`: `FigmaItem.paths` (drawn through Path2D with the layer's fills incl. gradients, strokes with cap/join and inside/outside/center, drop shadow, layer and background blur clipped to the outline), `FigmaItem.line` (one line layer per stroked side, on top inside the frame; a box with only a side stroke is the line itself). `figma-layer-settings.tsx`: gradient strokes are editable like gradient fills; no corner radius for outlines and lines. `mo-layer-icons.ts`: outlines show the vector icon.
- Checked in the browser on a throwaway design (deleted afterwards): gradient star with a white stroke, ring with an inside gradient stroke, a frame with a gradient bottom line, and a rectangle with background blur and layer blur (frosted and softened); `tsc` passes, 3 new tests in `figma-paste-import.test.ts`.
- Limits: an outline that is stretched on the artboard stretches its stroke too; a vector with a dash pattern or picture fill still travels as SVG/picture; text with a background blur is unchanged.

## 2026-10-20 — Layers panel: eye and trash stay in reach
- `src/app/mo/mo-layers-scroll.ts`: with deep nesting the rows are wider than the panel and the eye and trash buttons sat at the far right, so the list had to be scrolled sideways to reach them. The buttons are now `position: sticky; right: 0` (with a short fade behind them), so they stay at the right edge of what is visible while the list scrolls; the name scrolls under them.
- Checked on Cargora (list 390 px wide in a 235 px panel, scrolled to the left): the widest row's buttons end inside the panel; `tsc` passes.
- Follow-up: the fade behind the sticky eye and trash was a flat dark colour, so a hovered or selected row showed a darker block there. It is now the panel colour plus the row's own highlight (hover and selected tints), so the backdrop matches the row's fill (`mo-layers-scroll.ts`).
- Layers panel rows have the full layer name as a tooltip (`mo-layer-icons.ts`), because a deep row's name can run under the sticky eye and trash buttons.
- Replaced the sideways scroll (and the sticky buttons) in `mo-layers-scroll.ts`: rows are as wide as the panel, the indent per level shrinks with depth (14 px for three levels, then 6 px), and a name that does not fit ends with an ellipsis before the eye and trash; the full name is the tooltip. The buttons are always at the right edge and no name runs under them.
- Eye and trash now overlay the right end of a hovered row (absolute, with the row's highlight behind them) instead of reserving room in every row, so names keep the whole width. Checked on Cargora after a reload: no sideways scroll, deep names ("Vector 1 (Stroke) 2", "Indicators 3") are fully visible, and a hovered row shows the buttons at the panel's right edge. (A running page needs a reload to pick up the style, as it is added once.)
- The Layers panel's width now follows the nesting (`mo-layers-scroll.ts` `fitPanelWidth`): the deepest open row sets a minimum width (its indent, icons and about 160 px for the name; at least 248 px, at most 520 px or 40% of the window), set as the panel's min-width so the edge can still be dragged wider; it goes back to normal when groups are closed.
- The tab bar above the panel takes the same width (`--mo-layers-width`, set with the panel width; `mo-app-sidebar.module.css` `.tabs`). Checked on Cargora after a reload: deepest row at depth 8 made the panel 297 px (from 248), names fully visible; `tsc` passes.

## 2026-10-20 — Tree lines in the Layers panel
- `mo-layers-scroll.ts` `treeRules`: each row gets a thin vertical line under the arrow of every open parent above it, so a layer's parent can be followed up the list.
- Tree lines are drawn on each row's `::before` (a 1 px bridge above and below, so the 2 px gap between rows does not break them) in a soft white (`--mo-tree-line`). Checked on Cargora at 2.5x zoom: the lines run from each open parent's arrow down through its children, at every level.
- Glitch fix: the box behind the eye and trash never matched the row's own fill (panel colour is translucent; hover and selected tints differ). It is gone: the buttons sit directly on the row's fill and the end of the name fades out under them (`mask-image` on the name while the row is hovered).

## 2026-10-20 — Picking on the artboard opens the Layers panel on the layer
- `canvas-edit.tsx` `revealInLayersPanel`: a layer picked on the artboard shows the Layers tab (`mo-show-layers`), opens the closed groups around it and scrolls its row to the middle of the list. (This replaces the earlier choice of only dashing the closed group; with the groups open the layer's row is in view, so no dashes show.)

## 2026-10-20 — Background blur + layer blur on one layer: the layer blur softens the frosted area
- Cause (Cargora, layers `fg-220` and `fg-256`: layer blur 144 + background blur): the frosted backdrop was clipped to the layer's shape and then the layer blur only softened the fill, so the frosted area kept hard edges (a visible box beside the title).
- `figma-paste-import.ts` (drawing script): `paintShape` is the shape's own painting; a layer with both blurs (and no glass) now makes the frosted backdrop and its painting on an offscreen canvas and draws that blurred by the layer blur, so the frosted area has soft edges as in Figma. Other layers are drawn as before.
- Checked on Cargora against the original design: no hard-edged box beside "MV Pacific Meridian"; `tsc` and `vitest run src/app/animation src/app/mo` pass. Not in a PR yet.

## 2026-10-20 — Straight lines (zero height) were not drawn
- Cause: a Figma line (the progress and track lines in the Voyage progress card of Cargora) has no height, and its SVG picture was drawn at that height, so it was invisible (`fg-185`, `fg-186`, and two more: `fg-21`, `fg-41`).
- `figma-paste-import.ts` (drawing script): a picture layer with no height (or width) is drawn at the picture's own size, centered on the line, so the stroke and its round ends show. New pastes already send lines as outlines (`paths`), which draw with their stroke directly.
- Checked on Cargora against the original design: the yellow progress line to "Now", the dotted track to "Long Bch" and the dots now match; `tsc` passes.

## 2026-10-20 — Glow dots and lines keep their real size (picture bigger than its box)
- Cause (Trans-Pacific route card): Figma exports a layer with a glow or shadow, round line ends, or no height as a picture bigger than the layer's box. The drawing scaled that bigger picture into the small box, so the "Long Beach" and "36.2°N" dots shrank to a speck (the 42 px picture of a 13 px dot) and the glow vanished.
- `figma-paste-import.ts` (drawing script): when an svg layer's own size (its `width` and `height`) is bigger than its box by more than 1.5 px, it is drawn at its own size, centered on the box (this also covers the zero-height lines fixed earlier, replacing that special case). A picture the same size as its box is drawn as before.
- Checked on Cargora against the original design: the white Long Beach dot with its soft glow and the yellow route point with its glow now match; `tsc` passes. Not in a PR yet.
- Follow-up (dot size and position on the Trans-Pacific route card): a picture cut by a frame (a route that starts above the map, a glow at the map's top edge) is smaller than its layer's box, and scaling it into the box shifted and stretched it (the Shanghai dot sat 5 px high, the route ran above the dot and was stretched 30% tall). The drawing script now places each axis of such a picture on the end of the box that is inside the frame (a pictured cut at the top keeps the box's bottom edge, and the other way round), and a picture bigger than its box is centered on it and kept inside the frame. Checked on Cargora against the original design: dots 7 px with their glow at the right places (Shanghai 316,907; route point 488,999; Long Beach 572,1008), the route starts at the Shanghai dot and has the original shape; the whole artboard looks as the original. `tsc` passes.
- Follow-up (Sea state card, "Limit 4.0 m" dashed line missing): the dashed line (`fg-21`, 180 x 0 box, 1 px picture) was still drawn at zero height because its picture is only 1 px taller than its box (inside the 1.5 px tolerance). A layer with no thickness now always draws its picture at the picture's own size, centered on the line. Checked on Cargora: the yellow dashes show under "Limit 4.0 m" as in the original; `tsc` passes.
- The hairline (1 or 2 px) picture of a no-thickness line is placed on whole pixels, so the dashes are crisp and bright as in the original (before they were spread over two rows and faint). Checked on Cargora: one row (y 944) with 84 yellow pixels across the 180 px line.

## 2026-10-20 — Progressive layer blur (CO2 card bars fade out at the bottom)
- Cause: the CO2 card's "Column set" group has a progressive layer blur (start radius 0, end radius 4, from about half way down to the bottom). The plugin dropped its start and end places (`startOffset`, `endOffset`), the app blurred nothing on that layer (the blur sat on a background layer with no fill), and the bars stayed sharp to the bottom.
- Plugin `code.js`: effects keep `startOffset` and `endOffset` (0 to 1 of the layer's width and height).
- App `figma-paste-import.ts`: the drawing script blurs a group's layer blur over everything inside it, and a progressive one with a growing radius: nine blurred copies mixed by how far along the start-to-end line each pixel is (`progressiveBlur`). `blurMembers(spec, layers)` (called in `sceneParams`, passed as `params.__blurMembers`) lists what a blurred frame, or a plain group's "background" layer, covers, so frames and groups pasted before get it too. Without start and end places it blurs from the middle to the bottom.
- Checked on Cargora against the original design: the bars blur and fade toward the bottom, the yellow bar glows out; `tsc` and `vitest run src/app/animation src/app/mo` pass (1 new test). The Figma places of this project are not in its data (pasted with the old plugin), so the middle-to-bottom default is used until it is pasted again with the updated plugin.

## 2026-10-20 — Fuel gauge: arcs placed by their stroke, and the layer opacity counted once
- Cause 1 (the translucent olive band behind the gauge was almost invisible): a picture exported from Figma already carries the layer's own opacity (`opacity="0.2"` on its drawing), and the app applied the layer's opacity a second time (0.2 x 0.2 = 0.04). Every picture with a baked opacity was too faint (the gauge band, the glows). `drawItem` now divides the layer's opacity by the opacity the picture already carries.
- Cause 2 (the gauge arcs sat about 4 px low): a picture bigger than its box was centered on it, but the room around a stroked arc is half the stroke on its top and sides only (its ends are flat). `figma-paste-import.ts`: for a stroked shape the picture now starts half a stroke above and left of the box; glows and shadows are still centered.
- Checked on Cargora against the original design: the wide olive band (strong at the right end, fading to the left), the yellow ticks band, the grey track and the needle sit as in the original (arc centre at 1158, 753; ring of 17 px); the whole artboard looks as the original; `tsc` passes.

## 2026-10-20 — Plugin: pictures taken so they cannot be faded twice or misplaced
- Why: the Cargora comparison showed three faults that began in what the plugin sent: pictures that already carried the layer's opacity (faded twice), pictures bigger than the layer's box with no way to tell on which sides (glows and strokes drawn off by a few pixels), and a progressive blur without its start and end (fixed earlier).
- `figma-plugin/code.js`: `exportAtFullOpacity` takes every picture (SVG, the 2x pictures of shapes and layers, the text picture) at 100% opacity and puts the scratch copy's opacity back; `exportSvg` also sends `svgBleed` (how far the picture reaches past the layer's box on each side, from the layer's render bounds and box). Outlines (`paths`), one-sided strokes, progressive blur offsets and frames are sent as in the earlier entries.
- App `figma-paste-import.ts`: `FigmaItem.bleed` from `svgBleed`; the drawing places the picture at its own size exactly by it when it fits the picture's size (within 1.5 px), otherwise by the rules added before (so older pastes and a stale render bound do not break). A picture that no longer carries opacity is drawn with the layer's opacity as is (the earlier division by a baked opacity finds none).
- Not run inside Figma: the plugin was checked for syntax and by pasting made-up payloads (1 new test); reload it in Figma from `figma-plugin/manifest.json` and paste Cargora again to confirm.

- Request: split Scene_01 into four segments: Start, Logo animation, Motion In, End (1 s, 2 s, 6 s and 1 s, so 10 s together); a button for it, and every new project starts with them.
- `src/app/animation/scenes.ts`: `SEGMENTS` (names, ids, seconds), `SEGMENTS_SECONDS` (10), `splitIntoSegments(context)` (the only scene becomes Motion In and keeps its motion settings, Reactor, sound and camera; Start, Logo animation and End begin empty; Motion In is the scene on show; the timeline becomes 10 s; null when the project already has several scenes), and `defaultSceneSet()` now returns the four segments (a new canvas given the Animation_v2 type; its timeline is set to 10 s).

Checked in the browser on a throwaway design (deleted afterwards): the bar showed Overview 10 s, Start 1 s, Logo animation 2 s, Motion In 6 s (shown) and End 1 s, and the timeline said 10 s; 3 new tests in `scenes.test.ts`; `tsc` passes.

- A project's only scene that was saved with the old default name "Scene_01" (Cargora had one) is read as "Scene" too (`readScenes`); several scenes keep their names.
- The "Split scene" button sits closer to the right edge (28 px, was 52 px): with a single scene the drag grip (nothing to move it past) is hidden, leaving only the copy icon beside it.

Not checked: the drag of the plan bars in the timeline, sound.
- The "Logo animation" group now lives inside the "Cargora - Logistic Dashboard" frame (first child, above Container) instead of on its own at the top of the Layers panel (`cargora-motion.build.py`: `ROOT_ID`). In the Logo animation scene the plan hides Container (not the whole frame, which now holds the logo). ## 2026-10-20 — Timeline: pick several bars
- `motion-timeline.tsx`: Shift (or Cmd / Ctrl) + click adds a bar of the Layer effects (plan animations, timed changes, Reactor items) to the picked ones or takes it out; Cmd / Ctrl + A picks every bar that can be retimed in the rows shown. The picked bars show as picked, and dragging one moves all of them by the same time (dragging a right edge changes all their lengths), the arrow keys nudge them together (Shift = 0.5 s), Delete removes them, and Esc or a click on empty space lets them go; a plain click on one of several picked bars keeps the others picked for the drag and picks only it on release without a drag.
- `commitMany` / `removeMany`: the plan animations and timed changes go in one change of the animation's parameters, the Reactor items in one change of the Reactor (so one change does not overwrite the other); camera and the rest are retimed one by one. One undo step per drag.

A layer that rotates, loops or is shown through a wedge keeps every frame that cuts it in place (before, a turning layer turned its card's cut with it); the wave is drawn twice one wave length apart so nothing is left empty.

Start, Motion In and End: the wind dial's conic shape spins (two whole turns in the 11 s) and the two waves run one 90 px wave length every 2.2 s (five in the 11 s), each scene with its phase (0, 4 and 10 s), so the motion carries on from scene to scene and the end meets the start (48 animations in all).

## 2026-10-20 — Cargora: my updates no longer overwrite edits made in the app
- Report: "Logo bg mask out" was changed to Go out in the Animations tab, and it was Mask out again after loading.
- Cause: every update I sent through the agent channel (`cargora-motion.build.py`) wrote the whole plan of every scene as the script defines it, replacing what had been changed in the app in the meantime (the effect of that animation, a bar moved, a value changed).
- Fix: the script now reads the project's current plans and keeps every animation already there (same id, or same name in the same scene) exactly as it is; it only adds animations the project does not have yet and keeps the ones added in the app (`--force name,name` takes the script's version of those). Checked against the current autosave: the four plans come out unchanged (6, 10, 28 and 4 animations).
- The Go out edit itself was lost when the script ran (the plan went back to Mask out), so it has to be made again in the app (or asked for here).

## 2026-10-21 — Fix: a Layer section edit wiped the scene's motion (the real cause of "all motion gone")
- Cause: `resolveParams` (`animation/animation-spec.ts`) returns only the spec's controls and the `__` settings. The Layer section (Modifier tab: video, shape, position, text, picture, blend, mask, style) writes `{ ...resolveParams(...), <setting> }` back as the scene's params, so the hand-built Interaction plan (`plan`) was dropped and the scene on show lost all its layer effects. Scenes not on show kept theirs.
- Fix: `resolveParams` keeps `plan`. Test `animation/resolve-params.test.ts`. Checked on Cargora: Split / Delete part on the video layer in Motion In keeps 28 effects.
- Motion restored with `docs/mo-app/cargora-motion.build.py` (Start 6, Logo animation 10, Motion In 28, End 4).

## 2026-10-21 — Video parts: Start, Duration and End linked (Auto) or apart (Manual)

Auto: changing Start or End changes Duration so the part keeps its speed (the cut with the next part moves); changing Duration moves End. Manual: Start and End change only what plays (the part gets faster or slower, its time stays) and Duration changes only the time (the clip times stay). The timeline's edge drag follows the same mode (`motion-timeline.tsx`).

- Fix: the card is brought into view once, when the part is picked (`takeFreshPick` in `media/video-part-pick.ts`, an effect in `clip-settings.tsx`). Checked on Cargora: picking the 4th bar scrolls to its card (992 px); during playback the panel stays where it was scrolled (100 px stayed 100 px).

## 2026-10-21 — Auto timing: End is counted from Start + Duration (normal speed)
- Report: a part with Start 0 kept End 10 s for 1.3 s of timeline (the old Auto kept the part's own speed, here 7.7x), so End was not "counted" when Start was set to 0.
- `media/media-layer.ts` `retimeVideoPart` / `setVideoPartDuration` (new signature: limit, clip length): in Auto, Start, Duration and End are one set at normal speed (End - Start = Duration). Start moves the window along the clip (End follows, Duration stays unless the clip ends first); End sets Duration; Duration sets End. Manual is as before. The timeline's edge drag uses `setVideoPartDuration` too. Test in `video-parts.test.ts` (Part 5 case: Start 0, 1.3 s gives End 1.3 s).
- Parts that already had a different speed keep it until one of the three sliders is moved in Auto.

## 2026-10-21 — Scenes can overlap (a scene starts before the one ahead of it ends)
- Model (`animation/scenes.ts`): `Scene.lead` = seconds a scene starts before the scene ahead of it ends (0 = one after the other). `resolveScenes` gives each scene `start = ahead.end - lead` (lead is at most the shorter of the two scenes) and `total` = the latest end; the project length (`finish` / `lengthOf`) follows. `sceneAt` returns, of the scenes playing at a time, the one that started last; `scenesOverlapping`; `SceneFrame.overlapping`; `changeScene(..., { lead })`.
- Drawing (`animation/scene-overlap.ts` `mergeOverlapPlans`, used by `sceneParams`): while two scenes play, the picture uses the later scene's settings and both plans: the earlier scene's effects move onto the later scene's clock (negative delays; loops keep their phase) and come first, so the later scene has the last word on a layer. Only the later scene's camera, Reactor, sound and stage are used.
- Timeline (`animation/motion-timeline.tsx`): a handle at the left edge of every scene bar but the first (`.leadHandle`): drag it left to start the scene over the end of the scene before (steps of 0.01 s), right to undo. The scene slides with what it holds (no stretching) and the scenes after it follow; the project gets shorter by the overlap. The right edge still sets the length (Duration follows the end).
- Scene bar (`animation/scenes-strip.tsx` + css): the blocks sit at their own times (not in a flex row), and hovering a scene that overlaps another draws a dashed box (`.overlapBox`) over the time they share.
- Tests: `scenes.test.ts` (overlap), `scene-overlap.test.ts`. Checked on a throwaway project (deleted afterwards): dragging Motion In's left handle moved it from 3 s to 2.16 s, End followed (8.16 s), total 9.16 s. Cargora was not touched.
- Known: the Hide effect of a later scene (for example Motion In hiding the Logo group) also applies during the overlap; shift or disable it when the scenes overlap.

- Report: Logo animation's layers are above Motion In's, but with the scenes overlapping the picture still cut to Motion In. Cause: Motion In's "Logo hidden" (Hide on the Logo animation group, its whole length) started at the overlap and removed the logo at once, and Logo animation's hides of the frame bg / top bar / content held Motion In's layers back during the overlap.
- `animation/scene-overlap.ts` `mergeOverlapPlans` (now with `end` per source): in the overlap the earlier scene's Hide effects stop where the later scene starts, and the later scene's Hide effects start where the earlier scene ends. Other effects as before. Tests in `scene-overlap.test.ts`.
- Checked on Cargora (Motion In overlaps Logo animation by 0.43 s, set in the app; nothing changed in the project): at 3.7 s the Logo bg mask-out band is opening while Motion In has started; no cut. The Motion In frame bg comes in from 0.1 s of its own clock; start it earlier (a longer overlap, or a smaller delay of "Frame bg in") to have the picture behind the mask sooner.

## 2026-10-21 — Fix: the fuel gauge arc lost its first ticks during the sweep (dark gap under the arc)
- Report: the arc's stroke dashes did not match the design (compare the Start / End scene). Cause: `Sweep reveal` shows the arc through a wedge from the pivot (the needle's base, y 747.4); the arc's flat start end lies lower (box bottom 753), so the wedge's horizontal edge cut the arc's first ticks off and left a dark gap above the label.
- `mo/figma-paste-import.ts` `drawPatched`: the wedge begins a little below the horizontal when the layer reaches below the pivot (`pad`, at most the sweep so far, so nothing shows before the sweep starts). Checked on Cargora at 520 %: at 10 s the arc has all its ticks down to the label, like the End scene; mid-sweep (5.45 s) is fine. Applies to every Sweep reveal.

## 2026-10-21 — Fuel gauge: the revealed ticks follow the needle
- Report: during the swing the arc's ticks did not follow the indicator. Cause: the reveal was a wedge from the needle's base (1165.36, 747.39), but the ring is a half circle of radius 76.15 around (1158.1, 753), so the wedge cut the ring's radial ticks along a slanted line that met the needle at the wrong place.
- Sweep reveal (`mo/figma-paste-import.ts` `planState`, `animation/interaction-plan.ts`, `animation/interaction-plan-editor.tsx`): optional `bx`, `by` (the needle's base) and `reach` (the ring's radius around the pivot). The wedge is then drawn from the ring's centre and ends where the needle crosses the ring (it still starts at the ring's start and ends with the design's arc), so the ticks are cut along the ring's own radius, at the needle. Without them the sweep is as before. Test in `interaction-plan.test.ts`.
- `cargora-reactor.build.py` sets them on the two sweeps of Motion In (pivot = the ring's centre). Checked at 520 %: at 5.55 s the ticks end radially at the needle, at 10 s the whole arc is there.

## 2026-10-21 — Fuel gauge: the arc grows along its own path, ending under the needle tip
- The user chose "Arc grows along its own path" (an elliptical arc sweep mask). The Sweep reveal's `reach` is now the needle's length (it was the ring radius in the entry above): the wedge, from the ring's centre (the pivot), ends at the direction of the needle's tip seen from the pivot, so the ticks are cut along the ring's radius, under the tip (`mo/figma-paste-import.ts`, `planState`). Editor: "Needle length". `cargora-reactor.build.py` sets bx / by = the needle's base, reach = 87 (its length). Checked at 520 % at 5.9 s: the end of the ticks sits beside the needle tip; at the end it is the design's arc (45 deg).

## 2026-10-21 — Fuel gauge: the mask starts at the arc's start (down to the label)
- Report: the reveal must start at the arc's start (the arrow at the "00" label), not part way up the arc. The arc's wide glow (Ellipse 8, stroke 38.6 px) reaches 19 px below the ring's start; the wedge's lower edge (the horizontal through the pivot) cut it off, and early in the sweep (the lower edge was limited by how far the sweep had gone) only a block part way up showed.
- `mo/figma-paste-import.ts` `drawPatched`: the wedge always begins below the horizontal by what the layer reaches (its box bottom plus half its stroke, `pad`) as soon as the sweep has started (nothing shows before). ## 2026-10-21 — Reactor items follow the design at any artboard ratio
- Report: at 9:16 and 2:3 the cursor points were off. Cause: an item's X / Y (percent) were percent of the canvas; the design is laid on the artboard by the frame (contain / cover), so at another ratio the design is not the whole canvas.
- `animation/interactions.ts` `drawInteractions`: new option `place` (percent of the design -> canvas pixels); `animation/animation-canvas.tsx` and `mo/mo-render.ts` pass it (`mapDesignPoint` with the design's size and the frame's transform). Without `place` the percent is of the canvas as before. So an item on the marker stays on the marker at every ratio, in the preview and in Render. Projects whose design does not fill the canvas at their own ratio now have their items placed by the design, not the canvas.
- Checked on Cargora at 9:16 (the user had switched to it): at 7.8 s the cursor sits on the route's marker inside the small design. The throwaway project used for the check was deleted.

## 2026-10-21 — Layers panel: the tree line ends at the last child with a rounded corner and an arrow
- `mo/mo-layers-scroll.ts` (`paintTree`, `treeBackground`): each row's background (a CSS variable `--mo-tree` on the row, drawn in its `::before`) is worked out from the depths of the rows around it. A level's vertical line runs through the rows between the first and the last child of a group or frame; at the last child it stops at the middle of the row with a rounded corner (radius 5 px) and, for the first three levels (where the row's content has room), a small arrow pointing at the layer. The last child's own children no longer carry that level's line. Rows are repainted when the list changes (same observer as the panel width).
- Checked on Cargora at 220 % zoom: the Container (only child), Frame 8 (last child of Frame 7), Frame 9 / Route map / Frame 10 show the corner.
- Follow-up: the arrow at the end of the corner is gone (only the rounded corner and the short stub stay).
- Follow-up: the end of the tree line showed edges (a flat stub end). It is now one quarter circle (SVG arc, radius 5 px) with round line ends, no straight stub. Checked at 300 % zoom on the Container's corner.
- Follow-up: every tree line has round ends. Where a level's line starts (the first child, under its parent) it starts 3 px below the row's top as an SVG line with a round cap; where it ends it is the quarter circle with a round end. Between the first and the last child the line stays a seamless 1 px strip (the rows join).

## 2026-10-21 — Transparent artboard background shows a checkerboard
- `animation/animation-canvas.tsx` + `animation-canvas.module.css` (`.checker`): when the Artboard setting's Background is Transparent (`bg.mode`), the preview canvas has a checkerboard (16 px squares, light grey and white) behind the picture. It is only the preview's backdrop: renders and exports keep their transparency. Checked on a throwaway project (deleted): Background: Transparent shows the checkerboard with the design on it.
- Follow-up: the checkerboard was too busy: squares are 16 px now (a 32 px tile) in two close greys (#f6f6f7 / #e9e9ec) instead of 8 px squares in white / #d4d4d8.

## 2026-10-21 — Artboard: layers that Figma turned can be selected and dragged; any scene can be edited
- Report: in "vyroin_ Gifs" (a watch and a GIF frame, both rotated in Figma) a click on the artboard selected nothing and a layer picked in the panel had no outline or drag.
- Cause 1 (`animation/layer-geometry.ts` `readPlaced`): a layer that Figma turned carries a matrix (`item.m`) and its x / y are 0 (the matrix holds where it is drawn), so its hit box sat at the artboard's top-left corner, away from the picture. The box is now the box around the four corners the matrix gives.
- Cause 2 (`animation/animation-canvas.tsx` `canEditOnArtboard`): editing on the artboard needed the playhead's scene to be the scene being edited (the live one). A project made as Animation_v2 starts with four scenes, the playhead is in Start and the live scene is Motion In, so nothing could be edited. Any scene can be edited now (what a drag, resize or retype writes is kept under the shared "__" keys).
- Checked in the app on that project: a click on the watch selects it with its box (and the panel row), a drag moves it (Position X / Y change), Cmd+Z puts it back (164 / 224).

## 2026-10-21 — New project: one scene and the Split scene button (no automatic four scenes)
- `animation/scenes-strip.tsx`: removed the effect that gave an empty Animation_v2 canvas the four default scenes. A new project now has one scene ("Scene") and the Split scene button on the scene bar; pressing it makes Start, Logo animation, Motion In and End (Motion In on show). Animation_v2 stays the default type. Checked: a new project shows "Scene 8 s" with the button, and after the click the four scenes (the test project was deleted).

## 2026-10-21 — Split scene shows scene presets beside the button
- `animation/scenes.ts`: `SCENE_PRESETS` (Logo intro: Start 1, Logo animation 2, Motion In 6, End 1 = 10 s; Start and end: Start, Motion In, End = 8 s; Motion and end: Motion In 6, End 1 = 7 s), `presetSeconds`, `splitIntoPreset(context, id)` (the scene's own motion goes to Motion In, which is shown); `splitIntoSegments` is the first preset. `animation/scenes-strip.tsx` + css: pressing Split scene opens the presets in a row beside (to the left of) the button; a click on one splits the scene, Esc or a click elsewhere closes the row. Tests in `scenes.test.ts`. Checked in a new Animation_v2 project (deleted afterwards): the three presets show beside the button, "Start and end" gives Start 1 s, Motion In 6 s, End 1 s. The Split scene button is part of Animation_v2 (the default type), which starts every new project with one scene.

## 2026-10-21 — Layers panel: lock icon beside the layer icon
- `mo/mo-layer-lock.ts` (`useLayerLock`, called in `mo-app-sidebar.tsx`): every row gets a lock between its icon and its name. It is out of sight (opacity 0) and shows when the pointer is over its place (an open lock); clicking it locks the layer (a closed lock that stays shown; the layers a locked group holds show it dimmed, `data-mo-locked="own" | "inherited"`), clicking again unlocks. The press only locks: it does not select the row.
- `animation/layer-lock.ts`: the locks live in the project's settings under `__locked` ({ layerId: true }, shared by every scene, saved and versioned with them); `lockedLayers` adds what a locked group holds. `animation/canvas-edit.tsx`: a locked layer is not picked by a click on the artboard (the click goes to the layer under it) and cannot be moved or resized there; it can still be selected in the panel.
- Checked on "vyroin_ Gifs" (unlocked again afterwards): the lock shows only over its place, a locked Floating watch keeps its lock and a click on the watch selects the layer under it.

## 2026-10-21 — The scene length box in the timeline header shows only in a scene's view
- `animation/scene-length-input.tsx`: the box beside Duration (the length of the scene on show) is hidden in the Overview (all the scenes) and shown when a scene's view is open (`useTimelineView() === "scene"`). Checked: Overview: hidden; after clicking Motion In (view "Motion In · 3s → 9s"): shown.
- Follow-up: the gaps around the lock were too wide (the row's 8 px gap on both sides of a 16 px box). The lock box is 12 px wide with -4 px margins, so icon, lock and name sit about 4 px apart.

## 2026-10-21 — Layers panel: no trash button on a row
- `mo/mo-layers-scroll.ts`: the row's Delete (trash) button is hidden (`[data-layer-actions] button[aria-label^="Delete "]`); the eye stays. A layer is deleted with the Delete key (Layers panel selection or the artboard). To bring the trash back, remove that one CSS rule.
- Follow-up: the lock is at the right end of the row, to the right of the eye (the eye moved 22 px left): shown while the row is hovered, a closed lock stays shown on a locked layer. It is a child of the row (`li > [data-mo-lock]`), so the names no longer have a gap for it. Checked: lock and unlock from the new place on "vyroin_ Gifs" (unlocked again).
- Follow-up: the lock shows while its row is hovered or selected (the selected row is the one whose surface has the accent fill; `data-selected` stays false for a layer picked in the panel), and stays shown on a locked layer. Checked: with ezgif-frame selected and the pointer away, only its lock shows.

## 2026-10-21 — Reset scenes (while no scene has motion) and choose a preset again
- `animation/scenes.ts`: `scenesHaveMotion` (a layer effect in a scene's plan, a Reactor item or a camera in any scene) and `resetScenes` (one scene, "Scene", as long as the whole motion, keeping the settings of the scene on show). `animation/scenes-strip.tsx` + css: while the project has several scenes and none has motion, a "Reset scenes" button sits above the right end of the scene bar; pressing it puts the scenes back to one scene and opens the scene presets beside Split scene (Logo intro, Start and end, Motion and end). Tests in `scenes.test.ts`. Checked in a new project (deleted afterwards): Split scene -> preset -> Reset scenes shows -> "Scene 10 s" with the three presets; "vyroin_ Gifs" (no motion) shows the button too.
- Fix: the lock stayed on a row after the user clicked away from it (empty artboard click). The app keeps the runtime's selection but marks that row `data-deselected` and clears its fill; the lock rule looked only at the accent fill class. It now ignores rows with `data-deselected`. Checked: select the watch (lock shows), click an empty artboard place (lock gone).
- Fix: no hover fill on a layer row the user had clicked away from. `mo/mo-layer-selection.ts` covered that row's selected fill with an inline `background-color: transparent`, which also beat the hover fill. It is a CSS rule now (`mo/mo-layers-scroll.ts`: `li[data-deselected] > [data-layer-row-surface]` transparent, and on `:hover` the normal 10 % fill), no inline style. Checked: select the watch, click empty artboard, hover its row: the hover fill shows; pointer away: transparent.

## 2026-10-21 — vyron_ Gif-01: floating watch as a Lottie loop
- Open project "vyron_ Gif-01" (Lottie Animation) rebuilt through the agent channel as real Lottie data (`spec.lottie`): one image layer "Floating watch_caseback" (the layer's 600x650 picture, 50 % scale, same place on the 330 artboard), 30 fps, 8 s, saved as version `v_float`.
- Float: whole cycles in the 8 s (2 bobs of 6 px, 1 sway of 3.5 px, 1 tilt of 1.6°) with bezier handles from the sine's speed, so the end equals the start in position and speed (no loop seam). The picture's painted edge stays 7+ px inside the artboard all the time (measured while playing). Export check: "Everything in this project exports to Lottie".

- `animation/lottie-keys.ts` (new: the keyframe times per layer and property of `spec.lottie`), `animation/motion-timeline.tsx` + css: in a scene's view a Lottie layer gets a row (opens into Position X / Position Y / Rotation ...) with a bar from its first to its last key and a diamond at every keyframe in between (`Bar.marks`); click a diamond to take the playhead there. Before, only plan effects showed, so a Lottie file's motion left the timeline at "No layer effects yet". Checked on "vyron_ Gif-01": 3 property rows, diamonds every 10 frames.

## 2026-10-21 — Lottie: Export button inside Render and Export
- `mo/mo-inspector-labels.ts`: with Format Lottie the footer bar (whose button reads Export) was never un-hidden after a tab change, because it was only recognised by the label "Render", so Export was nowhere. Now the bar is recognised by the export mark, stays hidden for Lottie, and a copy of its button sits at the end of the Render and Export section (`placeExportButton`); a click on the copy presses the footer's button, so the export runs as before. Other formats keep the footer Render button. Checked: Lottie shows Export in the section, MP4 shows the footer Render.

## 2026-10-21 — vyron_ Gif-01: float rebuilt to stop jiggling on a website
- Version `v_float2`: 60 fps (was 30), a key on every frame with straight lines between them (the 10-frame bezier handles of v_float could overshoot where a curve peaked between two keys), and no rotation (a rotated, scaled picture shimmers at its edges in browsers). Bob 6 px x2 and sway 3.5 px x1 kept; the loop still ends where it starts.
- `animation/lottie-keys.ts`: a property with more than 48 keys shows a diamond about every 0.25 s only, so a baked curve does not draw a wall of diamonds.

## 2026-10-21 — vyron_ Gif-01: smaller Lottie file (v_float3)
- Keys every 4 frames instead of every frame (the line between two keys is within 0.01 px of the sine), values to 3 decimals: the motion data went from ~66 KB to ~17 KB. The embedded picture (216 KB PNG) was tried lossless (recompress + best row filters: -2 %) and as WebP (not smaller without a visible change), so it is unchanged. dotLottie stores the picture as a binary file, avoiding the 33 % base64 growth of the JSON file.

## 2026-10-21 — Lottie: file size shown under Render and Export
- `lottie/lottie-check-control.tsx`: the "Everything in this project exports to Lottie" line in Render and Export now ends with "File size: about N KB as JSON / dotLottie" for the chosen file type (the animation as the project holds it; a hidden layer or Background layer changes it a little). Checked: 298 KB as JSON, 218 KB as dotLottie.

## 2026-10-21 — Motion tab: Preset section with Floating
- New section **Preset** (Motion tab, under Motion Plan; Animation_v2 and Interaction Animation) with the control `motionPresets` (`animation/motion-presets.tsx`). Select a layer (or a group) in the Layers panel, press **Apply to selected layer** on Floating: it adds a "Floating · layer" animation to the open scene's Motion Plan (whole motion, as long as the scene, a bob and a sway of about 1.8 % of the artboard, whole cycles of about 8 s that fit the scene exactly so the loop has no seam). Apply again replaces it, **Remove** takes it out; it can also be changed in Animations like any animation.
- New plan effect **Float loop** (`float`: `interaction-plan.ts`, the drawing script in `mo/figma-paste-import.ts`, Period / Phase / Float distance in `interaction-plan-editor.tsx`, phase kept across overlapping scenes in `scene-overlap.ts`): `dy = d·sin(2·turn)`, `dx = 0.58·d·sin(turn + 1.2)`, no rotation (the same motion as the vyron Lottie float).
- `animation/animation-controls.tsx`: a scene whose script has no controls now shows the hand-built Motion Plan editor (it showed an empty plan that ignored `params.plan`), so presets are listed and editable there.
- Checked (browser pane, project Start with strategic clarity_Kit set to Animation_v2, then put back to Animation): Floating on the Content group moved the whole group (39k pixels differ at 0.99 s), the Animations tab listed it, Remove cleared it. Not for Lottie Animation projects (their motion is inside the Lottie file).

## 2026-10-21 — Preset section for every motion type
- `app-schema.ts`: the Preset section is no longer limited to Animation_v2 / Interaction Animation; it shows for every motion type.
- `animation/motion-presets.tsx`: in a Lottie Animation project Floating is written into the Lottie file as split position X/Y keyframes on the selected top-level layer (a key every 4 frames, whole cycles that fit the length, 0.58 sway : 1 bob, no rotation), through the animation library; Remove restores the still position (the mean of the whole-cycle keys). A layer that already moves in the Lottie file is left alone (message shown). Other types keep the plan animation.
- Checked on vyron_ Gif-01 (browser pane): Remove cleared the keys, Apply wrote them back (33 keys at 15-frame steps in the first build, first = last key, base position 127.995 / 121.172 restored), then the step was tightened to 4 frames.

## 2026-10-21 — GIF: 0.25K resolution
- `mo/mo-render.ts`, `app-schema.ts`: the GIF Resolution row has a third choice, **0.25K** (640 px on the long edge), next to 0.5K (1280 px) and Original (the artboard's own size); its description says what each gives. A 330 px artboard rendered at 0.5K came out 1280×1280 (36.2 MB for 240 frames); 0.25K is a quarter of the pixels.
